# The third engine: design

This is the design of record for the engine. It is one living document: every section is at its current value, and the ledger below records each revision with what changed, why, and the check that would show it wrong. Git holds the dated versions. `research/third-engine-design.r1.md` to `research/third-engine-design.r7.md` are the seven earlier revisions, kept unedited as records; they are not read for anything this file marks current, and the "highest `N` wins" naming rule of revision 3 no longer applies. The compiled tier's design of record is `jit-design.md`, in the same shape. The harness's is `harness-design.md`. Vocabulary is in `CONTEXT.md`. Decisions reversed or otherwise likely to be "fixed" by a later reader are in `docs/adr/0001` to `0006`. Research, in `research/`: `research.md` and `data/` for the two predecessors (Mahout `M/`, Dax `D/`), `jit-research.r1.md` and `llvm-research.r1.md` for the tier; the records and the research cite `data/` relative to that directory. php-src paths are cited `Z/path:line`; from revision 9 on at the tag `oracle.toml` pins, and in the records at 8.5.7, the tag the research read. Numbers that the harness enforces, budgets, edges, lock names and `unsafe` homes, are stated here once and carried by `seams.toml`; the oracle's version, digest and recipe are stated in `oracle.toml` and nowhere else; the instruction set is `ops.toml`, extracted from the pinned tree by `scripts/extract_ops.py`.

Goals as given, non-negotiable: insane performance, full parity with PHP 8.5.x, modern and future-proof architecture, the best modern Rust, memory efficient, smallest possible codebase, done in one month.

---

## Ledger: what changed, and why

Each entry is numbered as the revision that made it and names the check that would show it wrong.

**1. Revision 1 (2026-09-05).** Zend's execution model in Rust, once, with nothing beside it: Zend's layouts and algorithms, one executor, the library rule, witnesses and phpt per commit, nine crates, 260k lines. Check: the day-30 bars of §9.

**2. Revision 2 (2026-09-05).** Named every module's interface, measured it and locked it: `php_engine::api` sized from a census of what Mahout's extensions call, op-array identity against php's own dump, interface locks, `HeapHooks`, map pointers, the 25-slot object-handler table, frames in `php_heap`, handler bodies monomorphised over operand kinds. Check: `compile-identity` byte-identical on the per-commit corpus; `port-map` unresolved rows reaching zero.

**3. Revision 3 (2026-09-05).** Walked the inherited crates: one declaration model in a leaf crate `php_unit` (ADR-0001), validate dissolved into the compiler, the `Sapi` trait replaced by a request description and an output sink, the typed arena tree (ADR-0003), native entries taking the concrete request (ADR-0002), `php_proto`. Check: the manifest corrections of §13 name files that exist.

**4. Revision 4 (2026-09-05).** One crate per extension under `ext/` with `php_ext` below and `php_bundle` above (ADR-0004); the witness fence carries the exit status, captured and never authored; the phpt runner gains upstream's `run-tests.php` as its nightly control. Check: the bundle's order is a topological order of the crate graph; `exit=` written only by `--accept`; the two runners agree.

**5. Revision 5 (2026-09-05).** Extension state is request-scoped or immutable engine-scoped, and the engine owns the only cross-request cache (ADR-0005). Check: `Cache` hit cost on the pcre workload under thread-per-request.

**6. Revision 6 (2026-09-06).** The tentative return type as one bit on the declared return type, set only by natives, read by the linker and Reflection. Check: the 31 phpts of the `tentative` subset.

**7. Revision 7 (2026-09-06).** The compiled tier as the eighth seam, designed in from day one: `execute` generic over `Tier` with one hook, `step` reporting the edge it took, one opaque `Word` per safepoint in `Published`, `OpArray.safepoints` and the `safepoint` column, the `VmStack` entry slot, `php_jit` behind a feature, one compile worker per engine. Revision 1's `Func.code` slot, per-cache-slot feedback word and "baseline method JIT" go. Check: G1 within the A/A drift bound from the day `php_engine::tier` exists.

**8. Revision 8 (2026-09-07).** A review of every design, the seams, the month-one to month-two transition and the harness. Six changes, and the shape of this file:

1. **Three mechanisms the interpreter owes the tier, stated as mechanisms** (§5.6, §5.8). The request's interrupt flag is a named item, tested at back-edges, at frame activation and after every `Flow::Resume`, so that a compiled loop can be stopped; php's JIT tests `vm_interrupt` in every compiled loop and revision 7 had removed the back-edge check from compiled code and put nothing in its place, so the §8 witness `set_time_limit(1); for (;;) {}` would have hung under `jit.mode=force`. `Site::Entry` is raised by the activation of a user frame after its `RECV*` prologue, not "inside the first body opline's handler", which can be any opcode. A request-scoped op array (`eval`, an uncached include) carries sealed words, all ones, so the tier never claims on a `Func` that has no `Published`.
2. **Two invariants made assertions** (§5.7, §6.1). `JMPZ` joins the `safepoint` column, since `zend_compile.c` inverts a `BOOL_NOT` condition's branch, and the compiler asserts on every op array that every backward jump has a column and a numbered header; a fused compare-and-branch is one handler over two oplines for `step` and for `Site::Loop`, stated once.
3. **The threading model stated once** (§5.14). Revision 1 said a thread pool, ADR-0005 argued from "threads are not pooled". A host runs each request to completion on one thread; whether it pools threads is the host's and is invisible to the engine, because no engine or extension state is thread-local. ADR-0005's argument holds for that reason and is amended to say so.
4. **Names and sizes corrected.** `opcache.jit_buffer_size` is the one name; `tier.lock` is a face of a few dozen items plus a generated kernels section of about three hundred; `php_bundle` depends on `php_ext` as well as `ext/*`, and on `php_jit` only on the four targets the feature names; the harness manifest's example budgets are this file's.
5. **ADR-0006** records the eighth seam, the engine-level word and the deletion of revision 1 §9.3, which the design's own rule in §10 required and revision 7 did not write; ADR-0004's two-home `unsafe` sentence is amended to point at it.
6. **One living document** (§10). Seven files, each saying which sections of which predecessor still stood, cost every reader seven files and let stale sentences survive (`xtask opgen --check`, deleted in revision 2 and standing in revision 1's text; two `unsafe` lists in force at once). This file is the design at its current value with this ledger on top; the `rN` files are records; `jit-design.md` and `harness-design.md` have the same shape. This is harness `§14` item 5, taken.

Check: `Probe` sees `(Entry, 0)` once per call and never when a `RECV` throws; the time-limit and signal witnesses pass under `force`; an `eval` op array's words read as sealed; `php_compile`'s assertion holds over the compile-identity corpus; `xtask budget` against `seams.toml` passes with the numbers of §4.1 and no others.

**9. Revision 9 (2026-09-07).** The pre-day-1 checks (`data/pre-day-1.md`): the manifest, the oracle pin, `ops.toml` and `api.lock` v0 were written before any crate exists, and writing them against the source contradicted three sentences and corrected six numbers. Nothing architectural moves.

1. **`JMPZ` is not a pre-optimizer back-edge** (§5.7). Revision 8 added it to the `safepoint` column on the claim that `zend_emit_cond_jump` inverts a `BOOL_NOT` condition's branch. It does not (`Z/Zend/zend_compile.c:2387-2409`); `do { } while (!$x)` is `BOOL_NOT` followed by a backward `JMPNZ`. The backward jumps `pass_two` leaves are `JMPNZ` and `JMP`; a backward `JMPZ` exists only after `block_pass.c`'s `INV_COND`, so the column keeps `JMPZ` for a month-two optimizer's output and the sentence is corrected.
2. **`FAST_RET` is excluded from the compiler's assertion** (§5.7). It jumps to `fast_call + 1`, an earlier index, and is the `finally` subroutine's return with no CFG successor (`Z/Zend/zend_cfg.c:508`); "every backward jump has a column and a numbered header" fires on the first `finally` unless it excludes that opcode.
3. **Where php's entry counter sits, cited, and the difference accepted** (§5.6, §12). `i_init_func_execute_data` skips the `RECV*` run only for a function with no typed parameters that was passed no more arguments than it declares (`Z/Zend/zend_execute.c:4426-4437`); php's JIT installs its entry counter after the `RECV*` run in that case and on opline zero otherwise (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit.c:3219-3231`). `Site::Entry` stays where revision 8 put it, after the prologue has run without raising, so a `RECV` that throws is never inside a recording; for a typed function php counts a call whose `RECV` throws and this engine does not. That difference in trace roots is the third `trace` row of the divergence registry from day one, checked by the `TRACE start` diff.
4. **Numbers corrected from the source.** `Z/Zend/zend_vm_def.h` at 8.5.9 has 207 handlers, one inline handler, 42 type-specialised handlers and 34 helpers, not 25 (§5.7); `run-tests.php`'s `$ini_overwrites` has 38 entries, not 39 (§8); the phpt corpus at the pinned tag has 21,655 files; the extension surface at the Mahout pin is 389 items, not 345, because the revision-2 census missed the `Reentrant::ctx` receiver and the public fields of the request (§1, §4.4); `api.lock` v0 has 301 items (§11); `hash`, listed in §7.2 with no crate in Mahout and no row in §4.1, gets a row of 3k (§4.1).
5. **The `expect` fence and rustdoc** (§8). `exit=255` on the fence line trips `rustdoc::invalid_codeblock_attributes` under `-D warnings`; every crate that carries witnesses allows that lint at its root, since `xtask witness` is the fence's parser and rustdoc is not.
6. **`engine_limitation` has no successor** (§4.4). php has no engine-limitation error, so no witness can pin one; of Mahout's fifteen sites, ten go with the C bindings, two with `call` and the object handlers, and three are unported behaviour whose natives fail their own witnesses until ported.

Check: `scripts/extract_ops.py` over the pinned tree reproduces `ops.toml` byte for byte; `Probe` sees `(Loop, n)` on `do { } while (!$x)` from a `JMPNZ`; a witness with a `finally` compiles under the assertion; the third `trace` row's site is what the `TRACE start` diff reports on a typed kernel and nothing else.

**10. Revision 10 (2026-09-13).** Which way `xtask compile-identity` reaches `Unit::dump`, which §6.1 named the printer of and never the caller (#154). The lane compiles in this process, through `php_bundle`, the way the witness lane already reaches the engine: `xtask` depends on the bundle's test face for the reason §4.2 gives, a fresh `Engine` compiles the corpus file, and `Unit::dump` is an inherent method on what it hands back, so no edge of `seams.toml` moves and the lane waits on no binary. §6.1 says so and §4.2's parenthetical names `compile-identity` beside the other lanes. Rejected: the engine's binary honouring `-l` and dumping like php, which makes the compiler lane wait on `crates/php` and on the host face, and stays available for the day both exist; and `php_compile` and `php_unit` on the `xtask` edge, which widens a seam for a call the bundle already carries. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: on the day 1–2 engine of §11 the lane is red on every file of the per-commit corpus and skips none of them for a binary that is not built, with the `xtask` edge of `seams.toml` as it stands.

**11. Revision 11 (2026-09-13).** What takes a row off the porting checklist, which §4.4 made an instrument of and never said (#150). A row is resolved when every extension crate the census counted for it is ported, and *ported* is what the harness reads without a binary: the crate's `extension()` registers at least one native. The census gains the crate names per row, which `--tree` regenerates and the v0 seed does not carry; the reading stays one, at `php_ext`, the crate `api.lock` lives with (harness §7). §11 moves with it, because a measure that waits on a row's last crate cannot put every row's last crate before day 21: row 15–21's cell is the rows of the crates row 22–30 ports and no others, a predicate the instrument computes rather than a constant guessed here, and row 22–30 gains the zero. Rejected: the crate-count proxy `port_map::Ported::reached` wrote at #98, which counts how many extensions are ported and not whether the ported ones are the crates that called the item, so a row whose sites sat in `mbstring` alone resolves on the day `random` is ported; and any measure keyed on the `api` item's name appearing in a ported body, which #104 refused for its own reason, since any `len()` on a `Vec` would resolve `values::PhpArray::len` and a `gone` row would resolve by the absence of a name no ported body could spell. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: on the day 1–2 engine, with twenty empty `ext/*` crates, the instrument writes 651 unresolved and not a smaller number; porting one crate drops exactly the rows whose census crates are that crate and no others; and the number reaches zero on the last extension row 22–30 names and on no earlier day.

**12. Revision 12 (2026-09-13).** Nine figures of §4.9, §4.1 and §13 that the pinned Mahout tree does not confirm, each taking the measured number (#166). `data/syntax-extraction.md` read every one of them at `1b3104a8` against a named file and line range with `xtask budget`'s own counter, so where a sentence and a measurement disagree the sentence is what moves.

1. **The snapshot tests are 253, not 549** (§4.9). 549 is exactly the `#[test]` count of `mahout_parser/tests/*.rs` at the pin, but 296 of them are `fixtures_validation.rs`, and §6.1 sends the validator to `php_compile`: a test of the validator is not a test of the parser, and the crate that owns the code owns the tests. §4.9 now names where the 296 go, so it and §6.1 say the same thing in as many words.
2. **Four quoted figures take their measured values** (§4.9, §4.1). The `expecting` clauses are 61, beside the 74 hand-placed error sites in `grammar/` and the 79 `probed 8.5.x` oracle citations, which are the numbers a reader can check; the 80 reproduces from no grep and is dropped. The driver has 37 methods at the pin, where "≈ 30" is `~/Projects/mahout` HEAD, a different lineage carrying neither `php_names.rs` nor `unexpected_expecting`. The upward walks are 103: 91 validate's, 9 the red cursors' own and 3 reparse's, every one of them in a file this copy drops, which is what makes "no parent links" hold rather than what weakens it. The unused generated lines are 1,064 raw, of which 833 have no reader; the other 231 are `check_required_children`, whose soak ADR-0003 keeps.
3. **"One regeneration" reads per fixture file, not per commit** (§4.9). A fixture cannot assert what it cannot render, so the dump lands with the arena and not after the grammar; the grammar then arrives in three commits and each regenerates the fixture files it brings, once each. No snapshot is regenerated twice, which is what ADR-0003 asks for, but the 253 do not pass through one commit, and a person reads three diffs where the sentence promised one.
4. **§13's first row was wrong in both directions** (§13). It routed `mahout_source/{magic_method,builtin_attr}.rs` into `php_syntax`, where neither the lexer nor the grammar names either file — every site is `validate/`'s, the linker's or the runtime's — so they take a row of their own and follow the validator into `php_compile`. It omitted `mahout_source/{lib,diag,line_index}.rs`, which both inherited crates `use` and without which the copy does not compile. And the generator is three files, not two: `codegen.rs` emits the `SyntaxKind` enum, the punctuation and keyword tables, the token-class hold and the `rustfmt` pass, so `{codegen_ast,ungram}.rs` alone emit nothing that compiles, and 2,372 raw lines become 3,175.

None of this changes the answer §4.9 gives. The copy is 8,339 ceiling lines against the 26,000 of §4.1: each correction moves a figure inside that total or, like the 296 fixtures and the 833 generated lines, outside the ceiling altogether, and 17,661 lines of the ceiling stay unspent either way. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Rejected: reading the figures at `~/Projects/mahout` HEAD, the clone §13 pointed the work at, which is the lineage the "≈ 30" comes from and which prices validate at 1,580 ceiling lines instead of 7,726.

Check: every file §13's first row names exists at `1b3104a8`, and neither `magic_method.rs` nor `builtin_attr.rs` is among them; the ten slices of `data/syntax-extraction.md` §5 land `php_syntax` under `xtask budget`'s 26,000 with 253 snapshot tests green and no fixture file regenerated twice.

**13. Revision 13 (2026-09-13).** What a lock is a lock of (#188). §4.5 enumerated six locks and then said of all of them that `api-check` regenerates each "from the compiled crate's public items", which was never true of three: `sapi.lock` and `tier.lock` are what their consumers import from `php_engine`, and `ops.lock` is rendered from `ops.toml`'s `[[op]]` rows. The instrument branches on those three kinds, so §4.5 names them as a set and says which locks are of which, and the clause that follows keeps its force for all three: an added item is a lock line committed with the code, and any difference is a failure. `xtask.lock` is of the first kind, the public items of the crate it lives with, though the harness's own design declares it. The home stays out of §4.5: which crate a lock lives with is `seams.toml`'s, in the `[lock_home]` table where it is not the seam's producer, and a design document carries no fact the manifest carries. Rejected: hedging the sentence into "regenerates each from its source", which leaves a reader meeting an empty `sapi.lock` unable to tell a lock with no consumer from a lock nobody has written; and naming `ops.lock`'s home here beside its kind, which restates the manifest in the document that cites it. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: the subject `api-check` prints for each lock — the public items of a crate, the imports from a producer, or the opcodes of `ops.toml` — is the kind §4.5 gives it, and a seam whose lock is none of the three leaves the instrument no kind to take.

**14. Revision 14 (2026-09-13).** §4.1's budget column goes (#168). AGENTS.md's rule and §4.1's own sentence, "`seams.toml` carries these numbers and no other file restates them", were both contradicted by the table under that sentence, and the `xtask` cell read 14k one commit after the ceiling moved to 18000 (#160). Correcting the cell leaves the trap armed for the next rise, so the column goes and the rows cite the manifest as the rest of the document does: the crate map keeps role, interface and origin; the per-extension table, the same column in another shape, becomes the sentence that says where those ceilings come from; the totals paragraph names `[budget]` instead of restating `total`, `new`, `docs` and `lock_packages`; and §0's headline, §1's "110k new" and §10's budgets bullet go the same way, the 130k of the inherited tree staying where §1 measures it. Where a row's origin stated an inheritance ratio, `xtask`'s "Inherited pieces, 62k → 14k", the 62k is a measurement of Mahout's tree and stays and the ceiling goes, the clause reading as the claim it is about how much of the inherited harness survives. Interface item counts are not this entry's: the `≤ 400` of §4.4 and §4.5 is a ceiling `api-check` holds on a lock, not a line ceiling, and the sizing argument that reaches it keeps its number. Earlier ledger entries quote the figures §4.1 carried when they were written; they are records of their revisions and are not edited. Rejected: correcting 14k to 18k, which leaves the next rise the same trap; and rendering the column as a region from the manifest, which buys a second sensor for a fact `cargo xtask budget` already fails on and puts a computed block inside a hand-written table. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: a number in any of the three design documents that `cargo xtask budget` could disagree with — `jit-design.md`'s "17,450 against the 17.5k ceiling" and `harness-design.md` §5's Budgets row are the two that still would, filed as #192 with the manifest's two citations of where the numbers used to be.

**15. Revision 15 (2026-09-14).** The `Opline` carries php's `result_type` (#226). §5.7 laid the opline down as twenty-four bytes with `opcode: u16` and no result kind, one byte short of `zend_op`, and three observables read the byte this engine had no field for: the dump opens a line with the result operand, `V0 = DO_ICALL`, and §6.1's gate is byte-identity, so every corpus file that reads a call's value or an operator's stayed red whatever the compiler emitted (#207); `DO_ICALL` and `ASSIGN` read it as `RETURN_VALUE_USED`, so an unread return's destructor ran at the call in php and at the frame's pop here, which §5.10 lists as observable; and the compiler had nowhere to write the mark `zend_do_free` leaves. php's `opcode` is one byte and its numbers reach 210, so `Opcode` is `#[repr(u8)]` and `result_type` takes the byte the `u16` was spending; the opline is `zend_op` field for field and stays twenty-four bytes. §5.8's dense `u16` reads `u8` with it. Rejected: the high nibble of `op1_type` carrying the result kind, which makes one field two things and `OperandKind` no longer the byte's value; and no field, which holds the corpus gate red and asks a divergence row for a destructor that runs late where §5.10 stands on doing what Zend does. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `size_of::<Opline>()` is 24; `compile-identity --file` over `<?php echo 1 + extension_loaded('Core');` is byte-identical to the oracle's dump and `<?php set_time_limit(1); for (;;) {}` still is, its `DO_ICALL` bare; a site opcode's `result_type` is `UNUSED` on every backward jump and return the compiler emits.

**16. Revision 16 (2026-09-14).** The sink has two channels where php has two fds (#216). Seam 4's output face was `write`, `flush`, `send_headers`, `log`, and `Request::write_stderr`, the `display_errors=stderr` form's writer, was one entry on the log, which held while every host was the CLI, whose `log_message` is `fprintf(stderr)`. php's stderr form is `fputs(stderr)` past the SAPI (`Z/main/main.c:1484`) and its log is `php_log_err_with_severity`, which goes to the `error_log` file or syslog when the entry names one and to the SAPI otherwise (`:822-860`); under a host with a file `error_log` the two part, and the one-channel sink put the stderr form in the file. The sink gains `write_stderr`, bytes as given, the `\n` inside them as php writes it; `log` keeps its entries, ended by the host; the request hands the two to the sink in the order it wrote them, since under the CLI the log form of a diagnostic stands before its display form on one fd; and the `STDERR` stream constant lands on `write_stderr` when streams exist. `Capture` is the CLI-shaped host: its `stderr` is that one fd whole and its `log` the log's part alone. Rejected: a line here saying the CLI's fd is the model, which defers the same question to the first host that writes a file and leaves `fwrite(STDERR)` a log entry. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: one diagnostic at `display_errors=stderr` and `log_errors=1` is two calls on the sink, `log` then `write_stderr`, and a sink that parts them holds the stderr form off the file.

**17. Revision 17 (2026-09-14).** Where a compile-time diagnostic that is not a fatal travels (#249). §6.1 said where a compile-time fatal lives and nothing about the diagnostics php raises while a file compiles and goes on compiling it: the NAN coercion warning a fold raises through `zend_double_to_str` (`Z/Zend/zend_operators.c:3607`) and the compile-time deprecations, `Unparenthesized ...`, `Optional parameter ... declared before required`, each a `zend_error` under `CG(in_compilation)` reaching `php_error_cb` at `zend_get_compiled_lineno`. #218 built the channel and #249 settles that it is the design's answer: the unit carries them as `Unit::diagnostics`, and the engine raises each on every request the unit serves, before the main op array runs. §3's fourth principle decides it before the first does: a unit is immutable after publication and shared by every request in the process, so a diagnostic the compiler raised is a fact about the unit and belongs on it, and every request the unit serves raises it because every request compiled it in php's world. It is also php's own answer: opcache's `persistent_script.warnings` (`Z/ext/opcache/ZendAccelerator.h:130`) records exactly this on the script and `zend_emit_recorded_errors_ex` (`Z/ext/opcache/ZendAccelerator.c:1939`) replays it on a hit; that `opcache.record_warnings` defaults off is php's concession to an optimisation this engine does not need, since there is no second compile to skip, and the oracle runs `-n` with no cache, where every request warns. `Level` moves from `api::raising` down to `php_unit` so the compiler can name it, and `api::raising` re-exports it. Rejected: the compiler handed a sink, `compile(&Parsed, &Options, &mut dyn Raise)`, the engine raising as the compiler goes, which is php's order and php with `opcache.record_warnings=0`, and makes what a request sees depend on whether it was the one that missed the cache, the shared-unit model's one forbidden shape, with a divergence row to write for a difference the engine would be wrong about; and nothing in the design, the rustdoc on `Unit::diagnostics` alone, which sends a reader of §6.1 away with nothing. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: the witness beside `serve_unit`, `echo 'a'; echo NAN . 'z';`, raises the coercion warning at line 3 before `a` is written, on the request that compiled the unit and on the one that took it from the cache alike; and `php_unit::Level` is the one `Level` the workspace declares, `api::raising::Level` its re-export.

**18. Revision 18 (2026-09-14).** §4.4's Shape table carries the syntax group (#232). #224 gave `api` its eighth group in `api.lock`'s count, `syntax`: the token stream of one source and php's names for its kinds, what `ext/tokenizer` reads for `token_get_all`, `token_name` and the `T_*` constants at the pin, reached through `php_compile::syntax` because an extension's edge stops at `php_ext` (ADR-0004) and the engine's at `php_compile` (`seams.toml`'s edges). The table named five groups and not this one, and the session that landed it left the sentence for a person, rightly; #232 settled the row on the maintainer's behalf and this revision types it, the table's opening sentence counting six. The row's `tokenize` and the kind table, nothing of the tree, is the group at #224, eleven items; between the settling and the typing, `token_get_all` under `TOKEN_PARSE` landed as php's `tokenize_parse` does it, the parser run over the scanner with the grammar's names fed back (`Z/ext/tokenizer/tokenizer.c:455`), and the group carries `parse`, `Parsed`, `Tree`, `Element` and `Diagnostic` with it. Whether the row says so is a sentence a person writes, filed with this revision. Rejected: amending the clause here to what the lock says, which is a design decision about the seam's width, whether an extension reads the tree, taken by the hand that types it. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: §4.4's table has a row for `syntax` and its opening sentence counts the rows, and `api.lock`'s header counts a `syntax` group of the size the lock carries, 28 at this revision, with `decl` and `abi` the paragraph under the table and not rows of it.

**19. Revision 19 (2026-09-15).** What `zend.max_allowed_stack_size=0` detects on the request's thread, and where the thread's size comes from (#291). #246 landed php's compiler stack limit as `php_compile::StackLimit`, built as `zend_call_stack_init` builds `EG(stack_limit)`, and where php asks the platform for the thread's bounds (`zend_call_stack_get`) the crate, forbidding `unsafe`, stood on php's fallback with a size of this engine's own sentence: the platform's default on the main thread and `std::thread`'s 2 MiB on any other. §5.6 now says what detect reads: `php_heap::call_stack_bounds`, php's `zend_call_stack_get` in the one crate that may say `unsafe`, a `heap.lock` item `php_compile::StackLimit` reads through the lock, with the macOS and Linux arms copied from `Z/Zend/zend_call_stack.c` (`pthread_get_stackaddr_np` and `pthread_get_stacksize_np`; `/proc/self/maps` under `RLIMIT_STACK` on the main thread and `pthread_getattr_np` on any other) and `php_heap::call_stack_default_size`, `zend_call_stack_default_size` arm for arm, for the thread no arm describes. The native stack is the host's thread's, and the engine sizes none: §5.14 (ledger 8.3) says a host runs each request to completion on one thread and whether it pools is invisible to the engine, so an engine that sized its request threads would be a second threading model beside the one the design has. Rejected: the engine spawning every request thread at a size of its own, the thread's entry as the base, which is that second model; and the fallback standing with the assumed sizes named, which keeps a guess where php has a measurement, and a stack overflow's fatal and the depth it fires at are both observable (§3, first principle). Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `php_compile::StackLimit::init(0, 0)` on a thread spawned at one size reads that size less `zend.reserved_stack_size`, to the page, on both platforms the oracle runs on; the witness beside `StackLimit::check` still refuses the 200,000-link chain in php's sentence with the number masked, since the number is the rlimit's under the shell that runs the two engines.

**20. Revision 20 (2026-09-17).** What `xtask crosscheck` measures (#268). §7.1 specified it as a stub diff: each extension's declaration builders run against a throwaway interner and diffed per crate against `php-src/ext/<name>/*.stub.php` at the pinned tag, tentative bit in both directions, with §4.10, §7.2, §8, §12, §13 and §14 each carrying a clause that the stubs are what the sensor reads. That diff needs a stub parser this repository does not have, and it measures the builders alone: a builder right about a declaration that the linker or `ext/reflection` then misreports passes it. php prints the whole of an extension itself, `php --re <name>` constructing a `ReflectionExtension` and printing its `__toString()` (`sapi/cli/php_cli.c:1036-1091` at the tag), every constant, ini entry, function, class, method, property and the tentative-return row, byte for byte in php's wording and order. The maintainer settled on 2026-09-14 that the rendering diff is `crosscheck`'s reading: the oracle's `--re` bytes under the recipe are the verdict, the engine's rendering through `ReflectionExtension::__toString()` over the linked tables is the claim, and the linked tables, the linker and `ext/reflection` are all under the one reading. §7.1 now says so; §4.4 says the declaration types are held from the outside by it; §4.10 says the stubs are not copied and are read by nothing; §7.2's port row completes a port against the oracle's rendering; §8's sensor row and oracle paragraph, §12's risk row, §13's note on Mahout's `native_crosscheck.rs` and §14's pin bullet drop the stubs; and the `--ri <name>` column and the binary's `--rf`, `--rc`, `--re` and `--ri` flags are named as the same sensor's later halves. Harness §7's lane row keeps the scope and cadence, per extension on every commit for the default set, and harness §14 item 4 still owes crosscheck its fixture. Rejected: the stub diff beside the rendering diff, which is two sensors for one fact where the weaker is a subset of the stronger; and the stub diff alone, which measures less than php prints for free. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -n 'stub' third-engine-design.md` finds no sentence that says a sensor reads `*.stub.php`, and the first `crosscheck` reading in `target/harness/` is per extension and holds `--re` bytes, not declarations.

**21. Revision 21 (2026-09-17).** Seam 1 hands the heap back, and the object header is php's forty bytes (#242, #282). #242 found that `Heap::release` cannot take the request as its `HeapHooks` while the request owns the heap, and settled the shape in the one form Rust admits: `HeapHooks` gained `heap(&mut self) -> &mut Heap`, an operation that can free is an associated function over the hooks alone (`Heap::release(hooks, z)`, `Heap::array_update`, `Heap::array_del`, `Heap::object_set_slot`, `Heap::call_destructors`, `Heap::free_object_storage`) that asks the hooks for the heap at each step and again after every call up, as php re-reads `EG(objects_store)` after `dtor_obj`; `Request` implements the hooks over itself through a private `Hooks<'a>(&'a mut Request)`, so no second `&mut` exists. The design said otherwise in three places. §4.3 row 1 named five methods and `Bailout`; it now names six, the sixth the heap, and `Fatal`, the type the trait returns. §5.10 said every freeing operation takes the hooks but not that it reaches the heap through them, or why; it now says both. §5.4 drew `handlers: &'static dyn ObjectHandlers`, sixteen bytes where php's pointer is eight; the header now holds a thin `&'static` payload table beside the class word, the payload after the slots rather than before the header at `handlers->offset`, the bytes the same, and the 25-slot table (#281) is reached through the class. The maintainer settled the count on 2026-09-17: naming five and hiding the sixth would make the table wrong about a `pub` trait the seam lock carries, and naming it another way would be a second vocabulary for one thing. `jit-design.md` §4 names `HeapHooks` nowhere, so nothing there moves. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `heap.lock` lists `HeapHooks::heap`, and `grep -n "dyn ObjectHandlers" third-engine-design.md` finds no line in the `Object` layout.

**22. Revision 22 (2026-09-17).** §4.4's Syntax row says what the group carries (#267). Revision 18 typed the row as #232 settled it, "`tokenize` and the kind table, nothing of the tree", the group at #224, eleven items, and said in its ledger row that cc89535 had since landed `token_get_all` under `TOKEN_PARSE` as php's `tokenize_parse` does it (`Z/ext/tokenizer/tokenizer.c:455`), the group carrying `parse`, `Parsed`, `Tree`, `Element` and `Diagnostic` with it, 28 items; whether the clause moved was a decision about the seam's width and was filed as #267. The maintainer settled it on 2026-09-17: the clause is rewritten to what `api.lock` carries. php's `tokenize_parse` runs the parser over the scanner and reads its events, so an extension that serves `TOKEN_PARSE` reads the parse by php's own design, and sending it another way would be a second reading of one thing. What the seam widens by is the parse's report and the tree's shape and diagnostics, `parse`, `Options`, `Parsed`, `Diagnostic`, `Level`, `Tree`, `Element` and `TokenId`, not the node types the compiler walks, which cross nowhere; the row now draws that line and says an extension that needs a node type is a widening of the row, not a use of it. `api.lock`'s `syntax` group stays as it is, 28 items. Rejected: the clause standing and `ext/tokenizer` reading the parser's report some other way, with `parse`, `Parsed`, `Tree`, `Element` and `Diagnostic` leaving the group, which is a second path to one parse. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `api.lock`'s header counts a `syntax` group of 28 and lists `syntax::parse`, `syntax::Parsed`, `syntax::Tree`, `syntax::Element`, `syntax::Diagnostic`, `syntax::Level`, `syntax::Options` and `syntax::TokenId` and no item named for a node type the compiler walks, and §4.4's row names each of those and says the node types cross nowhere.

**23. Revision 23 (2026-09-17).** §12's re-entry risk row reads as revision 19 leaves it (#300, #291). Revision 19 settled where a request thread's bounds come from (#291): `php_heap::call_stack_bounds` reads the calling thread's as `zend_call_stack_get` does, and the engine sizes no thread of its own, since §5.14 has a host running each request to completion on one thread and an engine sizing its request threads would be a second threading model. §12's row for synchronous destructors and native re-entry still said "main thread stack sized at startup" in its mitigation column, a sentence from before that revision and in conflict with it. The maintainer settled the column on 2026-09-17, recorded on #300, and this revision types it: the clause is struck and in its place the row says the calling thread's bounds are read at request start as `zend_call_stack_get` reads them, the limit what the thread has, the engine sizing no thread of its own, citing §5.6 and §5.14. The "fibers get their own stacks" clause stays as written: a fiber's stack is one the engine does size, and `zend_call_stack_init` runs on it as on a thread, so that half of the row was never in conflict with revision 19. **24. Revision 24 (2026-09-18).** A native class's payload is declared with the class (#360). §5.4 said the payload is one typed slot on the object header and that the heap derives its tracing, and said nothing about what puts it there: `new` made every object with `zend_objects_new`'s arithmetic, so a native constructor had no slot to write, and `ext/reflection`'s reflectors kept php's `intern->ce` and `intern->obj` nowhere and looked the class up again by name. #360 posed the two shapes and the test between them, whichever the handler table admits without a twenty-sixth slot. The table is Zend's twenty-five and `create_object` is not among them, php keeping it on `zend_class_entry` and not on `zend_object_handlers`, so the hook shape would have been a twenty-sixth slot and the declaration shape is the one taken: `ExtensionBuilder::class_payload::<T>` declares the payload beside the handler table, `new` sizes the allocation for `T` and writes `T::default()` into the slot before the constructor runs, and the constructor writes through `PhpObject::native_mut`. A subclass takes the payload as it takes the table, since php inherits `create_object`. §5.4 now says so. Rejected: a `create_object`-shaped slot on `ObjectHandlers`, which would put on the table a thing php keeps on the class entry; and a payload handed to an object already made, which no allocation has room for. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `api.lock` lists `abi::ExtensionBuilder::class_payload`, and `echo new ReflectionMethod('JsonException', 'getMessage')` prints `inherits Exception` as the oracle does.

**25. The maintainer settled it on 2026-09-20, recorded on #379, and this revision types it: php's `zend_builtin_module` is a `zend_module_entry` named `Core`, registered by `zend_startup_builtin_functions` from `zend_startup` before any extension's and asserted to take number zero (`Z/Zend/zend_builtin_functions.c:46-68`, `Z/Zend/zend.c:1058`), and four observables read it as an object rather than a name, so principle 2 makes one value made at startup the faithful model and the smaller one both. §7.1 now says so, beside the paragraph that says what the tier is not. Rejected: `Core` as a name the api answers for what no extension declared, which reproduces `getExtensionName()` and none of the other three, each a `null` or an exception where php has an object.
