**26. Revision 26 (2026-09-21).** §5.10's first shutdown walk reads as php's `shutdown_destructors` does it (#405, #280). The section stated the shutdown order as "`__destruct` on globals in symbol-table order then objects-store order", and php's first walk is not in symbol-table order: `shutdown_destructors` (`Z/Zend/zend_execute_API.c:251` at 8.5.9) is `zend_hash_reverse_apply(&EG(symbol_table), zval_call_destructor)`, the last symbol to the first, each that holds an object with a refcount of one deleted, and the walk repeated while `zend_hash_num_elements` moves, because one destructor can drop another symbol's last other owner; only the store's walk that follows is forward, in handle order. The order is observable, a program's output says which object went first, so principle 2 makes php's walk the one to copy, and `Request::shutdown_destructors` copies it: #280 pinned `$a = new A(1); $b = new A(2);` at bf5b7e3 serving `d2` then `d1` under the oracle and under the engine, where the sentence read `d1` then `d2`. The code follows php; the design of record was what stood out of step, so the design is what moves. The maintainer settled the sentence on 2026-09-20, recorded on #405, and this revision types it, with where the symbol table is destroyed in the same sentence: `zend_hash_graceful_reverse_destroy` in `zend_shutdown_executor_values`, after the extensions' `rshutdown`, which `Request::destroy_symbol_table` is. Rejected: a divergence row for the difference, which would register the design's error as the engine's. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -n 'in symbol-table order' third-engine-design.md` finds this ledger row's quotation and nothing in §5.10, and the witnesses on `Request::shutdown_destructors` pin `d2` before `d1` and the store's walk, forward, after the table's.

**27. Revision 27 (2026-09-21).** §4.9 says the tree carries every fact `zend_ast` hands `zend_compile.c` (#599). The section said the tree is `zend_ast`'s positional-children shape and that the builder records the doc comment as a token index, and stopped one sentence short of the rule those are instances of, so each fact the port of `emit` onto the tree (#595) met was decided by accident. #599 found two. A literal's value: the lexer walks `"\101"`, `"\u{1F600}"` and `0x1F` to diagnose them and produces neither the bytes nor the number, and `read.rs` decoded the token's text a second time, `number` with the bases, `_`, the exponent and the overflow to float, `escape` with `zend_scan_escape_string`'s table. A node's line: the tree carried `range` and `Parsed::lines` where php's node carries `lineno`, and `read.rs` copied the line site by site, `lines.line_of(range.start)` being the wrong answer for the multi-line cases the witnesses pin on the oplines. php produces both before its compiler runs, `zend_scan_escape_string` and `ZEND_STRTOL` with the overflow retry in the scanner's rules, `zend_ast_create_1..5` taking the first non-NULL child's line and `CG(zend_lineno)` at the reduction for a node with none, so the parser's node already holds them. The maintainer settled it on 2026-09-21, recorded on #599, as one rule, and this revision types it: the tree carries every fact `zend_ast` hands `zend_compile.c`, and `emit` reads it and re-derives none; the value answered on demand from the token as `TokenValue { Int(i64), Float(f64), Bytes(Vec<u8>) }` by the one function the lexer diagnoses with (the word is #604's: the glossary avoids "literal" under Constant expression, and `TokenValue` is php's own name for the fact, `zendlval` and `RETURN_TOKEN_WITH_VAL`), the line one more field the generator emits beside `range` and `doc_comment`, and the `zend_compile.c` resets of `CG(zend_lineno)` staying in `emit`. Rejected: the value on the token, where every token pays for what three kinds carry; the value resolved onto the node, owned bytes in the arena for a `token_get_all` caller who never asks; and `emit` computing the line from `range` and tokens, the same fold with less information than the builder holds at `Finish`. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. The two build tickets are #602, the value, and #603, the line, with #601 the research on the childless node's line that #603 reads. Check: `grep -n 'zend_ast.lineno' third-engine-design.md` finds §4.9 and this row; once #602 and #603 land, the struct the generator emits for every node kind carries `line` beside `range` and `doc_comment`, and `grep -n 'fn number\|fn escape' crates/php_compile/src/*.rs` finds no line. *Amended 2026-09-21 by #603: this row and §4.9 read "the smallest line over the children, as `zend_ast_create_from_va_list` folds it"; #601 read `Z/Zend/zend_ast.h:27-28` at the pin, where `ZEND_AST_SPEC` is 1, so the constructors compiled are `zend_ast_create_1..5` and a node's line is its first non-NULL child's in php's child order, `ZEND_AST_YIELD` and `ZEND_AST_ARRAY_ELEM` being `(value, key)` and `ZEND_AST_CLASS_CONST_GROUP` `(list, attrs, type)`, and the fold is the `#else` branch. The maintainer settled the correction on #599's amendment; the decision is untouched and the sentence follows the measurement.*

**28. Revision 28 (2026-09-22).** §5.8 says where php tests the interrupt beyond the three places this loop does, and what that leaves unheld (#614, #606). The section said the loop tests the interrupt at a back-edge, at a frame's activation and after every `Resume`, and said nothing of php testing it anywhere else; #606 lifted #601's three `for (;;)` programs into witnesses and the third of them, `set_time_limit(1); $x = 0; for (;;) if ($x) ; else` with the block on line 3, could not be pinned. Its loop holds a taken forward `JMPZ` beside the jump back, php tests `EG(vm_interrupt)` after every jump that sets an opline (`ZEND_VM_SET_OPCODE`, `Z/Zend/zend_execute.c:5770-5787`), and the pinned binary at 8.5.9 names line 3 on ten runs of twelve and line 2 on the other two: the observation is a race inside php, between the timer's signal and the jump the loop is on when it is delivered. The compiler is already right, `compile-identity --file` reading all three programs as byte-identical with the oracle's dump, so what differs is only where the check is observed. The maintainer settled it on 2026-09-22, recorded on #614, and this revision types it: the interrupt stays at a back-edge, at a frame's activation and after every `Resume`, the engine changes nothing, and §5.8 now says that the line such a timeout names is not held. Rejected: a test after every jump as `ZEND_VM_SET_OPCODE` does it, which buys a load and a branch on the hot path of the interpreter and of every compiled loop and still names a different line whenever php's race falls the other way, against principle 1's one safepoint and `jit-design.md` §4.9's one load on a cold path; and a row in `docs/divergence.toml`, which has nothing to key on — a row excuses a reading, the two deterministic programs are witnesses that agree with the oracle, the op arrays of all three are identical, and no lane reads the third at all. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `docs/divergence.toml` carries no row for the interrupt, `crates/php_syntax/tests/line.rs` keeps the third program with this sentence as its reason, and twelve runs of the pinned binary over that program name two different lines where twelve of the engine's name one.

**29. Revision 29 (2026-09-23).** §5.11 says how a fiber's stack is switched, what the switch swaps, and what a frame off the main stack is (#558, #363). The section had fibers owning a `corosensei` stack and their own VM stack pages and the entry slot switching with the stack, and said nothing of the switch itself: whether it is an assembly context switch, a `ucontext` or a thread parked per fiber; what is saved and restored across it; what a fiber's VM stack page size and call-stack limit are; and what becomes of the interrupt and of the recorder while execution is on a stack that is not the request's. #363 met the hole from the other end, `Fiber` being no class here and the four `ReflectionFiber_*.phpt` reading `Class "Fiber" not found`, and #558 asked for the sentence before #565 builds the class. php answers every part of it in `Z/Zend/zend_fibers.c` at 8.5.9, and the maintainer settled it on 2026-09-23, recorded on #558, as five sentences this revision types. The switch is boost.context's `jump_fcontext` through `zend_fiber_switch_context` (`:476`), which `corosensei` is the Rust of, over a stack of `fiber.stack_size` with one guard page and php's 2 MiB default (`:206-280`, `Z/Zend/zend.c:244-249`). What it swaps is `zend_fiber_vm_state`'s eleven fields (`:105-155`), which here are the `VmStack` the request runs on, carrying the current frame and §5.6's entry slot with it, the error-reporting level and the `StackLimit`; the rule behind the list is that a switch swaps the engine's execution state and nothing an extension or the tier owns. A fiber's VM stack grows in php's 16 KiB fiber pages and not the request's 256 KiB (`:584-588`), and `memory_get_usage()` reads it. The call-stack limit inside a fiber is the fiber's own stack, `zend_fiber_stack_base` and `zend_fiber_stack_limit` (`:337-359`), so `php_heap::call_stack_bounds` is asked per thread and never per fiber, and §12's "fibers get their own stacks" is that one stack the engine sizes. And the interrupt is not swapped, because `EG(vm_interrupt)` is not one of the eleven: one fiber's timeout is the request's, raised at that fiber's next safepoint and carried out as a `Bailout` across the switch, which is php's `ZEND_FIBER_TRANSFER_FLAG_BAILOUT` (`:627-629`, `:669-674`) without the `longjmp`. A frame off the main stack is a frame and gets no word of its own: which fiber it belongs to is a fact about the stack, as being an `execute`'s entry frame already is, so the header gains no bit, `Edge` gains no arm, and the switch, happening inside `Fiber::suspend` or `Fiber::resume`, is one `Same` to `step` like any other native. Rejected: a thread parked per fiber, which makes a request two threads' at a `suspend` reached from a destructor and contradicts §5.14; `ucontext`, which is the arm php's own build does not compile and costs a signal-mask syscall per switch; a bit in the frame header naming the fiber, which puts on every frame a fact §5.6 already keeps on the stack for the same reason; and a `Stepped` arm for the switch, which would have the recorder read a frame chain it never walks. Where php draws the line one field further out, `jit_trace_num` being Zend's and not opcache's, this engine's recording exclusion stays the tier's request state (`jit-design.md` §4.9); the difference is in which roots are recorded and in nothing a PHP program reads, and it is #627, `for-a-person`, to settle against the `TRACE start` diff once the tier records. The manifest, the registry and the locks are unchanged; `Fiber`'s own items land with #565. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: with `Fiber` built, `memory_get_usage()` across a fiber that pushes one frame moves by 16 KiB and not by 256; `zend.max_allowed_stack_size=0` with `fiber.stack_size` at its minimum refuses the recursion at the fiber's depth and the same program outside a fiber refuses at the thread's; `set_time_limit(1)` with the loop inside a fiber raises php's timeout fatal at the fiber's line and the resumer sees the request end; and a `Fiber::suspend` inside a `usort` comparator returns to its resumer with `grep -n 'is_fiber\|fiber_id' crates/php_heap/src/stack.rs` finding no line.

**30. Revision 30 (2026-09-23).** §13's `zend_sort` row and §4.1's crate map name the crate the sort is in (#570, #381). The row read "the generic half of the `zend_sort` port … | Mahout | `php_engine` kernels", written when every ported algorithm was a kernel's, and #381 landed it elsewhere: `zend_sort` and `zend_insert_sort` are written once in `crates/php_heap/src/sort.rs`, beside `compare`, under `Heap::array_sort`, which is `zend_hash_sort_ex` (`Z/Zend/zend_hash.c:3077`); `heap.lock` carries `zend_sort` and `SortEntry`, and `api` publishes `values::zend_sort` over a slice for the native that orders an index table of its own, which `array_unique` reads. php divides it the same way — `zend_sort` is `Zend/zend_sort.c`'s, below the VM, and the sort every array runs is `zend_hash.c`'s — and the array here is `php_heap`'s, so the crate that owns the buckets owns the order they end in; `compare` is already there for the matching reason, that the compiler's constant fold and the kernel have to read one ordering. The maintainer settled it on 2026-09-23, recorded on #570, and this revision types it: the row's "Into" cell reads `php_heap`, beside `compare`, its parenthetical names `Heap::array_sort` as what the live-array half became, and §4.1's `php_heap` cell names the sort and the scalar ordering, so the crate map and the manifest say one thing. Rejected: the row left standing with §4.1 alone corrected, the ticket's own second option, which leaves two sentences about one home and a reader breaking the tie with `heap.lock`; and moving the code to fit the row, which would be a second statement of `zend_sort` in the crate that does not own the buckets, the thing #381 collapsed after #325 had copied it into `ext/standard`. The manifest, the registry and the locks are unchanged. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -rn 'fn zend_sort' crates/` finds `crates/php_heap/src/sort.rs` and no second file, `heap.lock` lists `zend_sort` and `SortEntry`, and §13's row and §4.1's `php_heap` cell both say `php_heap`. The same grep for `fn double_to_string` also finds `php_heap`, where §13's `php_gcvt` row two lines below still says `php_engine` kernels; that row is #628, found while verifying this one and not corrected here, because a row a person has not settled is not this commit's to type.

**31. Revision 31 (2026-09-25).** §4.10 says what an extension exports to one that depends on it, and lists each such surface (#716, #743, #756). The section said such an export is "a deliberate `pub` surface, kept to the driver trait for `pdo` and the RNG handle for `random`", and §12's risk row and the manifest's `ext.pub-beyond-extension` note said anything beyond those two "needs a line in this document", so each new edge asked a person for a sentence: #716 for ext/pcre's `Regex`, which ext/spl's `RegexIterator` compiles through and ext/filter's `FILTER_VALIDATE_REGEXP` is to (#734); #743 for ext/standard's `strip_tags_ex` and `escape_html_entities`, which ext/filter reads, and its `Serializer` and `Unserializer`, which ext/session's handlers read; #756 for ext/standard's `md5::Md5`, `sha1::transform`, `sha1::INIT` and `crc32::update`, which #737's `HashContext` serialises as php's struct. The three ask one question, and php-src has answered it: every one of those exports exists because php's dependent extension calls the other's `PHPAPI` rather than carrying a second copy, and a second copy here drifts, the shared var hash in session serialisation, the pattern cache in `RegexIterator`, `PHP_MD5_CTX`'s serialised layout in `HashContext`. The three were ruled together on the maintainer's behalf on 2026-09-25, by the coordinating session under the maintainer's standing instruction of that day to work a `for-a-person` ticket that blocks a slot, and this revision types the ruling: an extension exports to one that depends on it exactly what php-src declares `PHPAPI` in a header the dependent includes, with the types those declarations take, and nothing more; each such surface is a row of §4.10's list, item by item, beside the header it mirrors; `api-check`'s print of `pub` items beyond `extension()` is held against the list, and an item the list does not carry is a defect rather than a question for a person. The pdo connector face and the random handle are the list's first two rows, not exceptions to it, and ext/libxml's for ext/dom (#731) is a row whose items #732 writes when it lands them. The list carries one row the ruling did not enumerate, ext/standard's `base64::encode`, `base64::decode` and `quot_print::decode` for ext/iconv (#737), which `api-check ext/standard` prints on #737's branch and which the rule itself admits, since php's `iconv.c` includes `base64.h` and `quot_print.h` for `iconv_mime_encode` and `iconv_mime_decode`. Rejected: a sentence per surface, each a person's, which makes a person the bottleneck for a fact php-src already settled; a shared helper crate under `ext/`, which §4.10 already refuses; and each dependent carrying its own port, the drift the rule exists to prevent. The manifest's `ext.pub-beyond-extension` text still names the two first rows as the only surfaces and §12 as the place, and a person rewrites it; until then this section binds, since the manifest's rule only prints. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo xtask api-check` prints, for every `ext/*` crate, no `pub` item beyond `extension()` that §4.10's list does not name, and each row's Mirrors cell names a header whose `PHPAPI` lines (`PDO_API` in pdo) are the row's items.

**32. Revision 32 (2026-09-25).** §7.2, §4.10 and §12 say where libxml2 and lexbor live and how they are linked, and what `ext/libxml` exports to `ext/dom` (#731, #732). §7.2 said "libxml, dom, xml, simplexml, xmlreader, xmlwriter: `libxml2` bindings" and "`Dom\HTMLDocument`: vendored `lexbor`", and said neither where the binding lives nor whether the library is the platform's; #731 asked it with ext/dom's ceiling. The maintainer settled the ceiling on 2026-09-24; the rest was settled on 2026-09-25 on the maintainer's behalf by the coordinating session under the maintainer's standing instruction of that day, and this revision types it: `ext/libxml` is a crate of its own that binds the platform's `libxml2`, found through `pkg-config libxml-2.0` and never vendored, in its `src/ffi/`; `ext/lexbor` is a crate of its own that vendors php-src's lexbor; both are manifest rows. The oracle reads the platform's library, 2.9.13 on the dev machine, so the engine reading the same one keeps libxml's own output, error text and serialisation the oracle's. §7.2's row and its last paragraph now say so, §12's vendored-C row names the one platform library, and §4.10 carries `ext/libxml`'s surface as the rule #716, #743 and #756 settled in revision 31 has it, one listed surface beside the header it mirrors, `php_libxml.h`: safe types over the library, so `ext/dom` holds no `unsafe` and the binding is `ext/libxml`'s alone. Rejected: libxml2 vendored and built by `cc`, the timelib answer, which would make the engine's parser a version the oracle does not run; and libxml's natives kept inside `ext/dom`, which leaves simplexml, xmlreader and xmlwriter a second copy of the error state. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -rn 'link(name = "xml2")' ext/` finds `ext/libxml/src/ffi/sys.rs` alone, `ext/dom/src/lib.rs` carries `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`, and `cargo xtask api-check` prints no `ext/libxml` item beyond `extension()` that §4.10's paragraph does not name.

**33. Revision 33 (2026-09-26).** §6.2 says which crate owns `zend_inheritance.c`'s checks (#775). The section said "the linker is `zend_inheritance.c` over `php_unit` declarations" under the compiler's heading, and the instruction for #751 said the checks go "in the engine's linker", while #751 put them in `php_unit::link`. Mitchell settled it on #775: they live in `php_unit::link`, the leaf both the compiler and the engine sit above (ADR-0001). The compiler runs them from its early bind as `zend_try_early_bind` does, and their outcome decides between an early-bound class and `DECLARE_CLASS`. A refusal there is a compile-time error. The engine runs the same function when it links at run time. Check: no crate but `php_link` carries a variance, abstract-method or `#[\Override]` check, and a class the compiler early-binds links to the same linked class the engine's run-time path produces for it.

**34. Revision 34 (2026-09-26).** Four rulings Mitchell settled on their tickets.

1. **The request owns its sink** (§4.1, §4.3 seam 4; #946). #741 made output reach the host as the request writes it. A `Request` is `'static` and `Send`, so it cannot hold the `&mut dyn OutputSink` that `serve` borrowed. #741 answered with a provided `OutputSink::held`, which leaves two modes: a held sink written as the request writes, and a kept one handed everything at the end. Mitchell chose one mode: `serve` takes the sink owned, as a `Box<dyn OutputSink + Send>`, writes every call to it as it is made, and hands it back at the end. A web host owns its connection the same way. `held` is the step that went before, and it goes with the refactor.
2. **Constant arrays stay immutable** (§5; #917, after #770's interim). Under opcache, php's own model, they are immutable too; `php -n` refcounts them per request. Per-request copies would cost every request and reverse principle 3 for a few GC and refcount observables. The row and the regime stand as the answer, not as an interim.
3. **libxml's generic error channel is bound with `c_variadic`** (§7.2; #928). The callback `xmlSetGenericErrorFunc` takes is C-variadic, which Rust can define from 1.99. Measured on 1.99.0-beta.7: a Rust callback passing its `va_list` to `vsnprintf` formats as C does. The binding lands with the toolchain bump when 1.99 is stable, not as a C shim.
4. **zlib is pure Rust first** (§7.2; #924). A pure-Rust deflate is tried first and held to the oracle's bytes at every level; the oracle links the platform's zlib 1.2.12. `libz-sys` over the platform's zlib is the fallback where the bytes cannot match.

Check: `serve`'s signature takes and returns an owned sink; a copied constant array's GC root differs from `php -n` only under that row; `@` silences a libxml generic error; `gzcompress` at every level prints the oracle's bytes.

**34. Revision 34 (2026-09-26).** `php_macros` is designed: what the three attributes declare, what they expand to, and what the crate depends on (§2.1, §4.1, §4.2, §4.4–§4.7, §4.10, §5.4, §7.1, §8, §11, §13; ADR-0007).
- **Why.** The crate was a pass-through, and the port grew four ways of declaring a native without it: startup stub parsers, six copies of about 2,300 lines, which §4.10 and principle 10 rule out; per-crate declaration DSLs; hand-built declarations; and about four lines of argument reading per native in some 1,660 bodies. The stub readers also rendered defaults wrong (`SplTempFileObject`'s `= 2`, `round`'s `<default>`).
- **The merge.** Two proposals were merged: one with a working prototype, one with a stub census, an analysis of Mahout's overrides, probes of what rustc hands an attribute macro, and an adversarial review. Mitchell settled the merge's nine decisions on 2026-09-26, and a validation pass for modern Rust amended three of them. This revision types the result.
- **Types are traits.** Every fact a Rust type says is an item of `FromArgument` or `IntoReturn`, and the macro matches no type by name. Names are identifiers, `r#` stripped.
- **The entry is php's ZPP** over `coerce::argument`, the declaration-driven coercion the engine already had.
- **Defaults.** A default is php's constant expression, written as tokens read the way php's lexer reads them, and evaluated by the engine in the declaring class's scope when an argument is absent. The earlier sentence, resolved once at startup, could not hold an enum case, which is a request object.
- **Escape hatches are types:** `Raw<T>`, `Passed<T>`, `Untyped<T>`, `arity = manual`, so no string overrides a signature.
- **Classes** are a header on a struct or enum plus one member impl, with a shared payload spelled `payload = T` and body-less members for interfaces, abstract methods and implementation aliases.
- **Keys.** PHP visibility is a key, and the tentative bit is `#[php(tentative)]`.
- **Witness coverage** counts a native by its attribute, and a class witness covers the methods its fences call.
- **The model gains three facts, each a present divergence from the oracle:**
  - an integer's radix, since `mkdir`'s `= 0777` renders `= 511` today;
  - `not_serializable`, since `serialize(new ReflectionClass(..))` is not refused;
  - a property's `virtual` bit, since `DOMNode::$nodeName` answers `isVirtual()` false.
- **ADR-0007.** `php_macros` depends on no workspace crate and `php_engine` depends on it, so Core's natives use the attributes. ADR-0007 supersedes ADR-0002's edge sentence and ADR-0004's last consequence, whose facade stands on its first reason.
- **The api surface.** It grows by the traits, the vocabulary, `ExtensionBuilder::class` and `PhpObject::native_mut_or_fatal`. The helpers only an expansion names sit in `api::__private`, listed but counted apart. §4.4 now defers to the manifest's ceiling instead of restating a number the manifest had already moved past.
- **Rejected:**
  - signatures as strings, parsed at compile time, because Mahout's `sig =` strings drifted from their bodies 274 times;
  - defaults evaluated in Rust, because Rust reads `0777` as decimal 777;
  - Rust visibility as PHP visibility, which gives one spelling two meanings;
  - `properties = Struct`, `class_name!` and a `Native` trait per function, each a second place for a fact the member impl or the sibling module already carries;
  - finding a class's members by path resolution with a blanket fallback, which a probe showed can drop them silently;
  - the `php_macros → php_engine` edge, a host rebuild of the engine and a cycle with Core.
- **The manifest.** A person makes the changes: the edges and the `unit` seam's consumers (ADR-0007); `api`'s ceiling, the named `__private` exemption with its own cap, and the `api.lock-line` rule text, which still says "≤ 400"; and the rule that no `ext/*` source names `__private`. Until then this document binds, and `api-check` fails the first commit that adds the items.
- **Check:**
  - The prototype declares 29 php declarations through the attributes (`explode`, `htmlspecialchars`, `round`, `trim`, `array_push`, `sscanf`, `implode` and `join`, `RoundingMode`, `php_user_filter`, `Dom\AdjacentPosition`, the `Spl` constructors and more), and their Reflection renderings are byte-identical to the oracle's except `mkdir`'s radix.
  - A script over them is byte-identical to the oracle's output: arity errors, `TypeError`s, the null deprecation, named arguments over constant and enum-case defaults, `UNKNOWN` skipped by name, by-reference writes, and an array changed in place.
  - After migration, `grep -rln 'fn stub\|struct Reader' ext/*/src crates/php_engine/src` finds nothing, and `crosscheck` over every default extension is byte-equal or strictly closer than before.

**35. Revision 35 (2026-09-27).** §7.2 says how posix and pcntl reach the OS, and where they run. Mitchell settled it on 2026-09-27, working through the waiting decisions with the coordinator session. Each is a crate of its own, `ext/posix` and `ext/pcntl`, behind its feature and enabled for the `php` binary and the phpt lane alone, never the server hosts: php builds pcntl for cli, and a `fork()` in a process that serves requests on threads keeps the calling thread and whatever locks the others held. Both bind through `nix`, with raw `libc` in `src/ffi/` for the four calls `nix` lacks and the oracle has (`times`, `getlogin`, `ctermid`, `getpriority`/`setpriority`), so about six to eight `unsafe` sites instead of about sixty-five and one type system for pids, uids, signals and errno. `rustix` was measured and set aside for now: it declines `fork`, `sigaction`, `sigprocmask`, the `set*id` family and the user database as libc's to own, which leaves it two safe calls here at the price of a second set of types and three more `Cargo.lock` packages. Signals reach the VM only through `Request::interrupt` (§5.8), which a handler sets and nothing else does. The `[budget] lock_packages` ceiling moves from 120 to 135 in the same settlement, `nix` taking three of the fifteen. Kept in view: the compiled tier brings `rustix` (§4.1's `jit` set, "`rustix` or `memmap2`"); when it lands, whether posix's and pcntl's safe calls move onto it, so the workspace holds one syscall crate, is a question for that revision, not a change now. Check: `ext/pcntl` and `ext/posix` depend on `nix` and `libc` and on nothing else outside the facade; `grep -rn unsafe ext/pcntl/src ext/posix/src` finds sites only under `src/ffi/`; a server host's build carries neither feature.

**36. Revision 36 (2026-09-27).** §7.2's rows for sockets and phar, settled by Mitchell on #1078 and #1086 working through the waiting decisions with the coordinator session. ext/sockets joins the bundle as a row of its own, bound as revision 35 bound posix and pcntl, `nix` with `libc` in `src/ffi/` for what `nix` lacks, so the three socket and process extensions share one binding; it sits behind a `sockets` feature, since php builds it only with `--enable-sockets`, and needs no binary-only gate, since sockets are sound in a process that serves requests on threads. It is what lets #936's `socket.tcp_nodelay` be witnessed, through `socket_get_option`. ext/phar moves from month two into the bundle now, a port of php-src's own C whose compression rides on `ext/zlib` and whose signatures on `ext/hash` and `ext/openssl`, as php's do; bz2 is not in the bundle, so what needs it stays named in phar's spec. The manifest rows (budgets 5,000 and 14,000) and oracle.toml's `[regime.small-stack]` (#1080) landed with `xtask steer` as additions in the same settlement. Check: `ext/sockets` depends on `nix` and `libc` and nothing else outside the facade, with `unsafe` only under `src/ffi/`; `php --re phar` and `php --re sockets` are held byte-equal by crosscheck once each crate lands.

**37. Revision 37 (2026-09-27).** §5.8 lists the fourth site at which the loop tests the interrupt (#1062, #1079). The section named three, a back-edge `JMP*`, frame activation and after every `Flow::Resume`, and said the loop tests nowhere else; #1054 added a fourth when it delivered pcntl's asynchronous signals, the check php makes after an internal call returns, because without it a signal a native sends its own process (`posix_kill(posix_getpid(), …)`, a child's signal taken during `pcntl_waitpid`) runs its handler only at the next back-edge or activation, where php runs it before the next opline. php has that site twice: `ZEND_VM_FCALL_INTERRUPT_CHECK` in the `DO_ICALL`, `DO_FCALL_BY_NAME` and `DO_FCALL` handlers, and `zend_call_function`'s own check after an internal function, which is where a native a native called (`array_map('posix_kill', …)`, `iterator_apply`) takes it. The engine has both, the loop's call handlers and `call_function`, each with the call still on the stack. Mitchell accepted the site on #1062 on 2026-09-27, and this revision types it; `php_engine::interrupt`'s docs, `Request::interrupt`'s and the glossary's Interrupt say the same four. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. The cost is php's: one relaxed load after a native returns, which a request whose interrupt nobody raised pays and nothing more. Check: `grep -rn 'interrupt::check\|interrupt::interrupted' crates/php_engine/src` finds the back-edge handlers, the activation and the resume in `tier.rs`, the call handler and `call_function`, and nothing else; the witness beside `Request::checked_after_call` passes, a handler that throws out of a native a native called.

**38. Revision 38 (2026-09-27).** §5.8 says where a signal is recorded, split by role (#1062, #1079). #1054 put the whole record in `php_heap::signal`: the pool, the wake list, the `extern "C"` handler an extension installed and the `siginfo_t` decoding, with a `libc` type in a crate the design gives the memory model, and `api` re-exported the handler as `request::signal_handler`. Mitchell weighed three homes on #1062 and settled on 2026-09-27 the second, option B: `php_heap` holds a policy-free mechanism, a pool generic over a `Copy` record with higher-order `post(fill)` and `take(want, each)` and a wake set raising registered interrupts, no item naming a signal; `php_engine` holds the policy, all safe, the static pool, which numbers a request takes and the dispatch at the interrupt check; `ext/pcntl/src/ffi/` holds the `extern "C"` handler and the `siginfo_t` decoding beside `nix`'s `sigaction`. The seam set is unchanged: `heap.lock` trades `signal::*` for `post::*`, and `api.lock` drops `signal_handler` and `Posted` for `SIGNAL_POST`, the post alone, and `Siginfo`. Rejected: the record in `php_heap` as built, a signal handler in the memory model; the whole of it in `php_engine`, `unsafe` in a crate that has none; and a `php_process` crate for process-wide state, which signals and `LC_CTYPE` are the only instances of today, and which the pool can move into unchanged if more arrives. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -n signal crates/php_heap/heap.lock` finds nothing, `grep -rn unsafe crates/php_engine/src/signal.rs` finds nothing, `grep -rn 'extern "C" fn' ext/pcntl/src` finds the handler under `src/ffi/` alone, and the pcntl witnesses pass.

**39. Revision 39 (2026-09-28).** §7.2 gains a row for bz2, settled by Mitchell on #1107 working through phar's divergences with the coordinator session. phar's `php --ri` differs from the oracle's where the oracle links bz2; the ruling is to build ext/bz2 now rather than record the difference. Its binding takes revision 34's rule for zlib: a pure-Rust bzip2 first, held to the oracle's bytes, the platform's libbz2 only where none matches. It sits behind a `bz2` feature, as php builds it `--with-bz2`, enabled for the php binary and the phpt lane. The manifest row (budget 2,500) and oracle.toml's `[regime.phar-writable]` landed with `xtask steer` in the same settlement; the two trace rows #1107 proposed (`phar-zip-dos-time`, `phar-stat-owner-masks`) were refused, to be fixed. Check: `ext/bz2`'s compressed bytes equal the oracle's at every block size and work factor, witnessed; `php --ri phar` reports bz2 enabled.

**40. Revision 40 (2026-09-28).** §5.14 says where a request holds a named locale (#1114), settled by Mitchell working through the waiting decisions with the coordinator session. php's `setlocale` changes the process's locale, safe under one request per process; here a request's locale is a per-request `locale_t` from `newlocale()`, installed on its thread with `uselocale()` while it runs, so the natives that read `LC_NUMERIC`, `LC_COLLATE` and the rest run the C library's own locale-aware code, php's output, and no request sees another's. Rejected: reading the locale's data into Rust and reimplementing collation, which drifts from the C library php calls; and the process-wide `setlocale` php itself uses, which leaks one request's locale into every other in a threaded host (principle 3). Check: under `setlocale(LC_ALL, "de_DE")` a request's `sprintf("%.2f", 3.14)` prints `3,14` while a concurrent request in C prints `3.14`; `asort(..., SORT_LOCALE_STRING)` under `fr_FR` matches the oracle's order.

**41. Revision 41 (2026-09-28).** §4.4 gains the process group and §4.10 says what it is not, settled by Mitchell on #1128. The process's real user and group ids and its supplementary groups were bound three times, in ext/standard for `php_stat`'s permission masks, in ext/posix for `posix_getuid` and its kin, and in ext/phar for `phar_fancy_stat`'s masks (#1117), because §4.10 admits an export between extensions only where php-src declares it `PHPAPI`, and these are `<unistd.h>` calls. The ruling is one engine facet: the engine owns the process's identity as it owns the signal record (ADR-0005, engine policy on the api), `api::process::{uid, gid, groups}`, bound once in `php_heap`'s `unsafe` home, since `nix` has no `getgroups` on Apple's targets; ext/standard, ext/posix and ext/phar call it and their copies are deleted, and ext/session's real uid is read through it too (#1131). Process facts the engine exposes are not an extension-to-extension export: no row of §4.10's list carries them and no extension binds them. Rejected: three one-call bindings as the accepted cost of §4.10's rule, one fact in three places; and an export from ext/posix, which php-src does not declare. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -rn 'getuid\|getgid\|getgroups' ext crates --include='*.rs'` finds one binding, `crates/php_heap/src/process.rs`, beside prose and the natives that name them; `api.lock` carries `process::uid`, `process::gid` and `process::groups`; the facet's witness and `php_stat`'s mask witness pass.

**42. Revision 42 (2026-09-28).** §4.4 says where the `open_basedir` guard lives, settled by Mitchell on #1171. `open_basedir` was unenforced: `Request::fs_guard` answered `Ok(())`, the ini entry was unregistered, and about 60 tests need it (47 in `tests/security`). php checks in `php_check_open_basedir_ex` (`main/fopen_wrappers.c`) at 62 sites for this bundle, 18 in the plain files and glob wrappers, temporary files and two ini handlers, and 44 in natives that reach no wrapper, and those differ on a refusal (`glob()` and the `posix_*` path natives refuse silently, `ini_set` rejects the value), so where the check runs is observable. The ruling is both halves: the check runs where php runs it, with php's one bit of whether it warns, and it is the only constructor of `HostPath`, whose rustdoc already said it was a path after the request's `cwd` and `open_basedir` had been applied while its field was public and `chdir()` built one by hand at the site php checks. `fs_guard` and `PathIntent` go. Rejected: the guard once on the stream-opening path alone, which changes the output of the 48 sites that never reach a wrapper; and php's call-site placement with `HostPath` left constructible anywhere, where a missed site is silent. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `HostPath`'s field is private and `grep -rn 'HostPath(' ext crates --include='*.rs'` finds only the guard's constructor; `cargo xtask phpt --subset tests` meets the `tests/security` open_basedir tests the oracle meets; #1203's witnesses of a warned and a silent refusal pass.

**43. Revision 43 (2026-09-28).** §5.6 says which of two stack addresses a recursive walk asks, settled by Mitchell on #1217. #1194 found that `compare_arrays`, `json_encode` and a dozen walks recursed with no check and aborted the process on a deep nest. php guards some walks with `zend_call_stack_overflowed` (`execute_ex`'s entry, the compiler, object comparison, `var_dump()`'s properties, `serialize()`, `json_encode()`) and leaves the rest to the machine (`zend_hash_compare`, `print_r`, `var_export`, the `*_recursive` array natives), where a nest past the thread's end faults. Guarding every walk at the limit would refuse nests php takes whole under a small `zend.max_allowed_stack_size`; guarding none would let one request's fault end every request of the process, which threads cannot afford (principle 3, §10). The ruling is the floor beside the limit: guarded walks ask the limit, unguarded ones ask the floor, and the one difference left, php's SIGSEGV against a catchable `Error`, is the accepted row `trace:stack-floor-refusal`. Rejected: the limit for every walk, which diverges on nests php completes; and no guard on php's unguarded walks, a process abort under thread-per-request. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: every walk that `grep -rn 'call_stack_end\|StackLimit' crates ext --include='*.rs'` finds asks exactly one of the two; `ops::tests::a_nesting_deeper_than_the_thread_is_refused_at_its_floor`, `var_dump_and_define_are_refused_at_the_floor` and the ext/standard and ext/filter capture tests pass, each exiting 139 under the oracle.

**44. Revision 44 (2026-09-28).** §6.1 says no optimizer runs on an executed op array, §12 names php's optimizer as outside parity, and §5.7's optimizer-only opcodes are kept for an optimizer only if one is ever built, settled by Mitchell on #1220. 61 tests of `ext/opcache/tests` pin php's optimizer: 60 post-optimizer or DFA dumps and `optimize_static_002`, whose static-variable reference php's DCE elides. `Zend/Optimizer` at the tag is 20,425 lines, 14,197 of them its SSA stack, and ablating one pass at a time at the oracle shows 54 of the 58 measured tests need an SSA pass (DFA 52, DCE 29, SCCP 27), so a partial port buys four. What executes is what `compile-identity` holds, the compiler's op arrays before `zend_optimize_script`, and the compiled tier reads the same fact. The ruling is the principle's: no pass of the engine's own over executed op arrays; if an optimizer is ever built it is a port of `Zend/Optimizer` at publication, in its own crate, held to php's `0x20000` dump; and the 61 tests are one registry row whose `covers` lists them (harness §4.3). Rejected: porting the optimizer now, 20k lines for 61 tests with no observable gain beyond them; a partial port, which holds four; and 61 rows of one reason, three times the registry's day-30 target. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -rn 'optimiz' crates --include='*.rs'` finds no pass that rewrites a published op array; `compile-identity` holds at `0x10000`; the registry's one optimizer row covers the 61 ids and `phpt --subset ext/opcache` reads them diverged.

**45. Revision 45 (2026-09-28).** §6.3 says how the unit cache is keyed and when a hit is validated, settled by Mitchell on #1228. The cache keyed a file by its path with the mtime to the nanosecond and the size, and validated every hit, so `opcache.validate_timestamps=0` and `opcache.revalidate_freq` did nothing: at the oracle, `opcache_invalidate_deleted_file.phpt` serves a deleted file under `validate_timestamps=0`, and at php's default `revalidate_freq=2` a rewritten file is served stale inside the window. The ruling is php's: the key is the path, the whole-second stamp and the next revalidation time live on the held file, and opcache answers whether a hit is validated through the one hook the blacklist becomes, so `php_engine` reads no ini name; the unit itself is never changed, a failed validation replacing it for the next holder (principle 4). Rejected: validating every hit whatever the settings, which differs from php-fpm at its defaults; and the settings read by the engine, which puts opcache's policy in `php_engine`. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `opcache_invalidate_deleted_file.phpt` meets its expectation; a file rewritten inside `revalidate_freq` is served stale as the oracle serves it; `api.lock` carries `admission` and not `blacklist`.

**46. Revision 46 (2026-09-29).** §4.1 lays the hosts out as the extensions are: one crate per SAPI under `sapi/`, each its binary, with `php_sapi` as their facade over `php_engine`'s host face (ADR-0008), settled by Mitchell on #1248 after ruling that full parity means every SAPI is supported (#1164, #1165). php-src's SAPIs measure cli 6,641 lines of C with the `-S` server, cgi 2,118, fpm 11,284, phpdbg 10,795, litespeed 6,088, apache2handler 1,313, fuzzer 921 and embed 210; one crate of 5,000 lines could not hold fpm and phpdbg under an honest ceiling, would give fpm's process manager and phpdbg's terminal one `unsafe` home, and would build one binary where php-src builds one per SAPI with its own extensions. The contract stays revision 3's request description and sink; `php_sapi` holds only what php-src shares between SAPIs; `sapi/*/src/ffi/` are the new `unsafe` homes; the `php` crate folds into `sapi/cli`. Rejected: every host in one crate; hosts on `php_engine` directly; a trait per host. Check: `cargo xtask api-check` finds no `sapi/*` edge to `php_engine`, `api` or `ext/*`; each `sapi/*` row carries its `sapi/<name>/tests` subset and a budget by #727's rule; the binaries `php`, `php-cgi`, `php-fpm` and `phpdbg` each build from their own crate.

**47. Revision 47 (2026-09-29).** §7.2 and §12 settle mbstring's binding, settled by Mitchell on #1173. libmbfl is php-src's fork (`libmbfl/README.md`), LGPL-2.1 (`libmbfl/LICENSE`, `README.REDIST.BINS:2`), 19,510 lines of conversion logic and 39,848 of tables, compiled into php statically; its php 8 form includes `zend.h`. The ruling is php's: vendor the C byte for byte under its licence, bound through a Zend shim, and port the PHP-licensed `mbstring.c` beside it, so the 79 encodings, the illegal-character rules and the kana table are the oracle's own bytes and a pin bump is a re-copy. A crate that vendors or translates LGPL code names it in its `license` field, which reaches `ext/bcmath`'s port of `libbcmath` too. Rejected: `encoding_rs`, which has no counterpart for 45 of the 79 encodings and maps 15 more to WHATWG's tables, at least 136 of the 416 tests the oracle runs; a Rust port of libmbfl, which LGPL §0 still counts as the library's derivative and which re-ports php-src's fork at every tag; a shared library, which is kept as the answer if a binary-only release is ever ruled. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `ext/mbstring/libmbfl` is byte-identical to the pinned tree's (`diff -r`); `cargo metadata` shows `php_ext_mbstring` and `php_ext_bcmath` with LGPL in their `license`; `mb_list_encodings()` under the engine equals the oracle's 79; `cargo xtask phpt --subset ext/mbstring` reads its misses against the 416.

**48. Revision 48 (2026-09-30).** §4.1's `sapi/*` row says how the process title reaches the engine, settled by Mitchell on #1175 as that ticket recommended. `cli_set_process_title` and `cli_get_process_title` rewrite the memory `ps` shows the command line from (`Z/sapi/cli/ps_title.c`), which only the host can reach: php's CLI takes the argv strings and the environment strings after them in `save_ps_args` before it reads its arguments, and registers the two natives as its `additional_functions`, which `php_module_startup` puts under `standard`. The ruling keeps that split: the host saves the area in its `src/ffi/`, the `unsafe` home revision 46 grants, and hands it on the request description as one value; the engine owns the natives, registered under `standard` for a host that asks, and `PHP_CLI_PROCESS_TITLE` says whether the request holds an area. The engine needs no `unsafe` and no lock: the area is written a byte at a time atomically, so every request of the built-in server may hold it at once. Rejected: the natives in `sapi/cli` through `php_ext`, an edge ADR-0008 refuses; ruling the natives out, which leaves three tests php's default CLI meets. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `sapi/cli/tests/cli_set_process_title_basic`, `cli_get_process_title_basic` and `cli_process_title_unix` meet their expectations; `unsafe-audit` finds the save in `sapi/cli/src/ffi/` and none in `php_engine` outside `php_heap`; `(new ReflectionFunction('cli_set_process_title'))->getExtensionName()` is `standard` under the `php` binary.

**49. Revision 49 (2026-09-30).** §1 states parity as the engine's verdict equal to the oracle's on every test the oracle runs, and §12's cut order gives way to the rulings, settled by Mitchell on #1161 and #1243. §1 held each suite to a share of its whole directory (`Zend/tests` ≥ 99.7%, `tests/` 100%, `sapi/cli` ≥ 95%, a default `./configure`'s extensions ≥ 90%), so about 2,380 tests the oracle skips on this machine and the ones it misses sat in the denominator beside the engine's misses, a share below 100% was a difference no one had written a reason for, and the set it named was neither the oracle's build nor what #1166, #1238, #1164 and #1165 ruled. The target is what upstream's `run-tests.php` has the oracle meet, with the keg's `php-cgi`, `php-fpm` and `phpdbg` pinned beside `php`; an exception is a registry row; the extensions ruled out are one row; and the set of extensions parity counts is the oracle's build, less the rulings §12 names, so mbstring, curl and openssl are in (#1238) and the cut order no longer lists curl. Rejected: the per-suite bars re-pointed at the oracle's run, which keeps an unwritten allowance; the bars kept beside the sentence as milestones, two definitions of one word; the runner's own control as the target, which shrinks parity by whatever the runner cannot host; a manifest key for the ruled-out suites, a grammar change for what one row does. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: every test a subset's kept target holds and the engine misses is named on that subset's reading, and a target test whose id an accepted row holds reads met whatever the engine did; `grep -n 'default \`./configure\`' third-engine-design.md` finds §4.10's feature defaults and this row, and no definition of parity; `oracle.toml`'s `[sapi]` pins `php-cgi`, `php-fpm` and `phpdbg` by digest.

**50. Revision 50 (2026-09-30).** §5.13 says what reaches `mm` and at which size, settled by Mitchell on #1170. The allocator was `zend_alloc`'s arithmetic, but a closure was charged 96 bytes where php charges 384, or 760 with a `use`; a `DateTime` 80 against 320; a generator 224 against 416. A native's buffers, the URL rewriter's among them, lived on the system allocator. So `gh12073`'s 10,000 closures stayed under 2M and `gh15179`'s 20 MB pair under 64M, where php reaches both. The ruling is principle 1's: sizes as php asks for them, the payload inside php's allocation, and payload buffers on the request heap, with no `api` item that reports a number. Rejected: one `phpt` row whose `covers` grows with every payload class the port adds; and a thread-local heap for timelib's `emalloc`, which §5.14 forbids. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `gh12073.phpt` and `gh15179.phpt` meet their expectations; every `#[php_class(payload = …)]` carries a `memory_get_usage()` witness of its `new` that passes against the oracle; `grep -rn 'Vec<Value>' crates/php_engine/src/api/values.rs` finds no closure field.

**51. Revision 51 (2026-09-30).** §5.8 says the debugging host is a `Tier`, settled by Mitchell on #1308 (the ruling named §5.6; the `execute::<T: Tier>` paragraph is §5.8's). #1165 ruled phpdbg into parity, and php's phpdbg sees each opline through a loop of its own in place of `zend_execute_ex`, principle 1's second loop. The debugger is an adapter of the seam revision 7 designed, shaped as the recorder: `safepoint` at its three sites is the gate, `step` is the loop that sees every handler, watchpoints are compared between steps where php compares them, and the debugger lives in `sapi/phpdbg` over the tier face `php_sapi` re-exports, which opens `tier.lock` (`jit-design.md` revision 8). Rejected: php's second loop; a per-opline flag in the interpreter; `mprotect` watchpoints, a process-global fault handler in a process whose requests share it on threads; the debugger's policy in `php_engine`. ADR-0006 stands; nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `sapi/phpdbg/tests` read their misses against the 71 under the engine's `phpdbg`; G1 holds `execute::<Nop>`; `api-check` finds no `sapi/phpdbg` edge to `php_engine`.

**52. Revision 52 (2026-09-30).** §5.11's fiber switch saves the running context's trace number, the tier's recording exclusion, as php's saves `EG(jit_trace_num)`, settled by Mitchell on #627 (`jit-design.md` revision 9). Revision 29 left the exclusion as the tier's own request state outside the switch and named the difference as `TRACE start` lines only; it is also liveness: a fiber suspended during a recording and never resumed left the request unable to record a root for the rest of its life. The number is one word per execution context that the engine stores and the tier alone interprets, so revision 29's rule, the switch swaps the engine's execution state and nothing an extension or the tier owns, stands. Rejected: the bit in the tier's request state with a fourth `trace` registry row. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `jit-design.md` revision 9's.

**53. Revision 53 (2026-09-30).** §4.4 organises `php_ext`'s request face as capability views by area, each with its own ceiling, settled by Mitchell on #697 and amending what #684 settled. #684 ruled that a ceiling reached is met by restructuring the face and not by raising it; since then `api_items` has been raised from 480 to 900, api.lock holds 898 of them, and `Request` carries 185 items as one flat surface, so the sentence described what did not happen. The views are for structure: the face found by area, its rustdoc grouped by area, an area's invariants in its own module, and growth read per area. They wrap the whole `&mut Request`, because a stream, an error handler or a destructor can run PHP code, so split borrows are not a goal, and they do not lower the item count. Once they exist, each area's ceiling is its measured count and the headroom a person sets, and `api_items` retires, since a total over the areas restates them. Rejected: one cap raised by a person, which leaves the face flat; area traits on `Request`, namespacing by traits with one implementor; keeping `api_items` beside the areas, two statements of one budget. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check, once #696 lands: every item of `api.lock` under `request` sits in one area, `seams.toml` carries a ceiling per area, and `api-check` reports each area against its own.

**54. Revision 54 (2026-09-30).** §12 rules the server-bound suites and their extensions out of parity, settled by Mitchell on #1167. Without a server the oracle skips 412 of mysqli's 449 tests, 161 of pdo_mysql's 167, 99 of pgsql's 102, about 61 of pdo_pgsql's 65, 84 of ldap's 141 and nearly all of snmp's, odbc's, pdo_odbc's and pdo_dblib's. Service containers exist only on Linux runners, where the macOS keg the pin names cannot run (#424); on hosted macOS each service would be installed and seeded on every run, on an account whose jobs are being refused (#596); and a server on CI alone would make the oracle's verdict depend on the machine that took the reading. The roughly 80 tests of these extensions that run without a server are held by `phpt:ruled-out-extensions` beside #1166's outs, one row for one reason. Rejected: services in the oracle job now, which enter about 820 tests no crate can meet; the suites in with their servers skipped, which leaves the server-free tests in the target unowned. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: under `cargo xtask phpt --engine <oracle> --subset ext/pdo_mysql` the oracle skips at least 161 of 167 on the runner and on the pin machine alike; no workflow under `.github/workflows` carries `services:` or starts a database; the parity-extensions chart's *Out of scope* names the nine suites.

**55. Revision 55 (2026-09-30).** §4.10 says where a persistent resource lives, §4.4's state table and principle 12 gain the pool, and §12 no longer defers persistent connections, settled by Mitchell on #1172. php keeps `pfsockopen`'s, `STREAM_CLIENT_PERSISTENT`'s and `PDO::ATTR_PERSISTENT`'s handles in `EG(persistent_list)`, one list per fpm process or per ZTS thread, reached by key. Within a request the second ask answers the same resource (`bug54623`), the type is `persistent stream` (`gh14780`, `gh22617`) and `fclose` ends it for every holder; the next request on the process reuses it after a liveness check. The ruling is a pool the engine owns, lent to one request at a time, which is the list's own discipline under both of php's models, so what any one request observes is php's. ADR-0005 named the pool as a later decision, and nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR; ADR-0005 carries an amendment line. Rejected: a request-scoped list, which holds the phpt but opens a connection per request under fpm, `-S` and `cgi -b` where php reuses one, and drops a PDO session's state between requests where php keeps it; one connection shared by concurrent requests, which php never does; and a divergence row, which leaves `stream` where php prints `persistent stream` within one request. Check: `bug54623.phpt` and `gh14780.phpt` meet their expectations, and `gh22617.phpt` does on Linux; an engine test serving two requests in turn on one `Engine` sees one accept on its listener and two concurrent requests see two; `api.lock` carries `Pool`; `grep -rn 'Mutex\|RwLock' ext --include='*.rs'` finds none.

**56. Revision 56 (2026-10-01).** §4.4 says where an extension reads the locale name a request set, settled by Mitchell on #718 (2026-09-28). php's `pcre_get_compiled_regex_cache_ex` keys its cache by `BG(ctype_string)` before the pattern while `setlocale(LC_CTYPE, ...)` has set a locale other than C, and compiles under `pcre2_maketables` built in that locale, so `\w` matches `\xe4` after `setlocale(LC_CTYPE, "de_DE")` (`Z/ext/pcre/php_pcre.c:576`). The name is ext/standard's in php, which `php_pcre.c` includes, and here it lived in ext/standard's request state. The ruling makes it engine policy beside the request's `locale_t` (revision 40), read through the request's environment facet. Rejected: the edge `ext/pcre → ext/standard`, which ADR-0004 allows but which makes the name a `pub` item of ext/standard beyond `extension()` that §4.10 does not list. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: ext/pcre's `locales.phpt` and `ctype_back_to_c.phpt` meet their expectations; `api.lock` carries the locale names under the request's environment; no `ext/*` crate exports a locale item.

**57. Revision 57 (2026-10-01).** §4.1 and §4.3 say where a php.ini is found and read, and §7.1 what an `extension=` line does with no loader, settled by Mitchell on #1176 (2026-09-29 and 2026-10-01). The design said *whether* (§11's "php.ini discovery", the CLI's "ini discovery") and not *where* once ADR-0008 made cli, cgi, fpm and phpdbg separate hosts, all of which run php's one `php_init_config` (`Z/main/php_ini.c:409-723`). The search is `php_sapi`'s, one function every host calls with its own facts (the SAPI's name for `php-<sapi>.ini`, the binary's path, `-c`, `-n`, whether the cwd is searched); the reading is the engine's one grammar, fed through the builder after the host's defaults and before its `ini_entries`, php's order. Nothing reads a php.ini it was not handed, so `Engine::builder()` is `-n` by construction for xtask's in-process engine, every `Capture` test and an embedder. An `extension=` or `zend_extension=` line runs php's path building for its kind and fails as a load fails, with php's warning naming the paths it tried, since there is no loader; the reason text is `trace:ini-extension-no-loader`. Rejected: the whole of `php_init_config` in the engine behind a builder method, which puts `getenv("PHPRC")` and a directory scan inside `build()` with an opt-out every non-host caller must remember; a copy per host, which ADR-0008's shared half exists to prevent; and silence for `extension=` as php without `HAVE_LIBDL`, which leaves an operator no signal. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `sapi/cli/tests/023`, `bug74600`, `ext/standard/tests/ini_info/php_ini_loaded_file`, `php_ini_scanned_files` and `tests/basic/bug71273` meet their expectations; `grep -rn 'PHPRC\|PHP_INI_SCAN_DIR' crates/php_engine` finds no read of the environment.

**58. Revision 58 (2026-10-01).** §4.3 says a tier declares whether it hooks the executor and the engine compiles under that declaration, settled by Mitchell on #1349. php's `zend_get_call_op` picks `DO_UCALL` and `DO_FCALL_BY_NAME` only while `zend_execute_ex == execute_ex`, and `DO_ICALL` only while `zend_execute_internal` is unset (`Z/Zend/zend_compile.c:3919-3949`), so under phpdbg, which replaces `zend_execute_ex`, every user call and every call by name is `DO_FCALL`, and `print exec` and opcache's dumps show it (`sapi/phpdbg/tests/print_001.phpt`). The condition is php's by name, "the executor is hooked", a compile option `EngineBuilder::tier` sets for a tier that declares it; the units of one engine stay shared, and a debugging host has its own engine. The opcode choice is observable, so the dump shows what runs (principle 2), and the same switch serves any later tier that replaces the executor, as phpdbg and xdebug's step debugger do in php; an observer changes no call opcode (`zend_get_call_op`, `Z/Zend/zend_compile.c:3919-3949`, reads `zend_execute_ex` and `zend_execute_internal` alone) and is seam 9's. Rejected: the dump alone renaming the calls, which would list op arrays the engine did not compile; and a divergence row for `print_001`. ADR-0006 stands: the seam is still one method on `execute`, and the declaration is read at build, not at a safepoint; nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `print_001.phpt` under the engine's `phpdbg` lists `DO_FCALL` where php does; `compile-identity` over the per-commit corpus is unchanged under `Nop`.

**59. Revision 59 (2026-10-01).** §5.6 writes the frame header it draws into the room the VM stack keeps, readable from outside the process, settled by Mitchell on #1384. php-fpm's slow log stops a slow child and walks `EG(current_execute_data)` → `prev_execute_data` at fixed offsets (`fpm_php_trace_dump`), one `[0x…] function() file:line` line per frame. This engine's frames lived in `exec::FrameState` inside a `Vec` beside a second `Vec` of native calls, whose layout no other process can walk, and a copy taken at a safepoint never runs for a request asleep inside a native, which is the request a slow log is about. The header is php's shape, always on, at a per-call cost the bench holds: a bar on a call-bound program, recursive user calls and a loop of native calls, read against the merge base at the bound #1384 states, with the rest of the suite inside its A/A band. Rejected: a mirror kept only while php-fpm asks, a second representation of the chain that can drift by mode; and a divergence row, which leaves Linux operators frame-less slow logs. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: a slow request whose child is readable writes php's frame lines after the head, `[INCLUDE_OR_EVAL]` for an include, up to `request_slowlog_trace_depth`; the bar's reading is within the bound #1384 states.

**60. Revision 60 (2026-10-01).** §4.1 says the install layout is the build's, settled by Mitchell on #1371. The constants, the ini defaults and php-fpm's paths named the pinned Homebrew keg's layout (`PHP_PREFIX`, `include_path`'s PEAR directory, `extension_dir`, `PHP_SYSCONFDIR` and the rest), which configure wrote into that build's `main/build-defs.h`; no configure ran for this engine, so they named directories its binaries never install into, and php-fpm without `-y` looked for its config in another php's Cellar. #1176 had already ruled the php.ini search path and scan directory configure's way; the rest of `build-defs.h` follows the same rule, so a packager sets a prefix once and every value follows it. Rejected: the keg's layout, which answers as that build does on every machine; and php's defaults fixed in the source, which leaves a packager no prefix. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -rn 'opt/homebrew\|Sequoia-arm64' crates ext sapi` finds only `ext/openssl/src/config.rs`'s witness line, which names the linked libcrypto's `OPENSSLDIR`; `PHP_PREFIX=/opt/x cargo build` gives `php -r 'echo PHP_PREFIX;'` `/opt/x` and php-fpm's default config `/opt/x/etc/php-fpm.conf`.

**61. Revision 61 (2026-10-01).** §7.2, §12 and §4.10 say where mysqlnd, mysqli and pdo_mysql come from, settled by Mitchell on #1345 (2026-09-30 and 2026-10-01), which reverses revision 54 (#1167) for these two suites. php 8.5's mysqli and pdo_mysql run only on mysqlnd (`ext/mysqli/config.m4:73`), so the ruling is mysqlnd ported as php's algorithm over `mysql_common` 0.38, the one maintained crate without I/O, with its default features off: every read and write goes through the engine's streams, so `default_socket_timeout`, TLS over ext/openssl, unix sockets and the persistent pool (revision 55) behave as over `php_stream`, and the observable half (the statistics, the character sets and escaping, result typing, the state machine and its errors, prepared statements with cursors, authentication with RSA over ext/openssl, LOCAL INFILE) is mysqlnd's own. §7.2's table gains the row and the month-two row drops mysqli and pdo_mysql; §12's out-of-parity list drops `ext/mysqli` and `ext/pdo_mysql`, whose suites run against the server `ci/mysql/compose.yaml` starts, `mysql:8.4`; §4.10 names mysqlnd's `client` module as the surface pdo_mysql and mysqli reach, the row #1366 writes, and the pdo row gains the `PDO_API` items pdo_mysql calls, by revision 31's rule. Rejected: the full clients (`mysql`, `mysql_async`, `sqlx-mysql`), which bring their own sockets, TLS and runtime where php's goes through `php_stream`, and cannot serve `MYSQLI_ASYNC` and `mysqli_poll`; and `mysqlclient-sys`, a library php does not use. What is reversed is revision 54's list, which no ADR records, so there is no ADR. Check: `cargo xtask phpt --subset ext/pdo_mysql` with the compose server up meets every test the oracle meets; `cargo xtask api-check` prints no `ext/mysqlnd` item beyond `extension()` that §4.10's row does not name; `grep -rn 'TcpStream\|UnixStream' ext/mysqlnd` finds none.

**62. Revision 62 (2026-10-01).** §5.3 says a unit's literal arrays are immutable and shared, opcache's model, settled by Mitchell on #917, which ends the interim #770 set. Under the recipe's `php -n` php refcounts a literal per request, so a copy and its release leave a GC root there and none here; production php runs opcache, under which the literals are `IS_ARRAY_IMMUTABLE` exactly as this engine's are, and the difference reaches only introspection over a copied literal. Principle 3 stands whole, and the compiled tier's worker keeps reading literals off-thread through `Arc<Published>`. `trace:immutable-literal-arrays` and `[regime.immutable-literals]` become permanent, the row covering by name the tests #1158 finds. Rejected: a per-request refcount overlay, a branch and a side-table lookup on every copy of a literal and a call from every trace that copies one, paid to imitate a configuration nobody serves; refcounted literals per request, which reverse principle 3 and the immutable, `Sync` unit; a per-test oracle regime for phpt; and the whole recipe under opcache, which moves every subset's target for a narrow class. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: a witness that pins a copied literal's refcount or GC root names `[regime.immutable-literals]` and agrees with the oracle exactly; no phpt test misses on this class outside the row's covers.

**63. Revision 63 (2026-10-01).** §5.3 names the cause of the literal-array difference, not a list of its observables, settled by Mitchell on #1454. Revision 62 said the difference was confined to `gc_status()`, `gc_collect_cycles()`, `debug_zval_dump` and `memory_get_usage`; phpdbg's watch report (`phpdbg_print_watch_diff`, `Z/sapi/phpdbg/phpdbg_watch.c:170`) is a fifth, printing a literal's old value where `php -n` reports it inaccessible or destroyed, and the oracle under opcache prints it as this engine does. Every observable of a literal's refcount follows from the one model, so the sentence and the registry row name the cause and give the observables as examples, and the next one found needs no ruling (as #1305 and #1408 ruled for other enumerations). Rejected: widening the list by one, which keeps the closed list; a row of its own for the watch report, two rows for one cause; and reporting a literal as refcounted to the watch report, which fakes `php -n` in one place against revision 62. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: a difference from `php -n` over a literal array that the oracle under `[regime.immutable-literals]` does not share is a defect, not this row.

**64. Revision 64 (2026-10-02).** §5.2 says a unit's declarations hold their strings as its literals do, settled by Mitchell on #1472. An attribute's argument, a backed enum case's value and a static initialiser are interned under opcache and request-owned under `php -n` (`attr->args[i].value`, `backed_enum_table`'s key, `op_array->static_variables`), the question #917 settled for literal arrays. The same model governs: units and linked classes are immutable and shared and no shared value is refcounted (§5.1, §6.3, §9.4), and the registry row that names the cause covers the three. Rejected: php -n's per-request copies, a map-pointer slot per argument, case and initialiser holding a second representation of a string the unit already holds. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: #1464's snippet prints `interned` for all three under the engine, as php does under `[regime.immutable-literals]`.

**65. Revision 65 (2026-10-02).** §6.3 says which regime an include's memory is held to, settled by Mitchell on #1483: opcache's, the model the unit cache copies. Under `php -n` an include of a small file raises the peak by its compile; under opcache by the lookup alone, a fraction of it. The engine's units are shared and compiled once, so the request pays for the lookup, as under opcache, and #1467's include box is held under `[regime.jit-off]` with opcache's lookup allocations ported. Rejected: charging the compile to the request, which would move §5.13's approximation to imitate a cost the engine does not pay per request; and leaving include peaks unheld. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: an include of a small file reads the oracle's peak rise under `[regime.jit-off]` over a path of a fixed length.

**66. Revision 66 (2026-10-02).** §7.2 puts pcntl and posix in every host whose serving process is single-threaded, settled by Mitchell on #1494, revising revision 35's "never the server hosts". Revision 35's reason was a `fork()` in a process that serves requests on threads, which keeps the calling thread and whatever locks the others held. php-fpm's children serve one request at a time on one thread, as php-fpm's do, so the reason does not reach them, and the pinned php-fpm loads both (`php-fpm -n -m`). Upstream declares pcntl `cli`-only (`ext/pcntl/config.m4`; `build/php.m4` links it into `PHP_CLI_OBJS` unless the primary SAPI is cgi, embed or phpdbg), but parity is the pinned build less what is ruled out (revision 49), not upstream's default. The rule is by the property, so a host that serves on threads (embed, a threaded server) is excluded by it without a ruling of its own. Rejected: copying upstream's default, which leaves php-fpm without pcntl where the oracle's has it; and keeping revision 35 with a registry row. Nothing is reversed beyond revision 35's sentence, so there is no ADR. Check: `php-fpm -n -m` lists `pcntl` and `posix`; `sapi/fpm/tests/reload-uses-sigkill-as-last-measure.phpt` meets its expectation.

**69. Revision 69 (2026-10-02).** §7.2's sqlite row and §12's build row say where SQLite comes from, settled by Mitchell on #1519 for what #1510 built. `libsqlite3-sys`'s `bundled` amalgamation is that crate's release, not the one the pinned keg links, so by #624's rule ext/pdo_sqlite links the keg's libsqlite3 through `pkg-config`, statically, never vendored, as revision 32 has `libxml2`, and keeps `libsqlite3-sys` for its declarations alone. The design names no version: the version is the pin's, and a linked keg follows it. A Rust SQLite (a crate bundling the C at its own release, or a rewrite such as Turso) was considered: the first answers the crate's version, the second is a second implementation whose errors and PRAGMAs are not SQLite's. **74. Revision 74 (2026-10-02).** §5.4's object header names what `php_heap` holds, settled by Mitchell on #1561: `class: u32` and `slots: u32` in the eight bytes where php keeps `ce`, and `extra_flags` beside `handle`, as php 8.5's `zend_object` has it; `properties_table`'s length is read from `slots`, since the header holds no `ce` to read it from. The class is a number the heap reads nothing of, so a class identity costs no byte of any object, which a `&Class` word would not allow. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `php_heap`'s test holds the header to 40 bytes.

**75. Revision 75 (2026-10-02).** §7.2 and §12 rule intl, gd and ffi into parity, settled by Mitchell on #1573, reversing #1166's ruling for the three. Each binds the library the oracle links, revision 72's rule: intl the ICU4C the pinned keg links (icu4c@78), through `pkg-config`, never vendored, with the part of php's intl written in C++ over ICU's C++ classes reached through an `extern "C"` C++ shim of the crate's own, compiled by its `build.rs` and bound in `src/ffi/`, so php's algorithm is copied where php wrote it in C++, `CodePointBreakIterator` included; gd the libgd the pinned keg links, through `pkg-config`; ffi the platform's libffi, as the oracle links it, bound by hand in `src/ffi/`, with php's C-declaration parser ported. Rejected: ICU4X, whose CLDR snapshot is not ICU 78's data and which has no MessageFormat, pattern formatting or parsing; ICU's C API alone, which makes php's C++ paths re-derivations; php-src's bundled libgd, which is not what the oracle links. Nothing is reversed beyond #1166's sentence under §12, so there is no ADR. Check: `php -m` under the engine lists each as its crate lands, and `--ri` names the oracle's library version.

**76. Revision 76 (2026-10-02).** §7.2's gettext and readline rows say what their libraries' process-wide state does here, settled by Mitchell on #1603, #1604 and #1606. The keg's libintl ships no `pkg-config` file, so ext/gettext finds it as php's `config.m4` does (#1603). libintl's domain table is the C library's and process-wide, as under php's ZTS build, and php resets it at no request's end, so it is accepted as php's; today's hosts serve one request at a time per process (#1494), and only a host serving on threads shares it (#1604). libedit's readline callbacks carry no user data, so the binding keeps what php keeps in `ZEND_TLS` globals in per-thread slots of a `pthread_key_t` made once through `pthread_once`, C's primitives and not the cells and locks `ext.no-locks` denies, every access through a raw pointer (#1606). Rejected: saving and restoring the domain table per request, which is still racy and not php's; a process lock around libintl, which ADR-0005 forbids; and process-wide `static mut`s for readline's state. ADR-0005 stands. Check: `cargo xtask unsafe-audit` finds ext/readline's slots in `src/ffi/` alone.
