**115. Revision 115 (2026-10-09).** §5.14 gains the lend and §5.13 loses timelib from its recorded approximation, both sentences settled by Mitchell on #1658 (2026-10-03) and built by #1655. Revision 50 kept what php routes through `emalloc` only by a compile-time macro, timelib's allocations, off the request's heap, and rejected a thread-local heap for it. So every date constructor's peak read short of php's by what timelib allocates while it parses: 320 against 624 for `new DateTime`, 176 against 768 for `new DateInterval('P1D')`. Now `php_heap` lends the running request's heap to C for exactly one call: a per-thread slot that `Heap::lend` fills and clears on return, and `extern "C"` `emalloc`, `ecalloc`, `erealloc`, `estrdup`, `estrndup` and `efree` that reach through it. `estrndup` is php's sixth mapping, beyond the ruling's five. `timelib_sys` compiles timelib against them through a `timelib_config.h`, as php's `config0.m4` does, and they resolve at link time with no crate edge. Under a lend the heap tells its blocks from the system's by its own chunk and huge-block lists. Outside one, allocation falls back to the system allocator and `efree` frees nothing. ext/date keeps every free of timelib's memory under a lend of the same request. A refusal by the limit is served by the system allocator and raised when the call returns. ADR-0014 records the narrowing of revision 50's rejection; the ruling named it ADR-0009, a number taken by then. Rejected, by the ruling: revision 50 kept as it stood, and the peaks modelled after the fact. Check: the witnesses beside `DateTime::__construct`, `DateInterval::__construct`, `DateTimeZone::__construct` and `DatePeriod::__construct` print the oracle's peak and usage. `php_heap`'s `lend` tests hold a block lent, given back and refused. `cargo xtask phpt --subset ext/date` meets its 679.

**116. Revision 116 (2026-10-09).** §4.1's `sapi/*` row says how the debugging host reads what it stops in, settled by Mitchell on #1911 (2026-10-07, option 1): a host reads frames, op arrays and the linked tables through the native face from its own tier, as an extension does; `tier.lock` keeps the mechanism, the writes and nothing that reads. Frames and op arrays had two read paths, the tier's digests and the native face's views (#1827, #1829), against §3.2's one function per semantic; the digests move to `sapi/phpdbg` over the native face, which gains the enumeration of the linked tables it lacked. `jit-design.md` revision 15 carries §2.4's sentence and the items, and ADR-0008's edge sentence is amended with it. Rejected, by the ruling: the debugger's digests kept in `php_engine` as the tier's reads. Nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no new ADR. Check: `api-check` finds no `sapi/phpdbg` edge to `php_engine`, and `crates/php_engine/tier.lock` names no item that reads a frame, an op array or a linked table.

**117. Revision 117 (2026-10-09).** §4.1's `php_process` row, §4.4 and §4.5 name the stream layer's face, `php_engine::layer`, and its lock, `layer.lock`, settled by Mitchell on #2378 (2026-10-09), which carries #1959's ruling (2026-10-07, shape 1) and #1962's pool. #1875 split `php_streams` out of `php_engine` behind seam 5, and every engine item made `pub` for it alone was extension-facing through `php_ext`'s re-export of `api` and counted in `api.lock`: `serve_method`, `is_pending`, the raw fd calls and their kin. #1959 built the ruling: the layer's methods on `Request` are a sealed trait, `layer::Face`, implemented for `Request`, since a module `php_ext` does not re-export cannot hide a method of a type it does, and its free items stand beside it in `php_engine::layer`, `api::net`'s fourteen among them; `php_ext` re-exports neither, and `layer.lock` lists the module's 36 items, `Face` and its fifteen methods and twenty free items. #1962 moved the stream layer's persistent pool, php's `EG(persistent_list)`, out of the engine's shared state into the layer's engine state, `Pool<Vec<u8>, Entry>` typed in `php_streams`, which the engine builds from `StreamHooks::state` and closes first at engine end through `StreamHooks::engine_end`. §4.1's `php_process` row says the fd calls are reached through `php_engine::layer` where it said `api::net`; §4.4 gains *The stream layer's face*, and its `api::Pool` row and its paragraph on the engine's cross-request structures name the layer's engine state; §4.5 lists `layer.lock` and gives an item lock its second subject, one module of the producer, whose `use` by a crate outside the seam's consumers fails. Rejected, by the ruling: the widening accepted, §4.4 stating that the native face includes what the stream layer needs (shape 2); php exposes `main/streams/` and `main/network.c` to every extension, and §4.4 is narrower on purpose. Nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -n 'api::net' third-engine-design.md` matches ledger rows alone; `grep -c '^layer::' crates/php_engine/layer.lock` prints 36 and `grep -n '^net::\|PersistentList' crates/php_ext/api.lock` finds nothing; `api-check`'s `layer-face` fixture fails.

**118. Revision 118 (2026-10-09).** §4.3's seam 8 names the tier's second declaration, `Tier::HOLDS`, and its write, `tier::begin`, settled by Mitchell on #2480 (2026-10-09). #2359 (merged at 32bd6ca6) had phpdbg's held program begin for an observer at `run` and nowhere it is not run: php compiles the execution context at the prompt and calls `zend_execute` on it only at `run` (`Z/sapi/phpdbg/phpdbg_prompt.c:866`), and `zend_execute` raises `ZEND_OBSERVER_FCALL_BEGIN` for the main frame before `zend_execute_ex` (`Z/Zend/zend_vm_execute.h:121973`). So `tier::execute` raises the main frame's begin before the loop unless the tier declares `HOLDS`, default false, and a tier that holds the frame raises it with `tier::begin` where it executes the frame; `Request::push_frame` raises none. `tier.lock` carries `tier::begin`, and seam 8's paragraph named only the tier's other declaration, which of php's execution hooks it replaces (revision 58). `jit-design.md` revision 16 carries §2.4's sentence. Rejected: no sentence, the declaration left to `tier.rs`'s rustdoc while §4.3 names the other. Nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -n 'HOLDS' third-engine-design.md jit-design.md` matches §4.3, §2.4 and the two ledger rows; `crates/php_engine/tier.lock` carries `tier::begin`; `sapi/phpdbg/tests/observed.rs` passes.

**119. Revision 119 (2026-10-09).** §4.10's `pdo` row names `Connection::fetch_err` and `ParameterMap`, settled by Mitchell on #2485 and #2501 (2026-10-09). PDO kept a copy of each connector's native error, `native_error` on the connection and on the statement, where php's connectors keep their own `einfo` (`pdo_mysql_error_info`, `pdo_sqlite_error_info`) and PDO reads it back through `pdo_dbh_methods::fetch_err`: `pdo_mysql_fetch_error_func` reads `S->einfo` for a statement and `H->einfo` otherwise, `pdo_sqlite_fetch_error_func` reads `H->einfo` for both. One field in PDO could not say both connectors' retention, since `_pdo_mysql_error` frees the message at every call and `_pdo_sqlite_error` keeps it on `SQLITE_OK`. #2489 built #2485's ruling: `Connection::fetch_err` answers a `NativeError`, the code and the text made a string of the request's as `add_next_index_string` makes it, and a failure on a connection or a statement carries its SQLSTATE alone; only a factory's error, which php words through `pdo_throw_exception` for want of a connection to keep it on, carries its code and text, `Cause::Refused`. The row's Items cell gains `fetch_err` and `NativeError`, the type it answers, and its Mirrors cell `fetch_err`. #2483 (merged at 56701a75) made `pdo_parse_params`'s `bound_param_map` a request table, `connector::ParameterMap`, which `Prepared` and `Rewritten` carry, as `php_pdo_driver.h` declares `pdo_stmt_t.bound_param_map` for its drivers; by #2501's ruling the row's list of the types the connectors pass gains it, nothing else changed. Rejected, by the rulings: both copies of the error kept, two representations of one error that one field cannot give both retentions (#2485); the row left without `ParameterMap` (#2501). Nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `ext/pdo/surface.lock` carries `connector::Connection::fetch_err`, `connector::NativeError` and `connector::ParameterMap`; `git grep -n native_error ext/pdo` finds nothing; the witnesses beside pdo_mysql's and pdo_sqlite's `fetch_err` print the oracle's `errorInfo()` for a connection and a statement.

**120. Revision 120 (2026-10-10).** §4.1's `php_streams` and `php_engine` rows name the stream layer's face beside `api`, §4.5's lock set is a region rendered from the manifest, and principle 12 and §4.4's state paragraph say what `ext.no-locks` holds, settled by Mitchell on #2490 (2026-10-09) and landed by #2496. `crates/php_streams/src/api.rs:8-9` imports `php_engine::api::{abi, call, coerce, decl, raising, values}` and `php_engine::layer`, so the row's "over `api`" was half its edges: it now names both faces in the ruling's words, which leave out the open hook's php name, `zend_stream_open_function`, and the `php_engine` row's interface column gains "Stream-layer-facing: `layer.lock`." §4.5 listed the locks in prose, which had drifted from the manifest a second time, lacking `process.lock` and `link.lock`; the set is now `cargo xtask status --section locks --format md --tracked`, the region AGENTS.md renders, and the prose keeps what a lock holds that its row does not say (`heap.lock`'s entry slot and its peers, `tier.lock`'s two sections), a clause each for `process.lock` and `link.lock`, and the three kinds of lock. Principle 12 said no extension can reach a cross-request structure with a lock inside and counted four in the engine, and §4.4 repeated the count. ext/dba, ext/pdo, ext/curl and ext/mysqli each hold an `api::Pool`, a `Mutex` inside (`crates/php_engine/src/api/abi.rs:323-326`), in their engine state, and the engine holds more than four: the inheritance cache's shards, the interned strings, the arena and the interrupt schedule. Both now say what `ext.no-locks` enforces: an extension names no lock type, and keeps what lasts across requests in `api::Cache` or `api::Pool`, whose lock and bound are the engine's; the count and the list of engine internals go, and so does principle 12's clause that the one thing that mutates across requests is the engine's cache of immutable values, which a `Pool` makes false. The ruling also named the liveness check as the engine's, but the taker checks a pooled entry, as php's driver does (`ext/pdo/src/dbh.rs:790`, `crates/php_streams/src/streams/persistent.rs:68-73`), and `Pool::take` checks nothing, so that clause is left out and #2533 asks a person for it. The stray `---` between rows 114 and 115 goes. `jit-design.md` revision 17 carries §2.2's two consts. Rejected, by the ruling: `layer` named alone in the row, against the edges `api-check` holds; the two locks appended inline; each lock's description moved into a `[[seam]]` `text` field, a manifest schema change for prose; only the persistent pool re-homed, which leaves both false clauses standing. Nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `computed check third-engine-design.md` passes with the `locks` region; `grep -n 'four cross[-]request' third-engine-design.md` finds nothing; no `---` stands between two ledger rows; §4.1's `php_engine` row names `layer.lock`.

**121. Revision 121 (2026-10-10).** §4.6's deletion test gains `php_process`'s sentence, settled by Mitchell on #2286 (2026-10-09), word for word: "Delete `php_process`: `fork`, the signal pool, `LC_CTYPE`, the process identity and the fd calls fall into `php_engine` and the extensions that call them, and the process-wide `unsafe` gets a home per caller instead of one audited crate that depends on nothing in the workspace." §4.1 had listed `php_process` (revision 80, #1779), `php_streams` (#1773, #1874) and `php_link` (ADR-0012) with no sentence in §4.6 for any of them. The ruling confirmed one for each. The two others each carry a clause the tree contradicts, so they wait on #2534. The `php_link` sentence says the format crate would gain `php_heap`, but `php_unit` already depends on it (`crates/php_unit/Cargo.toml:16`) for its op arrays' literals. The `php_streams` sentence gives `php_engine` "~1.2k lines of room", a number that changes, and `cargo xtask budget` measures neither the room `php_engine` has now nor the room it would keep with the layer back at that figure. Rejected: landing the two as confirmed, which would type two false clauses into the design. Nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `crates/php_process/Cargo.toml` names no workspace crate; `grep -n 'Delete .php_process.' third-engine-design.md` matches §4.6 and this row.

**122. Revision 122 (2026-10-10).** §4.8's last sentence says the dev server serves on one thread in php's event loop, worded by #2260 under Mitchell's ruling there (2026-10-09: an agent words it where no draft does, under #2439's delegation). It said "The dev server's accept loop blocks on `accept`, since the worker runs a request per thread", which #1884 made false: as #1779 ruled ("No request of `php -S` runs on a thread other than the server's"), `sapi/cli/src/server/mod.rs` waits on the listening socket and every open connection at once, as `php_cli_server_do_event_loop` polls them (`Z/sapi/cli/php_cli_server.c:2770-2797`), and serves the requests one after another on that thread; `PHP_CLI_SERVER_WORKERS` forks the server into processes before it serves (#1885). `research/production-server.r1.md` §2.1 measured it and §8 item 11 asked for the sentence. Rejected: a sentence that also says where a production server's framing lives, which that research ties to a revision of its own; nothing here rules on a production host. Nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -n 'accept loop' third-engine-design.md` matches this row alone; `serve_until_stopped` in `sapi/cli/src/server/mod.rs` spawns no thread.

**123. Revision 123 (2026-10-10).** §7.1 gains one paragraph that settles php's `zend_extension` row handler by handler, drafted on #2038 for #2256 (2026-10-07) and confirmed by Mitchell there (2026-10-09) with its Xdebug sentence struck: a design sentence does not rest on an outside tool's history, and the fact stays on the ticket. #1823 built `statement_handler` (`abi::ZendExtension`, `crates/php_engine/src/api/abi.rs:484-494`), and the design of record named no statement handler, though #1770 item 12 has each of php's low-level hooks supported, given an equivalent, or refused by a written sentence. `fcall_begin_handler` and `fcall_end_handler` had none of the three: `ext_fcall_begin` and `ext_fcall_end` (`crates/php_engine/src/handlers.rs:147-168`) only advance. Mitchell ruled on #2038 (2026-10-07) that both are refused, with the observer named as the equivalent and the differences stated, and the paragraph also refuses the `op_array_*` handlers, which had no sentence anywhere. Their refusal cites principle 4 by §3's name as its ground and the Zend C ABI as its second, as drafted and confirmed. Rejected, by the ruling: building `fcall_begin_handler` and `fcall_end_handler`, which adds code nothing calls, and refusing `statement_handler`, which removes built and tested code that step debuggers need. Nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `abi::ZendExtension` carries `statement_handler` and no other handler; `ext_fcall_begin` and `ext_fcall_end` tell nothing; `grep -n 'Xdebug' third-engine-design.md` matches this row alone.

**124. Revision 124 (2026-10-10).** §8's **Divergence registry** says a row's kind is the lane that reads it and its cause whether it is parity debt, and that the day-30 target counts the `debt` rows against the manifest's number, settled by Mitchell on #2429 (2026-10-09, restated for #2460's field). #2460 gave a row a `cause` beside its `kind`, `debt` by default or `php-ub`, `by-design` or `oracle-build`, and #2462 built it at 57f46b7b (`Divergence::cause`, `xtask/src/divergence.rs:107`); the target, now counting `debt` rows alone, tightened and moved into `seams.toml` as `[divergence] target`. The paragraph listed five kinds where the registry reads six, lacking `crosscheck` (`Kind::ALL`, `divergence.rs:237-244`), and held "under 20 rows across every kind". The kind list gains `crosscheck`, the keys gain the extension's name a `crosscheck:` id carries, and the number leaves the prose for the manifest's. `harness-design.md` revision 116 carries §12's and §14's sentences, and CONTEXT.md's **Divergence** names the cause. Rejected, by the ruling: a kind per cause, lanes times causes (#2460). Nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -n 'under 20' third-engine-design.md` matches ledger rows alone; §8's kind list is `Kind::ALL`'s six; `cargo xtask status --section divergence` counts the `debt` rows against `seams.toml`'s `[divergence] target`.

**125. Revision 125 (2026-10-10).** Principle 9 says a function that can run PHP code holds a `&mut Request`, as a parameter or in a value whose lifetime names it, and §10's **Re-entry in signatures** bullet, the `xtask` lint, is struck, settled by Mitchell on #2465 (2026-10-09, a session) with #2464's deletion, the principle word for word. #2464's third diff, approved by Mitchell and landed at e27db22f, deleted the manifest's `engine.reentry-signature` rule: measured there at a1299fa1, 298 of the 300 non-test functions of php_engine and php_streams that call `execute`, `reenter` or a `release` take `&mut Request`, and the two that do not are `Engine::start_request`, which makes the request it serves, and `api::syntax::InRequest::event`, which holds `req: &mut Request` in a field. Every `execute` and `release` takes `&mut Request`, so the type makes re-entry visible at the call, and the lint could only have held an allowlist of those two shapes. The principle said "takes `&mut Request`" and "a function without that parameter", which the second shape is not, and §10 named a lint no instrument runs. `harness-design.md` revision 117 carries §3's and §6's rows. Rejected, by the ruling: "the parameter" wording, which claims more than the types enforce, and reversing the deletion. Nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR; ADR-0002 stands. Check: `grep -n 'a lint in .xtask. flags' third-engine-design.md` matches ledger rows alone, and `grep -n 'reentry' seams.toml xtask/src` finds no rule or checker.

**126. Revision 126 (2026-10-10).** §10's **Lints** and **Generated code** bullets say what `seams.toml` holds since #2464 and #1950, as #2547 found and worded. The **Lints** bullet denied the panic family in seven named crates, `xtask` and tests exempt, and put `disallowed-types` for `ext/*` in one generated `ext/clippy.toml`. #2464's first diff, approved by Mitchell (2026-10-09) and landed at e27db22f, widened `lints.no-panic` to `crates/*`, `ext/*` and `sapi/*`, exempting `tests`, `xtask` and `crates/php_macros`. #1950 (c71f8c03) moved `fork.threads` into clippy's `disallowed-methods`, so `generated.committed` now generates a `clippy.toml` at `crates/`, `ext/` and `sapi/`. The bullet now names the rule's scope and not a crate list, and every generated file and not one. The **Generated code** bullet's one exception becomes the generated `clippy.toml` files, the three that rule lists. The words are #2547's suggestion, which follows `harness-design.md` revision 117's §4.1 bullet and names no file list, so neither bullet goes stale when the rule's scope or its files move. They are an agent's to word under the delegation Mitchell ruled on #2439, whose own sentence waits on #2543. Nothing is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -n 'php_jit., .ext/\*., .php_sapi' third-engine-design.md` matches ledger rows alone; `grep -n -A3 'id = "lints.no-panic"' seams.toml` shows the scope and exempt list the bullet points to, and `grep -n -A1 'id = "generated.committed"' seams.toml` shows the three generated files.

**127. Revision 127 (2026-10-10).** §5.14's *Published, and the word* and §6.3's list of what the unit cache holds say the map-pointer offset assigned at publication is each op array's own, and `Published` holds the `Func`s and their words, settled by Mitchell on #527 (2026-10-09, a session) and worded by #527's taker under #2439's delegation. Both sentences, and CONTEXT.md's **Published**, put the offsets in `Published`, which the code never did: `php_unit::OpArray::runtime_cache` carries the offset, as `zend_op_array` carries its `ZEND_MAP_PTR(run_time_cache)`, so a frame reaches it in one hop. #2440 typed it as `php_unit::MapPtrOffset`, which php_engine converts at one edge (`exec::CacheAt::of`). Rejected, by the ruling: moving the offset into `Published`, a hop php does not have that buys nothing observable. The number is plain data written before the unit is shared, so ADR-0001's line is not crossed, and nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -n 'map-pointer offsets assigned at publication' third-engine-design.md CONTEXT.md` matches ledger rows alone; `OpArray::runtime_cache` is a `MapPtrOffset`.

**128. Revision 128 (2026-10-10).** §5.11's enumeration of what a fiber switch swaps, §4.6's deletion test for `php_link` and `php_streams`, and §5.1's debug owner sensor take the sentences Mitchell settled on 2026-10-10 (a session) on #2552, #2534 and #2542, each word for word, landed by #2592. §5.11 named the `VmStack` and `Vm`, "the frames' engine half", as two values side by side; since #2539 a frame's engine half is the `exec::Header` laid in the frame's own room on the VM stack, `Vm` owns the stack (`exec::Vm::stack`, `crates/php_engine/src/exec.rs:1187-1189`), and `fiber::Execution` holds `vm` and no `stack` field (`crates/php_engine/src/fiber.rs:184-194`); what a frame owns beyond its header's words is an `exec::Annex` in the request's slab (`AnnexId`, `exec.rs:781`), which no switch touches. §4.6 had no sentence for `php_link` or `php_streams` since revision 121 (#2286), whose confirmed words carried two clauses the tree contradicts: `php_unit` already depends on `php_heap` (`crates/php_unit/Cargo.toml:16`), and "~1.2k lines of room" was a number that changes; #2534's words drop both, the `php_link` one resting on `php_compile`'s edge to `php_link` (`crates/php_compile/Cargo.toml:18`). §5.1 said every owner dropped unconsumed is reported, but #2246's side table keys the record by a chunk's block (`php_heap::owners`), so an owner of what no count owns and an owner of a huge block are recorded nowhere, as `crates/php_heap/src/owners.rs:17-22` says; the ruled words are appended to the sentence that names the sensor, and its release-build clause becomes a sentence of its own, naming the owner type where it said "it", so the ruled words stand together. §4.8's closing paragraph, ruled on #2569 and #2570 in the same session, is revision 129's: its words reached #2570 after this revision was typed. Rejected, by the rulings: the restated §4.6 sentences of #2286 as confirmed, and §5.1 standing as it was. Nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -n 'and .Vm., the frames' third-engine-design.md` matches ledger rows alone; `grep -n 'Delete .php_link.' third-engine-design.md` matches §4.6 and ledger rows; `fiber::Execution` has no `stack` field; `php_heap::owners::dropped` records nothing for a value with no chunk block.

**129. Revision 129 (2026-10-10).** §4.8 gains a closing paragraph, **The production server's head is hyper's**, settled word for word by Mitchell on 2026-10-10 (a session) for #2569 and #2570 and posted on #2570, landed by #2592. §4.8 described the dev server's framing alone, and `sapi/makala` (#2337) takes its HTTP/1.1 head from hyper 1.12 (`sapi/makala/Cargo.toml:35`), which accepts a bare LF as a line end and `Transfer-Encoding` beside `Content-Length` with no option to refuse either and writes response header names lowercase, measured on t2340 against the binary. #2569 ruled hyper's head parse kept, since nothing in front of the edge frames a request a second way, and #2570 ruled the lowercase names by design, since HTTP/2 and HTTP/3 allow no other form; the paragraph states both and the condition that reopens the first. The divergence row `trace:makala-lowercase-header-names` cites it. Rejected, by the rulings: a strict RFC 9112 head parser in front of hyper, for now, and writing the response head outside hyper's encoder. Nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -n 'title_case_headers\|preserve_header_case' sapi/makala/src` finds nothing; `grep -n 'head is hyper' third-engine-design.md` matches §4.8 and ledger rows; the registry's `trace:makala-lowercase-header-names` cites §4.8.

**130. Revision 130 (2026-10-10).** §7.1's **The entry is php's ZPP** bullet names the step the entry takes before the arity, settled word for word by Mitchell on #2564 (2026-10-10, a session) and landed by #2596. #2423 gave `php_macros` a `before_args = path` key on `#[php_function]` and on a method's `#[php(..)]` (`crates/php_macros/src/function.rs:295`, `:305`), a `fn(&mut Request)` the entry calls after recording the native entered and before the arity, and ext/intl names `before_args = error::reset` on its natives whose php counterpart resets the request's error ahead of ZPP (`INTL_METHOD_INIT_VARS`), in place of taking each argument as `Raw<..>` under `arity = manual`. The bullet began at the arity and so no longer named every step the entry takes. Rejected, by the ruling: §7.1 left as it stood. Nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR; ADR-0002's concrete `Request` is the parameter the step takes. Check: `grep -rn 'before_args = error::reset' ext/intl/src` matches; `grep -n 'before_args = path' third-engine-design.md` matches §7.1 and ledger rows.

**131. Revision 131 (2026-10-11).** §4.8 gains a closing paragraph, **The production server names itself and refuses what hyper refuses**, settled word for word by Mitchell on #2611 ("Amended", 2026-10-10, a session) and landed by #2629. `sapi/makala` answers `php_sapi_name()` with `makala`, as #2335 built, and refuses 400 a request-target holding `<`, `>` or `"` unencoded, which #2609 ruled strict, and a byte of 0x80 and above, which #2638 ruled the same mechanism; nginx passes those bytes on to php-fpm. The registry's by-design rows of kind `makala` cite the paragraph: `divergence.reason` asks a by-design row to cite a design section or ADR, and the host's name and the refusal stood only in tickets. #2639 renamed the kind from #2611's first `fpm`, so one row serves the php-fpm comparison and the served phpt lane. Rejected, by the rulings: taking the bytes leniently as nginx does, which needs a second framing path in front of hyper; and masking the differences inside the comparisons. Nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -n 'names itself and refuses' third-engine-design.md` matches §4.8 and this row; `cargo xtask divergence-reason` passes with the three `makala:` rows citing §4.8.

**132. Revision 132 (2026-10-12).** §4.7's "What it holds" says what a name in the declaration model is, in the words Mitchell ruled on #2711 (2026-10-12, a session), landed with the code by #646's bundle (#641, #642, #2157). It read "names as interned strings", which `php_unit::decl::Name` has never been: it is owned bytes, `Box<[u8]>`, resolved by the compiler before it is made, and interned with the unit's literals at publication as §5.2 says. The ruling confirms the shape #646 proposed: a name looked up is `decl::Bare`, borrowed and stripped of one leading `\` once at the boundary where a name as written enters a lookup, with `Bare::resolved` asserting in debug builds that it was handed no `\`; every table by name folds case under `php_link::rows`'s one hash (`NameMap`, `NameHasher`), in php's two tables. Rejected, by the ruling: `Name` as an interned-string handle, which puts an interner in the leaf crate and touches about 260 construction sites for pointer compares the folded hash makes cheap; confirming without the debug assertion; and reversing the shape. Nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -n 'names as interned strings' third-engine-design.md` matches ledger rows alone; `grep -rn 'strip_prefix(b"\\\\")' crates/php_engine/src` finds no strip that feeds `class_named` or `function_named` but `constant()`'s and `defined()`'s, which copy `zend_get_constant_ex`'s strip before the class lookup's own, as php strips twice there; `Bare::resolved(b"\\Foo")` fails a debug build's test.

**133. Revision 133 (2026-10-12).** §5.13's **The allocator is the only accountant** and §5.6's revision-59 paragraph take the sentences Mitchell settled on #2665 (2026-10-11, a session), each word for word, landed with the code by #2553. Since #2539 a `FRAMELESS_ICALL_*` pushed its native's call record on the VM stack, and php's handler pushes no frame unless an observer watches the native (`Z/Zend/zend_vm_def.h:9775`), so where the record was the one the top page had no room for, the engine opened a page php does not, and `memory_get_peak_usage()` at a page's end read 256 KiB more than php's (#2553's measurement: depth 2973 under the engine, 2974 under the oracle, for `in_array` and `min`). #2553 lays an unobserved call's record in `php_heap`'s reused room off the pages and off `mm` (`VmStack::push_frameless`), which the two sentences did not allow: §5.13 said a frame cannot exist without passing through `mm`, and §5.6 that a native call's header takes room on the VM stack. §5.13 now names the record, its home and its bound as the one exception, so the next proposal to move something off `mm` cannot cite it as a pattern; §5.6 says the record is on the chain a backtrace walks and never on the chain another process reads, which #1384 has not built yet. Rejected, by the ruling: the sentences standing, with the record back on the pages and a by-design row, which leaves a request near its `memory_limit` a fatal at a page boundary where php has none; and no record at all, which is a second entry point per frameless native, as php's `zflf_` beside `zif_`. Nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -n 'one per such call in flight' third-engine-design.md` matches §5.13 and this row; `php_heap`'s room for the record holds a header and three operands and a push of more is the engine's error; the witness on `frameless_call` reads 909 and 909 under the engine and the oracle.

**134. Revision 134 (2026-10-12).** §4.1's row for `php_engine` names `serve`'s third parameter, `look`, as #949 built it with revision 34's owned sink (#946). t1968 had added `Engine::serve_started` beside `serve` for php-fpm's loop, which looks at its request only once `php_request_startup` has run (`Z/sapi/fpm/fpm/fpm_main.c:1838-1903`); #949's third box folds it into the one `serve`, so the engine keeps one way to serve a request. `serve` asks `look` what the request serves after every extension's `rinit`, and never where the start failed or was refused; a host that serves the program the description names answers `HostServes::Program`. The sink handed back is the host's own: `OutputSink` is `Any`, so a host that wants its type back has it from the box. Nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -rn 'serve_started' crates sapi` finds nothing; `Engine::serve` takes `look`.

**135. Revision 135 (2026-10-13).** §3.3 and §7.3 replace the permanent library rule with the faithful Rust destination settled by Mitchell on #2817, Q1–Q20 and its confirmed final packet, landed by #2825. The current §7.2 bindings remain transition adapters, not evidence of native removal. §7.3 records the ownership and proof contracts ruled on #2818, #2824, #2822, #2819, #2820 and #2823; §4.10 admits fileinfo's header-backed PCRE dependency and keeps replacement surfaces proof-gated. ADR-0015 records the reversal, including revision 47's rejection of a libmbfl Rust port and the date-specific retirement of ADR-0014's C protocol only after migration. No runtime, numerical manifest value, public signature, backend or particular divergence is selected. Check: the six contracts preserve actual allocation and callback behaviour; the native-removal inventory distinguishes transition from completed scopes; budget, interface and performance decisions wait on their bounded proof tickets.

**136. Revision 136 (2026-10-17).** Revision 135 is withdrawn with ADR-0015, as Mitchell asked on 2026-10-17 in withdrawing the Rust replacement effort (#2840 and its open children, closed as withdrawn). §3.3 is the library rule again, write what PHP writes and link what PHP links, and §7.2 the library rule applied, as they stood before revision 135; §7.3's replacement contracts, the **Replacement surfaces** paragraph and §4.10's `pcre` → `fileinfo` row, which no crate's edge uses, go, and ADR-0014 loses its migration amendment, so its contract governs every C caller as before. Revision 47's rejection of a libmbfl Rust port stands again. The evidence the closed proof tickets kept under `data/` stays as history and binds nothing. Check: `grep -rn 'ADR-0015' third-engine-design.md docs/adr` matches ledger rows 135 and 136 alone; `grep -n '### 7.3' third-engine-design.md` finds nothing.

**137. Revision 137 (2026-10-18).** §4.1's `php_jit` row and §9.3 state revisions 52 and 102 where those revisions did not amend them, beside `jit-design.md` revision 21 (#2739). Both said recording is exclusive per request, which revision 52 (Mitchell on #627) made exclusive per execution context, the trace number the fiber switch saves; and §9.3 had published traces hold every unit they inlined, which revision 102 (Mitchell on #2186) replaced with the epoch's lifetime, so no trace is freed alone. Nothing is reversed and no ruling is widened, so there is no ADR; a person reads this row at the merge. Check: `grep -n 'exclusive per request\|holding every unit' third-engine-design.md` matches ledger rows alone.

**138. Revision 138 (2026-10-18).** §9.2 names the seven kernels and their sizes, settled by Mitchell on #2736 (2026-10-12): `fib`, `nbody`, `mandelbrot`, `spectralnorm`, `binary_trees`, `fannkuch` and `string_munge` from Mahout's `benchmarks/src/`, each at the size where the pinned php interpreter, opcache on and JIT off, takes 0.5–1 s on the darwin-arm64 box, the sizes measured for the ruling on 2026-10-18 under the oracle's recipe and `[regime.jit-off]`, each the minimum of seven to nine runs, on a machine shared with other builders at load 16–41 on 12 cores, read by its CPU time where load spread its wall time (`fib` at 36: 0.675 s CPU, 0.93–1.33 s wall). The phrase carried G1, G2, the cost of `step` under recording and the trace-dump diff with no list in the tree, and §9.2's sentence counted six of the seven twice, "the seven kernels plus `binary_trees`, `fannkuch`, `nbody`, `mandelbrot`, `spectralnorm`, `fib`"; it now reads "on the seven kernels". No size meets the band for `fannkuch`, whose one parameter steps its time about ninefold, so the sizes rule gains the clause Mitchell ruled on #2875 (2026-10-18), word for word, "Where a kernel's one parameter steps past 0.5–1 s, it runs at the smallest size above 0.5 s.", and `fannkuch` runs at 10; `string_munge`'s 600000 is the largest the recipe's `memory_limit` admits, where at 610000 the oracle and the engine both exhaust it at the same line, and its wall minimum was 0.470–0.622 s across four sessions, inside the band in three. Rejected, by the rulings: `sort_heavy` as the seventh, and the six alone with the phrase renamed; Mahout's report-small sizes, where warm-up dominates; `fannkuch` at 10 as a named exception, at 9, and at 9 run four times in one process. Nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `docs/perf/workloads/` holds the seven at these sizes, each one's `$N` the one §9.2 names; `grep -n 'seven kernels plus' third-engine-design.md` matches ledger rows alone.

**139. Revision 139 (2026-10-18).** §11's row 31 has its exit bar, and its Work cell names `jit-design.md` §11 as month two's schedule, both settled by Mitchell on #2735 (2026-10-12). Row 31's bar was empty, the one row of §11 with nothing a reading could hold, so the `tier` track's chart had no destination to measure against; the bar is now that each prototype has its reading: `catch_unwind`'s per-call cost on the non-unwinding path, `step`'s per-opline cost under recording and the interrupt test's cost as `bench` readings on the seven kernels (§9.2, revision 138), the dual-mapped `mov eax, 7; ret` through both views on the aarch64 box with the cache maintenance and the reused-chunk `isb` question answered, the concurrent publish test passing on both targets, and the Cranelift crate set counted and fitting `[budget] lock_packages`. The Work cell named "`jit-design.md`'s phased plan (`research/jit-research.r1.md` §E.12)", a plan `jit-design.md` did not have, cited to `research/`, which is read for history only; `jit-design.md` revision 22 adds it as its §11, §E.12 rebased on what month one built. Rejected, by the ruling: charting month two one row at a time, and copying §E.12 into this section verbatim, whose days 31–33 month one partly built. Nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: no row of §11 has an empty exit bar; `grep -n 'phased plan' third-engine-design.md` matches ledger rows alone.

**140. Revision 140 (2026-10-18).** §5.7 and §5.8 state the opline's handler as #2753 built it on t2753, settled by Mitchell on #2903 (2026-10-18) and landed by #2906. The opline is php's `zend_op` with its `handler`, a `php_unit::Handler` resolved where the unit is published, as `pass_two` calls `zend_vm_set_opcode_handler`, and thirty-two bytes, php's size on a 64-bit build, aligned so an opline's place is its index shifted; the dispatch `match` is generated over `php_unit::Handler`, in §5.7's list of `ops.toml`'s consumers, in row 7 of the seam table and in §4.1's `php_engine` row, which said the same of `php_unit::Opcode` (#2906), and §5.8's loop matches the dense `u16` each opline was resolved to. This supersedes revision 15's check, "`size_of::<Opline>()` is 24", with "`size_of::<Opline>()` is 32". §5.8's SMART_BRANCH sentence is corrected: php marks a smart branch in `zend_emit_cond_jump` (`Zend/zend_compile.c:2399` at php-8.5.9), and `pass_two`'s `zend_vm_set_opcode_handler` picks the handler from that mark (`Zend/zend_vm_execute.h:129374`); `zend_dump.c` hides the mark under `#if 0`, so a comparison resolved at publication to its `_JMPZ` or `_JMPNZ` handler leaves the op array, and op-array identity, as they were. **155. Revision 155 (2026-10-21).** §5.6 states what Mitchell ruled on #3043 (2026-10-20, a session), as #3067 carries the sentence and built it: a request is served on a stack sized for php's depth through a native's callback, and the tail-call dispatch of §5.8 (revision 147) shrinks it later. The settled sentence is typed as the ruling has it, and after it what was built: the size stated once, `php_sapi::REQUEST_STACK`, which makala's request threads read where its own 8 MiB constant stood, and the CLI's `main`, the built-in server's included, served on `fork::main_thread`, a thread of that size started through `fork::spawn`, the engine's one home, and counted inside the engine as every thread it starts is, which a fork it asks does not count beside itself, since `fork(2)` copies the asking thread alone. The thread the process started on stands out of every signal's way, `php_process::stand_aside`: every signal blocked on it, and on XNU, which lands a signal every thread blocks on the oldest thread, ended, so a signal the request blocks is delivered when it unblocks, as on php's one thread; on Linux, which keeps such a signal pending for the process, parked. This amends revisions 19 and 23's "the engine sizes no thread of its own" as far as the ruling reaches: the engine still chooses no size, and a host that serves on its main thread moves it onto one the engine starts at the host's size; §12's re-entry row says so. Rejected here: the main thread left waiting with its signals blocked, under which XNU parks a signal the request blocks on the waiting thread for good. Nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. **157. Revision 157 (2026-10-23).** §5.6 carries the sentence Mitchell settled on #3192 (2026-10-21, a session), as #3051 built the scope and #3205 its fiber clause: "A frame whose op array the request compiled for itself (an `eval`'s, an uncached include's) is served in a scope of the loop's own, one native frame of the loop's body while it stands, so its unit is given back at its return as php's `zend_leave_helper` frees the op array. php's limit moves below each scope by what the scope takes (`StackLimit::nested`), and a frame met with too little of the thread left, or on a fiber's stack, is served without one." It follows "PHP→PHP calls do not recurse on the Rust stack: the loop switches `frame` and continues.", which #3051's scope contradicted alone: each such frame costs one native frame of the loop, about 1,760 bytes, while it stands, and in exchange the loop gives that unit's share back at the frame's return rather than letting every unit go and restarting once per `eval`, 1,057 against 686 instructions per `eval` outside `INCLUDE_OR_EVAL` and the frame's return. The ruling added the fiber clause to the proposed sentence: inside a fiber `StackLimit::fiber` pins the limit at the floor, so each scope spent the fiber's own budget and a native's recursion after deep `eval` nesting was refused before php's, which broke revision 156's invariant that the engine never refuses a recursion php serves; a frame on a fiber's stack is served without a scope, and costs the unit given back at the loop's restart, as before #3051, in fibers alone. The ruling rejected a by-design row for the fiber case, which would have been that invariant's first exception, and removing the scope. Nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. **158. Revision 158 (2026-10-23).** §5.8's paragraph on the interrupt carries the sentences Mitchell ruled on #3168 (2026-10-21, a session), as #3137 built them, and `CONTEXT.md`'s **Interrupt** and `interrupt::check`'s rustdoc follow: compiled code hands the interrupt check to the loop and never answers it itself. The loop's sites gain "and where compiled code hands it the check", and compiled code's sentence says how it hands it: by an exit, php's `ZEND_JIT_EXIT_TO_VM`, or, where php's trace takes its interrupt stub (`jit_stub_interrupt_handler`, `Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_trace.c:7210-7228`), by answering `INTERRUPTED`, at which the loop makes the check through `tier::interrupted` and enters the trace again. It said compiled code tested the flag through the tier's `ExitCtx` and exited when it was set. The ruling's reason: what keeps ADR-0006 and §3.2 intact is that the loop alone answers an interrupt, and #3168's wording, "the tier makes the check", read as a second place that answers one. The old sentence's last clause, "so a compiled loop is stopped one iteration after an interpreted one would have been", was to be kept, reworded, only where #3137's loops measure it still true, and it is dropped: under `[regime.jit-inline]` on the featured debug build, a `SIGALRM` handler that records the loop's counter and ends the loop reads `$i - $seen` 0 compiled and 1 under `opcache.jit=off`, in a loop that takes the stub (`while (!$done) { $n++; }`, #3137's witness), one whose body php serves through its handler (`$s = (string)$i;`) and one that takes the `/VM` exit (`for ($i = 0; $i < $lim; $i++) { $x = 5; }`), the pinned php reading the same in each. A compiled loop meets the interrupt at the end of the turn it is in, before the condition, where the interpreted loop meets it at the back edge after the condition and runs one more body; in none of the three is it one iteration later. The ruling rejected #3168's wording and holding the ruling until t3134 merged. Nothing an ADR records is reversed, so there is no ADR. Check: `grep -n 'hands it to the loop, never answering it itself' third-engine-design.md` matches §5.8 and this row; `grep -n 'where compiled code hands it the check' crates/php_engine/src/interrupt.rs` matches; `dispatch::run`'s witnesses pass under `jit-force` and `jit-inline`.

**159. Revision 159 (2026-10-23).** §5.7's list of what a handler is resolved over gains `RETVAL`, as #3273 built it: an opcode the hot list specialises by kind whose line says `SPEC(RETVAL)`, `ASSIGN`, `PRE_INC` and `PRE_DEC`, is resolved twice for each pair of kinds, its `out` unread and read, as php's `_RETVAL_UNUSED` and `_RETVAL_USED` handlers are, so its inline path makes no run-time test of `result_type`. Resolved where its unit is published from the opline alone, as the kinds are; the op array is not changed and no dump prints the handler, so op-array identity holds. `DO_ICALL` and `DO_UCALL`, whose lines say `RETVAL` over operands of no zval kind, are resolved over nothing new, and `ASSIGN_OP`, whose line does not say it, is not resolved over it. This widens no ruling and reverses none; it states what the ticket built, and there is no ADR. Check: `publication_resolves_a_retval_opcode_over_whether_its_result_is_read` passes; `grep -n 'RETVAL. beside the kinds' third-engine-design.md` matches §5.7 and this row.

**160. Revision 160 (2026-10-23).** §5.8's fused comparison is resolved over its jump's direction too, as #3273 built it: a comparison fused to the `JMPZ` or `JMPNZ` after it takes one handler where the jump's target is past the jump and another where it is at the jump or before it, a loop's back-edge, so the fused handler makes no run-time compare of the target with its own place to decide whether to test the interrupt and raise `Site::Loop`. php makes no such compare: its `ZEND_VM_SMART_BRANCH_JMPNZ` sets the opline and tests the interrupt on every taken jump, so the direction is no `SPEC` of php's, and this engine resolves it where nothing observes the choice (§3.1). Resolved from the jump's `op2` and its place where the unit is published; the op array is not changed and no dump prints the handler, so op-array identity holds. A comparison whose line does not say `SMART_BRANCH` keeps its run-time test of the opline after it, and of the direction. This widens no ruling and reverses none; it states what the ticket built, and there is no ADR. Check: `publication_resolves_each_oplines_handler_from_it_and_the_jump_after_it` passes with its `JmpzBack` and `JmpnzBack` rows; `grep -n 'the one whose jump goes back' third-engine-design.md` matches §5.8 and this row.

## 0. The one-paragraph design

Build **Zend's execution model in Rust, once, with nothing beside it, behind nine named seams.** One value representation with Zend's layouts (16-byte zval, 8-byte refcounted header, packed/hash arrays, objects with inline property slots), one Zend-shaped allocator, one single-pass compiler from the inherited syntax tree to op arrays that are **identical to php's own**, one operand-specialised interpreter whose handler bodies are written once and monomorphised over operand kinds, synchronous destructors, Bacon–Rajan GC with Zend's root-buffer placement, generators as heap frames, fibers as real coroutines. **No second executor, no MIR, no DSL; no JIT in month one, and the tier's seam, word and safepoints designed in from day one.** Correctness comes from the real `php` binary at every interface that has one: op arrays diffed against php's dump, witnesses embedded in rustdoc, php-src's `.phpt` corpus per commit, grammar fuzzing nightly. Extensions follow one rule, **write what PHP writes, link what PHP links**, and see the engine through one locked module, `php_engine::api`, whose first version is generated from what Mahout's 19 extension crates already call. Every class, function, constant and attribute, whether written in PHP or as a native, is described once in the unit format. Immutable compiled units are shared across threads through map pointers, so one process serves many requests without a fork and without recompiling. **The crates of §4.1 in `crates/`, `php_jit` among them behind a feature, one crate per extension in `ext/`, nine seams, one declaration model.** The interpreter runs op arrays through `execute::<Nop>`; the same op arrays, the same frames and the same kernels serve `execute::<Jit>` when the feature is on, and the compiled tier reaches the engine through one lock file. Budget: the line ceilings `seams.toml` carries, per crate and in total, and the new-code ceiling within them.

Why this and not a smarter architecture: the two predecessors already ran the experiments. Mahout built three IRs and three tiers and measured its interpreter 17.5× behind php's interpreter, with the JIT tiers refused by its own gates. Dax built a semantics language that generates four hosts and measured 20× behind php with no optimizer and 118 builtins. Both spent their budgets beside the engine rather than on it. Zend's design is the one PHP VM that is known to reach the target numbers, its algorithms are fully specified by `php-src`, and its observable behaviours (GC status, memory usage, hash order, dtor timing) are only reproducible by using the same algorithms. A Rust engine that copies those algorithms with better tooling has a floor near php's interpreter by construction, and a genuine ceiling above it (threads, shared immutable units, PGO'd whole-program LTO).

---

## 1. What the month can and cannot mean

The goals are contradictory on their face. Mahout reached 99.74% of `Zend/tests` in 85 days at 3,621 commits and is at 67% of the runnable full corpus (`research/data/mahout-conformance/docs_conformance_phpt_burndown.md`). Dax's own roadmap puts practical use at "2027 with wide error bars". Nobody has done this in 30 days.

What makes 30 days defensible here, and only here:

1. **The front end is done.** Mahout's lexer, syntax tree, parser and validator (about 33k lines) parse every 8.5 construct and agreed with php on 172,118 of 172,212 verdicts over the top-1000 Packagist corpus at M0. Dax vendored them byte-identically for the same reason.
2. **The extension bodies are largely done.** Mahout's 19 `ext_*` crates are 90k lines of pure Rust behind a typed ABI that derives arginfo from Rust signatures. They port, and where they are weak (`date` 29%, `mbstring` 26%, `pcre` 62%) the weakness is a policy (pure Rust) that this design reverses.
3. **The test instruments are done.** A byte-faithful `run-tests.php` port, a grammar-driven differential fuzzer, 21,548 pinned `.phpt`, and a measurement protocol with negative controls all exist in Mahout's tree.
4. **The design is done.** `php-src/Zend` is the specification. There is no research phase.

What is written new is the core: value model, allocator, GC, compiler, interpreter, class linker, request lifecycle, SAPI. That is the part both predecessors got wrong for performance and it is about 90–110k lines.

Demonstrated velocity is the hard limit. Mahout produced ~7.6k lines/day and Dax ~6.5k lines/day of hand-written code in the same single-author, agent-driven modality (`research/data/*-git-weekly.txt`, `research/data/*-loc-*`). Thirty days at that rate is ~200k lines of new code. The budget asks for the new lines `seams.toml` ceilings plus the adaptation of the inherited tree, which fits with roughly zero slack. The schedule in §11 therefore has a published cut order, and §12 states what "parity" will and will not mean on day 30.

**Parity, defined.** Parity means that for every `.phpt` of the pinned tree the pinned oracle runs under the recipe, as upstream's `run-tests.php` runs it with the oracle's own `php-cgi`, `php-fpm` and `phpdbg` beside it, the engine's verdict is the oracle's, and every exception is an accepted row of the divergence registry with a reason a person wrote (harness §4.3). A test the oracle skips on the machine the pin names, or misses under upstream's runner, is outside the target and named on the reading; a test the oracle meets under upstream's runner and misses under this engine's is the runner's defect, a `runner` row whose target is zero (§8). What the oracle builds is what parity counts: every extension the pinned binary loads and every SAPI its build installs, less what `seams.toml`'s `[parity] out` lists, each with the ruling that put it there and its reason in §12. The reading is met-of-target per suite and in total (`xtask status`); a suite's share is its meter and not its bar.

**The port is the schedule.** Of the 130k inherited lines, 90k are extension bodies that cross an interface which does not exist yet. The census says the bodies call 89 distinct `mahout_value` methods, 83 distinct `RequestCtx` methods, 135 distinct `semantics::` functions and 38 ABI items, and 12 of the 19 crates import the linker's `ClassDescriptor` family directly (`research/data/r2-mahout-ext-seam-census.txt`). That is a 389-item interface once the `Reentrant::ctx` receiver and the request's public fields are counted (revision 2 counted 345), and every item is either provided under the same name with the same meaning, or has a line in a porting table. §4.4 makes that table an instrument (`xtask port-map`) rather than a hope.

**The tier's month is month two.** What month one carries is its seam, its word, its unit and heap sentences, its safepoints, the interrupt flag, and G1.

---

## 2. Learnings ledger

Each row is a decision for the third engine, with the evidence that forced it.

### 2.1 From Mahout

| Keep | Why |
|---|---|
| Lexer, lossless token stream, parser (`M/crates/mahout_{lexer,syntax,parser,source}`) | M0 token differential 193,377/193,377 vs php; validator verdicts 99.95% on 172k files. Dax reached the same conclusion and vendored them. The tree's representation and the validate pass are not kept (§4.9, §6.1). |
| Native ABI shape: `#[php_function]`/`#[php_class]`, marshalling traits, arginfo derived from the Rust signature (`M/crates/mahout_native_abi`, `mahout_native_macros`) | Signature written once; the shape is taken and the macro rewritten (revision 34): Mahout's `sig = "…"` strings and `raw` natives are not, since every fact they carried has a type or a `#[php(..)]` key here. |
| `run-tests.php` port (`M/crates/mahout_phpt`), grammar fuzzer (`mahout_fuzz_gen`), 14 libfuzzer targets, corpus lock file | Instruments first; both projects' own rule ("a new instrument ships with its negative control"). |
| Line-for-line `zend_sort` port, `php_gcvt` port, `serialize_precision=-1` path, the 85 php-exact message wordings, the ~800 lines of probed coercion ladders | Byte-identical on 12,343 sort calls; probes 25–28 matched. These are the parts of Mahout that copied Zend's algorithms and they are the parts that are byte-correct. See §13 for where they actually are. |
| Effect atoms on the op schema driving may-throw check placement; one `has_pending()` site | Correct and cheap. The 2026-07-28 effect audit that found 16 under-declared rows is the gate to keep. |
| Measurement protocol: status labels on artifacts, paired reps, A/A negative controls, load average recorded (`docs/perf/measurement-protocol.md`) | Every perf claim in this design is made under it. |
| `unsafe` hygiene: `SAFETY` per block, one op per block, `unsafe_op_in_unsafe_fn = deny`, Miri job, `unsafe-audit` gate | 249 unsafe sites in `mahout_value` with 206 SAFETY comments and no known soundness bug. |
| Release profile `lto="thin"`, `codegen-units=1`, measured at −33%; PGO lane | Free performance, already measured on this toolchain. |
| Interned atom table: frozen global layer + per-request overflow | Correct model for a multi-threaded worker. |
| The census itself as an instrument: the set of engine items the extension bodies call | It is the extension interface, measured. 345 items, top of the list `as_bytes` (828 sites), `len`, `get`, `set`, `throw_internal` (128), `raise_text_warning` (115). |
| `Reentrant` as a marker on the runtime context | The idea that "may run user code" should be visible to the compiler is right; it is a parameter type here (§5.10). |
| The lexer's heredoc dedent into trivia tokens and its structured interpolation runs | A deep decision: string cooking downstream never sees indentation. The tokenizer extension reconstitutes php's undedented tokens from it. |
| The phpt runner's `ScriptExec` seam (two adapters) and its byte-faithful section parser and `--EXPECTF--` translation | The seam is real; the pooled-worker adapter is replaced by the in-process one (§8). |
| The FastCGI record codec and the dev server's HTTP framing, mime and status tables | Engine-free, tested, and they port as is into `php_proto`. |
| Per-extension request state: **the shape**, one type-keyed method on the request, the state type private to its crate | A native reaches its state through the request it already holds, and no extension can name another's. The contract around it is replaced (ADR-0005). |
| Mahout's fix that keyed the tentative-return deprecation on the parent return's tentativeness and not on "the parent is a builtin" (`4802a1d5`) | Internal methods with real return types exist, `getMicrosecond(): int` among them, and override of those is a fatal as it is for user code. |

| Reject | Evidence |
|---|---|
| Two executors sharing one semantics | 83.6% co-fix rate before inversion; R168 (single-homing onto the wrong answer) after it. A second in-house executor is a second place to be wrong that agrees with the first. The oracle is the second opinion. |
| Three IRs (HIR 7.6k → MIR 58k with 12 passes → bytecode 19.6k) | Per-request compile 64.4 ms vs php's 2.4 ms; 99k lines that never produced a measured runtime win. |
| Three JIT tiers (jit1/stencil/codemem/jit2/tier2_host, 43k lines) | Tier 1 entered a compiled body on 0.28% of corpus rows forced and 0% shipped; Tier 2's default flip refused at 2.2× slower; stencils never executed on the dev machine. A JIT before an at-parity interpreter is negative value; nothing of the five crates is taken (`research/jit-research.r1.md §B.9`). |
| 16-byte `Value` with `word` tag but object properties in a `PhpArray`, `Vec<Option<(Key,Value)>>` arrays with tombstones never compacted, separate hash index | Heap charges ~2.7× php's bytes (R118), 40 MB RSS on `binary_trees` vs php's 24 MB; property access is a hash lookup where Zend does an offset load through a run-time cache. |
| Per-op summary work-word check, always-on profiling cells, generic IC keys, kernels returning `*Step` sums that the executor re-dispatches | These are the per-op terms behind "Tier 0 running 17.5× behind upstream's interpreter". Two callers of one kernel with a protocol between them is a shallow module. |
| Destructor queue drained at op boundaries | Deviates from Zend (dtors run synchronously at rc→0) and produced R84 (quadratic drain), R127 (temporaries live to frame end), R24/R31/R35. |
| `!Send` request context, process pool, panics that abort the pooled worker; `Value` with `Drop` that may run destructors (R46) | Not adoptable under a process manager. A zval is `Copy` and has no `Drop` (§5.1). |
| Allocation accounting as a caller obligation: `note_alloc` | 327 call sites in the extension crates alone. Every forgotten site is an R120-class bug. The allocator is the only accountant (§5.13). |
| A partial object-handler table (`NativeState`: 7 methods) | Zend's has 25. Every missing slot is a place an internal class cannot behave like php's: R23, the `count`/`compare`/`cast` holes. |
| Extension crates depending on `mahout_linker` and `mahout_code_meta` (12 of 19 each) | The ABI leaked into the linker's data structures. Extensions see one module (§4.3). |
| Pre-reading all of stdin when not a terminal (`sapi_cli/src/lib.rs:838-856`) | Hangs under any supervisor that leaves stdin open; added so two executors would see identical bytes. |
| Pure-Rust policy for `pcre`, `date`, `mbstring` | Caps parity at 62% / 29% / 26% and R121 (regex recompiled per call, 250× slower). PHP's behaviour in these areas *is* the C library's behaviour. |
| 60 crates, 333 lock packages, 829 markdown files, 385 plans, 62k-line xtask | Process and structure that a second engineer cannot carry; README stale by three milestones. |
| Committed generated code (`generated/` dirs, 51k lines) | Diff noise; drift between generator and output. |
| A validate pass separate from the compiler | 9,284 lines; 49 `ancestors()` and 42 `parent()` climbs to rebuild scope the compiler has; three code-number tables that deliberately disagree; `scope_import_events` quadratic in declarations. |
| Two message voices joined by a code number (`MHT` codes) | 1,124 lines of `codes.rs` whose only engine use is two columns; the join exists for a language-server voice the engine does not have. |
| Lexer-to-parser glue as a caller obligation | `parse_file` is documented and does not exist; the same four steps appear at 70 sites in 36 files. |
| A native ABI whose declared seam is 8 methods while bodies cross ~400 items | `NativeCtx` versus 130 `RequestCtx` methods, 135 `semantics::` functions, 53 field paths; one adapter. |
| Native declarations as PHP source text re-parsed at engine start; registration order as call position; class ids as a fixed table in the heap crate | ~1,000 signature strings parsed on every build; 19 hand-ordered `register()` calls; 131 extension class ids in `mahout_value`. |
| A GC contract in safe extension code (`NativeState::sever` must equal `trace_children`) | A mismatch is a double decrement. Memory safety belongs in `php_heap` (§5.4). |
| Five copies of the request transaction and three copies of header commit; a `Sapi` trait whose call order is the contract | `RunPolicy::new` at five sites; nine getters consulted in a fixed sequence at `M/crates/mahout_driver/src/run.rs:2259-2573`. |
| An oracle module that owns the path but not the recipe | The `-n -d …` recipe is spelled five ways across lanes. |
| One crate for every extension | `mahout_runtime` is a dependency of all 19 extension crates and grew to 4,649 lines of coercion alone because it was the only shared place. |
| Two extension registries that must agree | `mahout_driver`'s feature list and its 19 hand-ordered `register()` calls. |
| Lazy, first-touch extension state torn down in first-touch order; a state trait with a downcast hook; two request-end channels; contract as doc comment; no scope for cross-request state | `M/crates/mahout_runtime/src/lib.rs:7198-7210, 7337, 2818, 2781-2810`; pcre, unit and tier state in process globals under their own locks. |
| Encoding a tentative return as "no return type" and probing the oracle to recover it; modelling a tentative interface method as an abstract user method; discovering the tentative set by failing phpts | `mahout_ext_date/src/php.rs:1901-2168`; `ba3d31b0`; six commits from `188ed320` to `26cf50d5`. |

### 2.2 From Dax

| Keep | Why |
|---|---|
| **Witnesses beside the meaning**: a PHP snippet pinned to the oracle's bytes, next to the rule it tests, executed on every test run (4,446 witnesses, 4,446 pass) | The one instrument that detects convergence on a wrong answer. Kept as rustdoc blocks on kernel functions, not as a DSL. |
| Oracle pinned by digest and keg path with the exact `-n -d …` recipe (`D/oracle.toml`) | Homebrew re-pointed `php` from one patch release to the next mid-campaign (ticket 239). |
| A locked effects artifact checked by a gate (`D/engine/effects.lock`) | Generalised: every cross-crate interface has a lock file (§4.5); the effects column itself lives in `ops.lock`. |
| Dependency footprint as a tracked number (43 lock packages) | Ours will be larger because of C bindings; it is still a number in CI. |
| Refuse to specialise before measuring (D5 NO-GO honoured) | Good discipline; applies to the tier's default flip (§9.3). |
| 0 TODO/FIXME, 8 `unsafe` sites in hand-written code | The standard for every crate outside the heap core. |
| Two witness marker rules: a status is captured, never authored, and a duration discloses and never gates | Both are about capture, not syntax; they survive the move from marker lines to fence attributes (§8). |
| `tools/phpt-run.sh`: upstream's `run-tests.php` with `-p <binary>` under the oracle php, the oracle's own verdicts frozen as the control | The one arrangement in which a disagreement is never the runner's fault (§8). |
| ADR-0007's form for a gate decision: thresholds frozen before any gated number is seen | The form of the tier's backend ADR (§9.3). |

| Reject | Evidence |
|---|---|
| A semantics language that generates hosts | 433k generated lines committed, 94k–140k-line single files, 968 `unreachable!`, 74 MB RSS on a small script, no JIT emitter after D6 was declined; `mir` slower than `tier0` on every measurement. |
| A pattern-binder roster that refuses unbound productions | 322 forms cover the PHP 5-era subset after 22 days; 33 of 50 probes refused. |
| No cycle collector | Probe 24: a two-object cycle leaks to shutdown. |
| Process weight: 345 tickets (61.8k lines), 18 maps, a 12,750-line draft SPEC | Two-to-one process over product. |
| `LowerHost` with 101 methods; `Core` with 83 fields and 236 methods | Interfaces that grew until the implementation showed through. Budgets apply to interfaces, not only to lines. |
| Witness status as an output line (`exit>` inside the pin) | Needs an ordering rule so an output line beginning `exit> ` is not read as a claim. An attribute on the fence has no such line. |

### 2.3 From php-src

| Keep | Why |
|---|---|
| php-src's `ext/<name>/` layout, and `ZEND_MOD_REQUIRED` as the statement of one extension's dependency on another | The directory joins directly to `php-src/ext/<name>`, whose every `.phpt` is the crate's subset, as run-tests.php walks the directory (revision 77), and its declarations are held to the oracle's `php --re <name>` rendering (§7.1). The dependency statement becomes a Cargo edge. |
| php-src's split of pcre state at `RSHUTDOWN` (`Z/ext/pcre/php_pcre.c:474`) | The compiled pattern persists, the per-request match scratch is freed: request state versus cached immutable value, exactly. |
| `ZEND_MAP_PTR` as the one mechanism for "a shared structure holds an offset, the request owns the value" | Statics, run-time caches and request state are one table, not three (§5.14). |
| The tentative return type as one bit on the return type (`Z/Zend/zend_compile.h:1120-1128`); `gen_stub.php`'s two rules (methods only, never `final`); the optimizer's rule that a tentative type is trusted only through an exactly resolved callee | The type is the same type; only its force on overrides differs (§4.7, §6.2). The tracing tier infers nothing from a declared return type, so the third rule has no counterpart yet (§9). |
| php's trace JIT over unmodified op arrays, with deoptimisation as a jump to an opline of the identical frame (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit_trace.c`) | It is why the tier can be a seam on `execute` and not a second executor. |
| php's counter handlers at loop headers and after `RECV` as the only places the interpreter consults the tier (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit.c:3219-3244`) | Three safepoints, one hook. Anything else the tier wants it learns by stepping. |
| php's `vm_interrupt` test in every compiled loop and at every exit (`research/data/jit-research/rfc-jit.md:118`, `php-src-jit-exit-points.txt:298`); php's per-process `tracing` flag that bumps and does not trace while a trace is being recorded (`php-src-jit-hot-root.txt:30-33`) | The two rules revision 7 lacked; both are one load on a cold path (§5.8, `jit-design.md` §4.9). |
| php's `opcache.jit_debug` outputs as the tier's oracle; php's thresholds, stop list, guard set and trace bounds as numbers in one policy | The recorder, the type pass and the table each have a line of php's to diff against; behaviour php users see, under php's names and defaults. |

| Reject | Evidence |
|---|---|
| Per-thread module globals as the answer for cross-request state | The engine serves a request per thread; per-thread would rebuild pcre's pattern cache on every request against a bar of at least php-fpm's throughput (ADR-0005). |
| Compiling on the request thread inside the safepoint | php's shape because php has one thread; here the engine is `Sync` and the table publishes under one lock from any thread (`jit-design.md` §4.8). |
| One code mapping flipped RW around each publish | Sound on macOS with `MAP_JIT`, unsound on Linux where `mprotect` is per mapping (`jit-design.md` §4.4). |

### 2.4 From both, and from this engine

Both predecessors are single-author, agent-driven, and both spent most of their effort on machinery around the engine. The third engine's process rule is in §10: one design document per subject, an ADR only on reversal, an op spec that is its own documentation, and no ticket corpus in the repository.

**From this engine.** Rows are added here, Keep/Reject shape, when a sensor catches the same class of mistake twice, with a query of the store's log, `target/harness/log/<instrument>.jsonl`, as the evidence (harness rule 1). None yet.

---

## 3. Principles

1. **Zend's algorithms, Rust's compiler.** Where php's behaviour is observable (hash order, `nNextFreeElement`, `gc_status()`, `memory_get_usage()`, dtor timing, float printing, sort order, error message text), implement php's algorithm, not an equivalent one. Where php's behaviour is not observable, be free.
2. **One executor. The oracle is the second opinion.** Every semantic lives in exactly one function, called from exactly one dispatch loop. Disagreement is measured against `php`, never against another in-house executor. The compiled tier is not a second executor: it runs the same kernels on the same frames and returns to the same loop.
3. **Write what PHP writes, link what PHP links.** Zend and `ext/standard` are hand-written C in php-src; they are written in Rust here. `pcre2`, `timelib`, `libxml2`, `zlib`, `openssl`, `sqlite3`, `ICU`, `libsodium`, `libcurl`, `lexbor`, `uriparser` are libraries php links; they are linked here through `-sys` crates.
4. **Immutable after publication, shared across threads.** Compiled units, linked classes, interned strings, constant arrays and published traces are immutable and `Sync`; request state is `Send` and single-threaded. This is opcache's memory model applied to threads instead of processes; the mechanism that makes it true for classes with mutable statics is §5.14.
5. **No panic reaches a request boundary.** A PHP fatal is a value. `unwrap`/`expect` are lint-denied in engine crates; `catch_unwind` at the request boundary exists as a last line and every trip of it is a bug.
6. **The instrument ships first.** Witness runner, phpt runner, fuzzer and budget gate are in CI before the first opcode executes.
7. **Size is a gate.** Line budgets per crate and a total, checked in CI. Generated code is generated in `build.rs` and never committed.
8. **Interfaces are measured, then locked.** Every cross-crate interface is a listed set of items in a lock file. The first list is derived from the callers that already exist; growth is a diff in the same commit. Nothing is `pub` across a crate boundary that is not in a lock.
9. **Re-entry is a parameter.** Any function that can run PHP code holds a `&mut Request`, as a parameter or in a value whose lifetime names it (or the `HeapHooks` port in `php_heap`); one that holds neither cannot re-enter. Zend carries this fact in comments; here the types carry it.
10. **Declarations are data, linked once.** One declaration model describes every class, function, constant and attribute. The compiler produces it for PHP; the native macros produce it for Rust. The linker consumes it at module startup for natives and at publication for units. No parser runs at engine start; no registration depends on call order; nothing panics. The safepoints of an op array are a fact about the op array and belong in the same model.
11. **An extension crosses one name.** An extension crate depends on `php_ext` and on the extensions it declares; it cannot name `php_engine`, `php_compile` or `php_syntax` because they are not in its manifest. Enforced by crate visibility, not by a lint over the bodies.
12. **An extension holds no lock.** Request state is `&mut` and owned by the request; engine state is immutable after construction and shared by reference. An extension names no lock type: `Mutex`, `RwLock`, `Cell`, `RefCell` and the atomics are denied in `ext/*` (`ext.no-locks`). What it keeps across requests it keeps in one of the engine's two types, `api::Cache` and `api::Pool`, whose lock and bound are the engine's (§4.4).

---

## 4. Modules, interfaces, seams

Vocabulary. A **module** is anything with an interface and an implementation, at any scale. Its **interface** is everything a caller must know: signatures, invariants, ordering, error modes, cost. A module is **deep** when a lot of behaviour sits behind a small interface. A **seam** is a place where behaviour can be swapped without editing there; an **adapter** is a concrete thing that fills a seam. One adapter is a hypothetical seam; two is a real one. **The interface is the test surface.** `CONTEXT.md` carries the domain terms.

### 4.1 Crate map and budgets

The crates are `seams.toml`'s `[[crate]]` rows, counted below by the directory each is laid down in, with `xtask` apart; `xtask status --section crates --uncut` names each and says whether it is created. Budgets are ceilings enforced by `xtask budget` (non-blank, non-comment lines, tests excluded); `seams.toml` carries these numbers and no other file restates them.

<!-- computed exec cmd="cargo xtask status --section crates --tracked" inputs=seams.toml,xtask/src/status.rs,xtask/src/status/graph.rs name=crates | do not edit; run computed -->

| directory | crates | behind a feature |
|---|---|---|
| crates/ | 15 | 1 |
| ext/ | 59 | 31 |
| sapi/ | 9 | 0 |
| xtask | 1 | 0 |
