| Crate | Role | Interface (what callers learn) | Origin |
|---|---|---|---|
| `php_syntax` | Lexer, lossless token stream, typed tree in a safe arena (§4.9), parser, parse-error text. Typed nodes from `php.ungram` in `build.rs`. | `parse(&[u8], Options) -> Parsed { source, tokens, lines, tree, diagnostics }` and the typed nodes. Tokens are `(kind, offset, len)` over the retained source; the line table is built while lexing; diagnostics arrive merged in php's phase order. | Inherited: four Mahout crates merged; the green/red layer, `NodeCache`, validate, reparse, checkpoints, `codes.rs`, `root.rs`, `level.rs` removed, and of `generated/ast.rs`'s 1,064 generated lines the 833 with no reader; the remaining 231 are `check_required_children`, whose soak ADR-0003 keeps. |
| `php_heap` | **The memory model and the stacks** (revision 110, #1935). Allocator, refcounted header, zval, string, array with the `zend_hash_sort_ex` that orders it and the `zend_sort` that runs under it, object and handle table, reference, resource, weak refs, GC, VM stack and frame layout with the entry slot, the thread's call stack bounds and a fiber's machine stack, run-time cache storage, map-pointer table. `zend_compare` over the scalars is here for the reason the sort is: the crate that owns the buckets owns the order they end in, and the fold and the kernel read one ordering. The float's text, `zend_gcvt` as `numeric::double_to_string`, is here beside the numeric-string read for the same reason: the compiler's constant fold and the kernel render one text. | The value API (≈ 90 census items + the hot accessors), `Heap`, `VmStack`/`Frame`, `MapTable`, and one port: `HeapHooks`. Locked in `heap.lock`. | New; ports `zend_alloc`, `zend_hash`, `zend_string`, `zend_gc`, `zend_vm_stack` layouts; Mahout's SAFETY discipline and Miri lanes. |
| `php_process` | **The process-wide mechanisms** (revision 80, #1779): `fork`, the signal pool (§5.8, moved unchanged from `php_heap`), the process's `LC_CTYPE`, and the process's identity and its file descriptors (revision 110, #1935): its real user and group and its supplementary groups (§4.4's process group) and the calls on a host fd that `std` has no answer for, `poll`, `fcntl`, `fsync`, `dup` and the socket calls, which `php_streams` reaches through `php_engine::layer` (§4.4, revision 117). `php_heap` holds memory and stacks; `php_process` holds the process and its file descriptors. A leaf crate below `php_engine` and an `unsafe` home of its own; `php_engine` holds the policy: it joins every thread it owns, restores in the child what php's `php_child_init` restores, and re-arms its timer in the parent; pcntl asks through the request, hosts through `php_sapi`. Revision 38's condition for the crate, more than signals and `LC_CTYPE`, is met by `fork`. | `fork`, `post`, `reset_lc_ctype_locale`, `process::{uid, gid, groups}`, the fd calls (`poll_fd`, `select_fds`, the socket calls). Locked in `process.lock`, whose one consumer is `php_engine`. | New (revision 80); the pool and the locale from `php_heap`, and the process identity and the fd calls (revision 110). |
| `php_unit` | **The unit format.** Declaration model for classes, functions, constants, attributes; op arrays with their tables, safepoints, literal tables, line tables, try/catch and live ranges; the constant-expression tree; the `ops.toml`-generated opcode enum with operand kinds, effects, `may_throw`, the `safepoint` column and the disassembler in php's dump format. Types and their invariants only. | The unit value types; `Unit::dump()`. Locked in `unit.lock`. | New. |
| `php_link` | `zend_inheritance.c`: the linker that the compiler's early bind and the engine's run-time link both call, and the linked class record it returns. | `link`, `linked_class`, `LinkedClass`. Locked in `link.lock`. | Moved from `php_unit::link` (ADR-0012, #2193). |
| `php_compile` | Single pass typed tree → `php_unit::Unit`; constant expressions; early binding; compile-time fatals at the site that owns each construct; safepoint numbering with its invariant asserted. | `compile(&Parsed, &Options) -> Result<Unit, CompileError>`. | New, after `zend_compile.c`; the 136 probed edge cases of Mahout's validate move in as guards. |
| `php_engine` | Kernels, generic handlers plus generated dispatch over `php_unit::Handler`, `execute::<T: Tier>`, `step`, the `tier` module with `Nop`, exceptions, generators, fibers, linker and linked tables, object handlers, errors, ini, output, `serve`, unit cache and `Published`, native ABI traits, `api`. | Host-facing: `Engine::builder`, `Engine::serve(RequestDescription, Box<dyn OutputSink + Send>, look)`, which asks `look` what the request serves once it has started, handing it where the request's frame chain starts (§5.6), and hands the sink back with the `Observables`, `Engine::unit`. Extension-facing: `php_engine::api` (`api.lock`, under the ceiling `seams.toml` carries). Tier-facing: `tier.lock`. Stream-layer-facing: `layer.lock`. | New; tables ported from Mahout; ~3,500 lines of Zend builtins from `mahout_ext_standard`. |
| `php_macros` | `#[php_function]`, `#[php_class]`, `#[php_const]` on `fn`, `struct`, `enum`, `impl`, `const` and inline `mod`, with the inert `#[php(..)]` they read: each native's declaration derived from its Rust types and identifiers into a startup builder, and its entry emitted as php's ZPP (§7.1). Depends on no workspace crate (ADR-0007). | Three attributes. | Rewritten (revision 34); the attribute shape after `mahout_native_macros`. |
| `php_streams` | php's `main/streams/` and `main/network.c`: wrappers, transports, filters, the user wrapper and the stream context, above `php_engine`, over `api` as an extension is and over `php_engine::layer`, the engine's face for the stream layer alone (`layer.lock`, revision 117); reached by the engine only through the open hook and request state. | Extension-facing through `php_ext`, counted in `api.lock`. | Split from `php_engine` (#1773, #1874). |
| `php_ext` | **The facade.** Re-exports `php_engine::api`, `php_engine::tier::Nop` for the bundle's alias, and the three attributes, under one prelude. Nothing is defined here. The macros expand to paths in `abi` and in this crate's private module, `__private`, which holds the helpers only an expansion names. | The `api` items and the attributes. `api.lock` is this crate's public surface. | New. |
| `php_bundle` | **The enabled set, and the tier's name.** One cargo feature per non-default extension and the `jit` feature; `enabled()` returns the extensions in module-startup order, php's registration order sorted as `zend_sort_modules` sorts it; `Tier` is one alias. php-src's `internal_functions.c`. | `enabled() -> &'static [fn() -> Extension]`; `type Tier`; the feature list. | New. |
| `php_jit` | **The compiled tier.** As `jit-design.md` designs it: recorder over `step`, trace SSA with php's guard set, lowering behind a backend value with the interrupt test at the loop header, one table in a dual-mapped arena, one dispatch with recording exclusive per request, the crossing, php's thresholds as a pure state machine, one compile worker per engine. `#![deny(unsafe_code)]` with the allowance on `table::code` and `enter`, about 300 lines. Behind the `jit` feature on Linux and macOS for x86-64 and aarch64 and nowhere else until a second `Pages` adapter exists. | `Jit: Tier`; nothing else is `pub`. Reads `php_engine` through `tier.lock`. | New. Shape after `zend_jit_trace.c`; nothing from Mahout's five JIT crates. |
| `timelib_sys` | **timelib, vendored** (revision 109, #711): php-src's `ext/date/lib` byte for byte at the tag `oracle.toml` pins, with its `LICENSE.rst` and bundled timezone database, compiled by `build.rs` from `config0.m4`'s `timelib_sources` and `PHP_TIMELIB_CFLAGS`, and its C interface declared in Rust. An `unsafe` home of its own, below `ext/date`, whose `src/ffi/` wraps it in owned values. | timelib's structs and functions as `timelib.h` and `timelib_private.h` declare them; `timelib_time`'s two pointers read through methods and written through one `unsafe fn`. No lock: one caller, as a `-sys` crate has. | Moved from `ext/date` (#656's `lib/`, `build.rs` and `src/ffi/sys.rs`). |
| `ext/<name>` | One extension each; §7.2 lists them. `src/ffi/` only where a `-sys` crate is bound. | `extension() -> Extension`, plus what the crate deliberately exports to dependent extensions. | Ported (`M/crates/mahout_ext_<name>`) + bindings. |
| `php_proto` | FastCGI record codec; HTTP/1.x framing, static file serving, mime and status tables for `-S`; CGI environment parsing into plain fields. Names no engine type. | Codecs. | Ported (`M/crates/mahout_sapi_fcgi/src/fcgi.rs`, `mahout_sapi_cli/src/server.rs` minus its engine coupling). |
| `php_sapi` | The hosts' facade (ADR-0008): `php_engine`'s host face, `sapi.lock`, re-exported, and what php-src shares between SAPIs: the CGI environment made a request description, header emission, `php_getopt`, and `php_init_config`'s search for php.ini and the scan directory (revision 57). | The host face. | New (revision 46). |
| `sapi/*` | One crate per SAPI, each its binary: `sapi/cli` (`php`: argument parsing, `-c`, `-n`, `-d`, `-r -f -l -i -a -S -m -v --ini --ri`, the `-S` server), `sapi/cgi` (`php-cgi`), `sapi/fpm` (`php-fpm`), `sapi/phpdbg`, `sapi/embed`, `sapi/apache2handler`, `sapi/litespeed`, `sapi/fuzzer`: each builds a request description from `php_proto`'s output and supplies a sink. A host's natives are declared in its own crate over `php_ext` and listed in its `additional_functions`, as php registers a SAPI's (`Z/main/main.c:2361`; #1348): `cli_set_process_title`, `cli_get_process_title` and the title's argv area, made in `src/ffi/`, are `sapi/cli`'s, and the request description carries only `PHP_CLI_PROCESS_TITLE`'s state (`php_sapi::ProcessTitle`) (revisions 48, 67). The debugging host reads frames, op arrays and the linked tables through the native face from its own tier, as an extension does; `tier.lock` keeps the mechanism, the writes and nothing that reads (revision 116, #1911). | Hosts; argv. | CLI ported; the rest new (revision 46). |
| `xtask` | `witness`, `phpt`, `compile-identity`, `port-map`, `api-check`, `fuzz`, `bench`, `budget`, `unsafe-audit`, `crosscheck`, `vocab`, `permit`, `done`, `status`, `new-ext`, with `oracle` as the one module every lane calls. `mahout_phpt` and `mahout_fuzz_gen` live here as modules. | Subcommands; readings (harness §4.2). | Inherited pieces: of Mahout's 62k-line `xtask`, what survives is the instruments named here, under `seams.toml`'s ceiling. |

Each extension's ceiling is `seams.toml`'s, derived from Mahout's line counts, `standard` less the 3,500 lines of Zend builtins that §13 moves to the engine, with one pooled ceiling for the bindings across every extension that binds a `-sys` crate.

The total ceiling, the new code within it, the `docs/` lines and the lock packages are `[budget]` in `seams.toml`, and `xtask budget` is what holds the workspace to them; the tier's own ceiling is month two's. Third-party dependencies, default feature set: `corosensei`, `memchr`, `hashbrown` (raw table for the compiler's symbol maps only; PHP arrays are `php_heap`'s own), `pcre2-sys`, `libz-sys`, `libsqlite3-sys`, `libxml` bindings, `timelib_sys` (vendored, a crate of the workspace), RustCrypto hash crates, `libc`, `arc-swap`; under `jit`, Cranelift's crates and `rustix` or `memmap2`. The lock packages are counted for the default build on day 31, with the Cranelift set.

**The install layout is the build's** (revision 60). What configure writes into php's `main/build-defs.h` (the prefix and the directories under it, `include_path`'s and `extension_dir`'s defaults, the php.ini search path and scan directory) `php_engine`'s `build.rs` reads from the build's environment beside the build stamp, each defaulting to what php's configure computes with no option given; every host reads them from the engine, and nothing names another installed php's directories.

**`unsafe`** is permitted only in the homes `seams.toml`'s `unsafe.homes` rule names: `php_heap`; `php_process`; `ext/*/src/ffi/`; `sapi/*/src/ffi/`; `timelib_sys`; `php_jit::table::code`; `php_jit::enter`. Every other crate is `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`; an extension or a host with bindings and `php_jit` are `#![deny(unsafe_code)]` with the allowance on the named module alone; `xtask unsafe-audit` reads the homes from that rule and fails on a site outside them (ADR-0004 as amended, ADR-0006 as amended, ADR-0008; revision 80).

### 4.2 Dependency graph

The permitted edges are `seams.toml`'s `[[edge]]` rows, rendered here: a crate depends on a workspace crate only along a row, and a row marked `denied` is a dependency nothing may take.

<!-- computed exec cmd="cargo xtask status --section edges --tracked" inputs=seams.toml,xtask/src/status.rs,xtask/src/status/graph.rs name=edges | do not edit; run computed -->

| from | to | as | adr |
|---|---|---|---|
| php_unit | php_heap |  |  |
| php_link | php_unit, php_heap |  |  |
| php_compile | php_syntax, php_unit, php_link, php_heap |  |  |
| php_engine | php_compile, php_unit, php_link, php_heap, php_macros, php_process |  | ADR-0007 |
| php_streams | php_engine, php_macros |  |  |
| php_ext | php_engine, php_macros, php_streams | re-exports only |  |
| ext/* | php_ext, ext/*, timelib_sys | these only | ADR-0004 |
| php_bundle | ext/*, php_ext, php_jit | registration order | ADR-0004, ADR-0006 |
| php_jit | php_engine, php_unit, php_link, php_heap | imports only what tier.lock lists | ADR-0006 |
| php_sapi | php_proto, php_engine | through sapi.lock |  |
| php_engine::{executor,kernels} | php_engine::{serve,headers,gpc,output} | denied |  |
| sapi/* | php_sapi, php_proto, php_bundle, php_ext |  | ADR-0008 |

<!-- /computed in=0a4e0b338657a6573ec10acfd4ae23b08d66ef6834eaa2ea1957817a3d3fd16e out=5f28278f2b9b7afde8e31c26cde0c1e4273513e0448f142cb62c5a2fb9516f2b -->

What the rows do not say. `php_heap` depends on nothing in the workspace. `php_unit` depends on `php_heap` for names interned and literals as heap values. `php_engine` depends on `php_macros` because its attributes declare Core's natives; `php_macros` depends on no workspace crate, only on `syn`, `quote` and `proc-macro2`: a macro emits paths and never links what they name (ADR-0007). `php_ext` re-exports `api` (with `api::__private`), `tier::Nop` and the attributes only. An `ext/*` crate depends on `php_ext` and on the `ext/*` crates it declares, never on `php_engine`, `php_compile`, `php_syntax` or `php_bundle`. `php_bundle` reaches `php_jit` under `jit` on the targets `php_jit`'s `[[crate]]` row names, and names `php_jit::Jit` and nothing else; nothing depends on `php_jit` but the bundle, and `php_jit` depends on nothing under `ext/`. `php_sapi` never sees `api`; `php_proto` sees nothing. `xtask` links no workspace crate: it reaches the engine through the binaries it builds and starts, `php-linked` and the hosts' own (harness revision 84). Workspace members are the directories §4.1's count names; an extension's package is `php_ext_<name>` at path `ext/<name>`, a host's `php_sapi_<name>` at `sapi/<name>` (ADR-0008).

`xtask api-check` fails the build on a manifest edge outside these rows, on a `use` path that crosses a seam, on a bundle feature that does not switch on what its extension requires, and, inside `php_engine`, on any `use` from the executor and kernel modules into the serve, headers, GPC or output-buffering modules, which keeps a later `main/`-style crate split possible without making it now. `php_jit` may import from `php_engine` only what `tier.lock` names.

### 4.3 The nine seams

Each has at least two adapters, so each is real. Zend's equivalents are from `research/data/r2-zend-seam-sizes.txt`.

| # | Seam | Lives in | Interface | Adapters | Zend equivalent |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `HeapHooks` (port, upward) | `php_heap` | 6 methods: `heap() -> &mut Heap`, `destruct(obj)`, `free_native(obj)`, `gc_children(obj, visit)`, `free_resource(res)`, `out_of_memory(bytes) -> Fatal`. Five are what the heap needs done above it; the sixth hands the heap back. | `Request` (production); `Recorder` (heap tests under Miri, records what would have run) | `zend_objects_store_del`, `get_gc`, `zend_mm` limit callback |
| 2 | `ObjectHandlers` | `php_engine` | Zend's 25 slots (`read_property` … `get_gc`, `do_operation`, `compare`, `get_properties_for`) with `Std` as the default impl of every slot | `Std`; `Closure`, `Generator`, `Fiber`, `WeakMap`, `WeakReference`, enums, `ArrayObject` family, `SplFixedArray`, `SplObjectStorage`, `DateTime` family, `Reflection*`, DOM nodes, lazy proxies | `zend_object_handlers` (25) |
| 3 | `Extension` | `php_engine::api`, named to extensions as `php_ext` | The `Extension` value of §7.1: declarations paired with entries, constants, ini entries, lifecycle functions, `process_state(ctor)`, `engine_state(ctor)` and `request_state::<T>()`; one `extension()` per crate; `Engine::builder().extensions(..)` takes the slice | One crate per extension under `ext/`; `TestExt` in engine tests; the empty slice, which must serve a Zend-only script; the tier's own state through `Tier::extension()` | `zend_module_entry`, including `globals_size`, `globals_ctor` and `globals_dtor`; the bundle is `internal_functions.c` |
| 4 | **Request description** (input) and **`OutputSink`** (output) | `php_engine` | Input: one plain `Send` value built before the request starts: argv, env, cwd, server vars, request headers, body stream, sapi name, `is_web`. Output: `write`, `flush`, `send_headers(block)`, `log`, `write_stderr`. The request owns its sink while it runs: `serve` takes it, and hands it back when the request ends, and every call reaches it as the request makes it, php's `sapi_module` timing, so a child process sharing the host's stdout interleaves as under php's CLI (revision 34). The ini files a host found are part of what it hands the builder, each a path and its bytes, which the engine reads with its one grammar after the host's defaults and before its `ini_entries`, php's order; a builder handed none has read no php.ini, as `-n` (revision 57). The engine owns the header list, status, default content type, output buffering and the commit moment; the log and the error stream are two channels, and whether they land on one fd is the host's, as php's CLI puts both on stderr and a file `error_log` parts them. | Inputs built by `cli`, `cgi`, `fcgi`, dev server, embed, xtask in-process; sinks: stdout, CGI, FastCGI, dev-server response buffer, **`Capture`** (in-process tests) | `sapi_module_struct` split into `sapi_request_info` and the four output callbacks, and the process's `stderr` that `php_error_cb`'s stderr form and the `STDERR` stream write past the SAPI |
| 5 | `StreamWrapper` / `Stream` | `php_streams` | Zend's wrapper ops (open, opendir, stat, unlink, rename, mkdir, rmdir, metadata) and stream ops (read, write, seek, flush, close, stat, cast, set_option) | `file`, `php`, `data`, `glob`, user wrappers; `phar`, `http` (month two) | `php_stream_wrapper_ops`, `php_stream_ops` |
| 6 | `Operand` (compile-time) | `php_engine` | `read(frame, opline) -> Zval`, `read_ptr`, `free` for one operand position | `Const`, `Tmp`, `Var`, `Cv`, `Unused` marker types; handler bodies are generic over two of them | `OP1_TYPE`/`OP2_TYPE` in `zend_vm_def.h`, expanded by `zend_vm_gen.php` |
| 7 | `ops.toml` → opcode enum and dispatch | enum, operand kinds, effects, `may_throw`, `safepoint` column, disassembler generated in `php_unit`'s `build.rs`; dispatch `match` generated in `php_engine`'s `build.rs` over `php_unit::Handler` | One spec, two build scripts, one enum | `php_compile` (emits), `php_engine` (dispatches), `php_unit::dump` (prints), xtask (disassembles) | `zend_vm_opcodes.h` generation |
| 8 | `Tier` | `php_engine::tier`; `execute` is generic over it; bound per `Engine` as an `ExecuteFn` by `EngineBuilder::tier::<T>()`, which also registers the tier's state; named once in `php_bundle` | One method, `safepoint(req, frame, Site) -> Flow`, where `Site` is `Loop(n)`, `Entry` or `Return(n)` and carries the safepoint's number, and `Flow` is `Continue`, `Resume(frame)` or `Bail`; one provided method, `extension()`, through which the tier declares its engine state, request state and ini under seam 3 without being an extension. Beside it, `step(req, frame) -> Result<Stepped, Bailout>`, which runs exactly one handler with `Nop` at the safepoints and reports `Same`, `Pushed`, `Popped`, `Moved`, `Threw` or `Done`; and one opaque `Word` per safepoint on `Func`, zero at publication, sealed for a request-scoped op array, whose bits only the tier interprets | `Nop` (month one, in `php_engine`); `php_jit::Jit`; `Probe` (engine tests, asserting `(site, number)` sequences) | `zend_execute_ex` as the bound entry; the counter handlers at loop headers and after `RECV` (`Z/ext/opcache/jit/zend_jit.c:3219-3244`); `zend_jit_trace_execute` has no equivalent, because `step` is what it exists to be |
| 9 | `Observer` | `php_engine` | Declared on the `Extension` value, as every declaration is, with no registration interface at engine start. A per-function initialiser (php's `zend_observer_fcall_register`) that may decline is resolved on a function's first call into a slot in its run-time cache, not indexed by the function's identity number; begin handlers run in declaration order, end handlers in reverse; handlers are added to and removed from one function at run time. Checked at the call and return homes against one engine-level word fixed at engine build; `end` pairs through exception unwinds and bailouts. Notices: errors, declared functions, linked classes, fiber creation, switch and destruction. A handler receives a borrowed, frame-focused view, `&mut Observed<'_>`, which derefs to the request; no frame handle outlives the handler, and a handle that can be stored is #1827's | ext/zend_test's observer (`ext/zend_test/src/observer.rs`, driven by php's observer tests); the recording test observer (`crates/php_engine/src/tests/observer.rs`, #1809); and none declared, which the loop pays nothing for, held by a bench reading with its A/A control (#2011) | `Z/Zend/zend_observer.h` |

What a caller of seam 8 must know. `safepoint` runs inside two handlers and one activation: a backward `JMP*` after operand reads and before the jump; `RETURN*` after operand reads and before the pop; and the activation of a user frame, by the call handlers and by `execute` for its own entry frame, after the `RECV*` prologue has run without raising (§5.6). `Site::Return` is never raised for the entry frame of an `execute`: the handler compares the frame with the stack's entry slot before calling the tier. `Site::Entry` is raised for the entry frame, as php's counter fires whatever the caller is. After a `Resume` the loop performs the interrupt check it performs at a back-edge and the pending-exception check it performs after a `throw`-effect op, then dispatches from the given frame. `step` never runs `HANDLE_EXCEPTION`, so one call is one handler and never an unwind; a fused compare-and-branch is one handler over two oplines (§5.8); `Threw` wins over a push or pop the same handler did, and `Done` may carry a pending exception, as php's leave helper returns to a native caller with `EG(exception)` set. `Nop` is one inlined function returning a constant and the branch on `Flow` folds; `Stepped` is a discriminant the loop already holds. A tier declares whether it hooks the executor, as php's replaced `zend_execute_ex` and `zend_execute_internal` do, and the engine compiles every unit under that declaration, so a unit's calls are the ones `zend_get_call_op` picks for the same host (revision 58); the same switch serves any later tier that replaces the executor, as phpdbg and xdebug's step debugger do in php; an observer changes no call opcode (`zend_get_call_op`, `Z/Zend/zend_compile.c:3919-3949`, reads `zend_execute_ex` and `zend_execute_internal` alone) and is seam 9's (revision 79). A tier also declares whether it holds the main frame before it executes it, `Tier::HOLDS`, false by default, as phpdbg holds the program it compiled at its prompt: `execute`, php's `zend_execute`, raises the main frame's observer begin (seam 9) before the loop unless the tier holds the frame, and a tier that holds it raises the begin with `tier::begin` where it executes the frame, phpdbg's `run`; `Request::push_frame` raises none (revision 118, #2359). The deletion test: month one has `Nop` and `Probe`, two adapters, so the seam is real before `php_jit` exists. Full interface, invariants and the tier's modules: `jit-design.md` §2.

What a caller of seam 9 must know (revision 79). A frameless call whose function is observed falls back to a full call, as `Z/Zend/zend_vm_def.h:9775` does. The named escape if G1 (#1808) refuses the plain check: php's own, observer-specialised variants of the call and return handlers alone, never a second instantiation of the loop. What compiled code emits at observed calls, returns and native calls: `jit-design.md` §4.3.

**Deliberately not seams.** The executor (one). The value representation (one). The allocator (one; the system allocator used for compiler temporaries is a different domain, not an adapter). The syntax tree. The backend behind the tier's lowering, which is a value inside `php_jit` and not a seam of the engine. Anything with one adapter is an indirection, and this design has none.

### 4.4 The extension-facing interface: `php_engine::api`

This is the widest interface in the system and the one on which 41% of the budget sits. It is a module interface, not a seam: it has one implementation and many callers. It is designed as a deep module in the sense that matters for the port: a large amount of behaviour (the whole engine) behind a list of items that is smaller than what Mahout's extensions already use.

Sizing. Zend's extension-facing headers declare 832 `ZEND_API`/`PHPAPI` functions. Mahout's extensions use 389 distinct engine items at the pin (`data/api-lock-v0.md`; the revision-2 census said 345). `php_ext`'s request face is organised as facets by area, `req.symbols()`, `req.ini()` and their kin, each a zero-cost value over the whole request, since nearly every operation can serve PHP code, so split borrows are not a goal. The facets are for structure: the face found by area, its rustdoc grouped by area, an area's invariants in its own module, and growth read per area; they do not lower the item count. Each is a type of its own, `Symbols<R>`, read through `req.symbols()` over `&Request` and changed through `req.symbols_mut()` over `&mut Request`, as `iter` and `iter_mut` are, so a read keeps the shared borrow a `&self` method had; a method keeps its typed signature in its facet and is never collapsed into an untyped `op(Kind)`, an enum standing only where php's own operation set is one (`WrapperOp`). The extension state accessors of the table below stay on `Request`, and the stream layer's operations stay `request::Streams`, the trait `php_streams` implements for it (#1875). There is no ceiling per facet: one ceiling, `api_items` in `seams.toml`, holds the whole face, measured on `php_ext`, where a public-items dump is the whole crate, and a ceiling reached is raised by a person with the diff that needs it (#697, revision 154). `api::__private`, the helpers only a macro expansion names, is listed and diffed like every other line but counted against a cap of its own rather than the ceiling, so its growth is also a person's decision (revision 34); v0, drafted from the census before day 1, has 301.

Shape. Seven groups, six mirroring the census rather than Zend's file layout, and the process's identity, which the engine owns (revision 41):

| Group | What | Census origin |
|---|---|---|
| Values | zval constructors and accessors, strings, arrays, objects, references, resources, `Key` normalisation | 89 `mahout_value` items; the 20 most-called are kept by name (`as_bytes`, `len`, `get`, `set`, `is_empty`, `as_object`, `contains`, `append`, `as_array`, `class`, `tag`, `as_int`, `instance_of`, `lookup`, …) |
| Raising | `throw(class, msg)`, `warning`, `notice`, `deprecated`, `fatal`, `arg_type_error`, `arg_count_error`, `too_few_arguments`, with `Thrown` as the error type every native returns. The engine knows the active native's qualified name and parameter names before the body runs, so the raising items take the argument position and the tail of the message, and the engine renders `fn(): Argument #N ($name) must …` itself; a native body never hand-types its own name (Mahout's did at 203 sites). | `throw_internal` (128), `raise_text_warning` (115), and the 135 `semantics::` functions collapse to ≈ 40 |
| Coercion and conversion | `to_long`, `to_double`, `to_string`, `truthy`, `coerce_param` honouring the caller's `strict_types`, `loose_eq`, `compare` | the `coerce_*`/`cast_*` family (12 items) |
| Calling back | `call(callable, args)`, `instantiate`, `new_object(class)`, `lookup_function`, `lookup_class`, autoload | `Reentrant`, `native_call_by_name`, `instantiate_no_ctor`, `lookup_func` |
| Request environment | output, headers, ini get/set, streams and resources, `cwd`, `temp_dir`, superglobals, error/exception handler stack, `current_line`, `gc_enable`; **`req.state::<T>() -> &mut T`**, **`req.engine::<E>() -> &E`**, **`api::Cache<K, V>`** | 83 `RequestCtx` items less `note_alloc` (gone) and less what was linker leakage; the state accessor's shape |
| Syntax | the token stream of one source and php's names for its kinds, what `ext/tokenizer` reads (`token_get_all`, `token_name`, the `T_*` constants at the pin); `tokenize` and the kind table, and the parse under `TOKEN_PARSE`: `parse`, its `Options`, the `Parsed` report with its `Diagnostic`s and `Level`, and the `Tree` as `Element`s over `TokenId`s, the shape `token_get_all` reads where php's `tokenize_parse` reads the parser's events. The node types the compiler walks cross nowhere; an extension that needs one is a widening of this row, not a use of it. Reached through `php_compile::syntax`, since an extension's edge stops at `php_ext` (ADR-0004) and the engine's at `php_compile`. | none: Mahout's tokenizer read the lexer directly |
| Process | the process's real and effective user and group ids and its supplementary groups, `process::uid`, `euid`, `gid`, `egid` and `groups`, which php reads from `<unistd.h>` wherever it asks: `php_stat`'s and `phar_fancy_stat`'s permission masks, `ps_files_open`'s ownership rule, `posix_getuid`, `posix_geteuid`, `posix_getgid`, `posix_getegid` and `posix_getgroups`. Engine policy as the signal record is (ADR-0005), bound once in `php_process`'s `unsafe` home, and not an export between extensions (§4.10). | none: each extension bound its own |

**The stream layer's face** (revision 117). What `php_streams` alone needs of the engine is a second face of `php_engine`, sealed from extensions, and not a widening of this one, as Mitchell ruled on #1959 (2026-10-07, shape 1). It is the module `php_engine::layer`: a trait, `layer::Face`, implemented for `Request` and sealed, since a module `php_ext` does not re-export cannot hide a method of a type it does, and free items beside it, `StreamHooks`, what the engine asks of the layer in turn, and the fd calls `php_process` binds (§4.1). `php_ext` re-exports none of it, and `layer.lock` lists it (§4.5).

**The `open_basedir` guard** (revision 42). php's `php_check_open_basedir_ex` is one function in `php_engine`, beside the virtual cwd it resolves through, and the only constructor of `HostPath`, which every `api` item that reaches the host's filesystem by name takes; it runs where php runs it, in the plain files and glob wrappers for what a stream opens, stats, renames or removes and in the native for what reaches no wrapper, with php's one bit of whether it warns, so the warning, its order and a silent refusal stay php's and a filesystem call the guard did not see is a type error rather than a missed site.

**The locale a request set** (revision 56). The request's environment facet carries the locale name `setlocale` set for each category, beside the request's `locale_t` (§5.14), as php keeps `BG(locale_string)` and `BG(ctype_string)`; an extension that reads the locale, as ext/pcre keys its compiled-pattern cache by the `LC_CTYPE` name, reads it there through `php_ext`, and no extension exports its own locale state to another.

**Walking a table** (revision 92). A native walks a table in one of four ways. A rows walk borrows the request, so a body that can run user code does not compile in it. A foreach walk reads the table's rows as they stand, up to where they ended as it began, as php's `ZEND_HASH_FOREACH` does; it holds the table and guards its recursion where php's native does, and where it holds nothing it ends at the table's destruction rather than read it freed. A Live walk follows php's hash iterator, re-reading the holder after each step and raising php's `TypeError` when it no longer holds an array or object. A Detached walk works on a private copy it swaps back. The three callback walks keep each step's key and value alive for the step and lend them to the callback, owning them only where php's native does and otherwise pinning them, which php's counts do not show; they take an array or an object's property table, and clean up on every exit. A pin, the walk's and the one a frame takes of a receiver it holds no owner of, keeps only a block's bytes: where the pinned block's last owner goes, its destructor, its rows or members, an object's id and the memory php counts for it go at php's moment, and its bytes wait. The engine's hold is the owner type.

The declaration types in `api` (`FunctionDecl`, `ClassDecl`, `ConstDecl`, `IniDecl`, parameter and attribute descriptors, the constant-expression tree) are re-exports of `php_unit`; there is no second model. The marshalling traits are `FromArgument` and `IntoReturn`, and every fact a Rust type says about a parameter or a return is an item of one of them, so the vocabulary widens by an `impl`, never by teaching the macro a name (§7.1). The traits a class header and its members implement (`Class`, `Header`, `Members`, `Cases`, `PropertyValue`, `IntoConst`) are `abi`'s, beside the one builder method they need, `ExtensionBuilder::class::<C>()`; the helpers only an expansion calls are `api::__private`'s, and an `ext/*` source that names `__private` is a manifest rule's failure. Native classes declare properties, methods, constants and, where needed, an `ObjectHandlers` impl, and never see the linker's descriptors. What these types declare is held from the outside: `xtask crosscheck` renders each bundled extension as `php --re <name>` does, through `ReflectionExtension::__toString()` over the linked tables, and holds the bytes to the oracle's under the recipe (§7.1, §8); no sensor reads the declaration model beside it.

**State** (ADR-0005). An extension declares its state on its `Extension` value and reaches it through the request:

| Item | Scope | Declared on `Extension` as | Built | Dropped |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `req.state::<T>() -> &mut T` | one request | `request_state::<T: Default>()` | at request start, in startup order | after the `rshutdown`, `deactivate` and `post_deactivate` hooks, in reverse startup order |
| `start.process::<P>() -> &'static P` | the process | `process_state(fn() -> Result<P, Refusal>)`, `P: Send + Sync + 'static` | at most once per process, the first time an engine carrying the extension is built, immediately before that extension's engine start; it sees no ini and no host name | never: no host ends the process scope, and process exit releases it (ADR-0010) |
| `req.engine::<E>() -> &E` | one `Engine` | `engine_state(fn(&Start) -> Result<E, Refusal>)`, `E: Send + Sync + 'static` | as the engine is built, in startup order, from what `Start` carries: the startup ini, the host's name and the process states; this is `MINIT`, and a `Refusal` is its failure | after the `engine_end` hooks, which the drop of the engine's last clone serves, in reverse startup order; this is `MSHUTDOWN` |
| `api::Cache<K, V>` | one `Engine` | a field of `E`, given a capacity | with `E` | with `E` |
| `api::Pool<K, V>` | one `Engine`, lent to one request at a time | a field of `E`, or the stream layer's engine state, which the engine builds from `StreamHooks::state` and closes first at engine end through `StreamHooks::engine_end` (#1962) | with `E` | with `E`: every entry closed, as `zend_destroy_rsrc_list(&EG(persistent_list))` closes them |

The state type is private to its crate; a request for a type no bundled extension declared is a bug and panics with the type name. The slot is a `MapPtr<T>` assigned when the engine's build publishes the extension, so access is an index read into the request's map table. `E` is built once per `Engine` from the startup ini view, which is what makes it per engine rather than a crate `static`: tests build a fresh `Engine` per phpt in-process, and engine state can depend on system ini such as `pcre.jit`. **`E` names no lock type** (principle 12). What it may contain is tables, handles to immutable library objects, and the two engine types in which an extension keeps what lasts across requests, `Cache` and `Pool`, whose lock and bound are the engine's. `Cache<K, V>` has one method, get-or-build by key, returning a shared handle to an immutable `V: Send + Sync`; bounding, eviction, sharding and the lock are the engine's. php's pcre bound (4,096 entries, then a partial clean, `Z/ext/pcre/php_pcre.c:817`) becomes the capacity pcre passes. A zval held in request state is released in `rshutdown`, because the state drops before the request leak gate runs; the gate catches what is not and names the state type.

**`xtask port-map`.** Regenerates the census against Mahout's tree and joins it to `api.lock`: every Mahout item is `same`, `renamed` (with the new name), or `gone` (with the replacement rule, e.g. `note_alloc → nothing; the allocator accounts`; `ext_state_mut::<T>() → state::<T>()` at 35 sites in 6 crates; `ExtState::ext_teardown → rshutdown` where it reads the request, `Drop` where it only releases; `sig_tentative → #[php(tentative)]`). The join is the porting checklist and its unmatched rows are a CI failure from day 3; it writes a reading like any instrument.

**Matched, and resolved.** A row is *matched* when its replacement exists: a `same` or `renamed` row's `api` item is a line of `api.lock`, a `gone` row's rule is written. That is the join above, and an unmatched row is the CI failure. *Resolved* is the port's own work: **a row is resolved when every extension crate the census counted for it is ported.** The census carries, per row, the `ext/*` crates whose bodies called the item, a column `--tree` regenerates; the v0 seed carries the count alone and gains the names with the first port. **Ported** is what the harness can read without a binary: the crate's `extension()` registers at least one native. The twenty crates `new-ext` lays down on day 1–2 have empty bodies and call no `api` item, so none of them is ported, no row is resolved, and the instrument writes all 651 rows unresolved — §11 row 1–2's number reproduced by the measure rather than asserted beside it. The reading is one, at the crate `api.lock` lives with, `php_ext` (harness §7's `crate` scope); its `unresolved` is the number of rows an unported crate is still left on, and it reaches zero on the day the last inherited extension is ported, which is why §11 carries the zero at row 22–30.

**Design it twice.** Two shapes were considered for the value half. (A) Zend's own vocabulary (`zend_hash_find`, `zend_string_init`, `ZVAL_LONG`) as safe Rust: honest to the port, and unfamiliar to every line that has to cross it. (B) Mahout's vocabulary over Zend's layouts: 90k lines of callers already know it, and layout knowledge stays inside `php_heap`. (B) is chosen for `api`; (A)-shaped accessors exist as `php_heap::hot` for the handlers only and are not re-exported by `api`.

### 4.5 Interface locks

The set is declared once in `seams.toml`, as its `[[seam]]` rows, and `xtask status --section locks` renders it:

<!-- computed exec cmd="cargo xtask status --section locks --format md --tracked" inputs=seams.toml,crates/**/*.lock,xtask/xtask.lock,xtask/src/status.rs name=locks | do not edit; run computed -->

| seam | lock | items | ceiling |
|---|---|---|---|
| heap | heap.lock | 463 |  |
| process | process.lock | 40 |  |
| unit | unit.lock | 125 |  |
| link | link.lock | 62 |  |
| ops | ops.lock | 209 |  |
| api | api.lock | 1499 | 1500 |
| sapi | sapi.lock | 58 |  |
| tier | tier.lock | 130 |  |
| layer | layer.lock | 38 |  |
| harness | xtask.lock | 423 |  |

<!-- /computed in=bcaeeabfa6a2f7c159e03383560ff2b592b40598bea2b8169f68cf8936002448 out=f8e92ab88ad8754cc8c037a877336aa2edb9b1f2523d5bed6ca5efcb864de02d -->

What a lock holds that its row does not say. `heap.lock` carries the VM stack's entry slot and its peers, `VmStack::entry`, `push_entry`, `current` and `MapTable::base`. `tier.lock` is the widening of `php_engine` toward the tier, in two sections: the face, a few dozen items reviewed one by one, and the kernels named by `php_jit`'s `kernels!` list, about three hundred, generated from the list and diffed as one section. `process.lock` is `php_process`'s surface, whose one consumer is `php_engine`. `link.lock` is `php_link`'s surface, the linker and the linked class record that the compiler and the engine both call (ADR-0012). Dax's `effects.lock` is retired, its content being `ops.lock`'s effects column. A lock is one of three kinds, and `xtask api-check` regenerates each from what it is a lock of: the public items of the crate the lock lives with (`heap.lock`, `process.lock`, `unit.lock`, `link.lock`, `api.lock`, and the harness's own `xtask.lock`), or of one module of its producer (`layer.lock`, where a crate outside the seam's consumers that `use`s the module fails); what the consumers import from the producer (`sapi.lock`, `tier.lock`, which is why both are headers carrying nothing until a consumer exists); or the rows of the file the seam is generated from (`ops.lock`, rendered from `ops.toml`'s `[[op]]` rows). All three fail on any difference; adding an item means committing the new lock line with the code. This is how "smallest possible codebase" is measured at the interface, where callers pay, not only in lines.

### 4.6 The deletion test, applied

Delete `php_compile`: every caller would need to know how to turn a tree into an op array. Delete `php_heap`: layout and refcount knowledge would spread through every kernel and every native, which is Mahout's `note_alloc` at 327 sites. Delete `php_process`: `fork`, the signal pool, `LC_CTYPE`, the process identity and the fd calls fall into `php_engine` and the extensions that call them, and the process-wide `unsafe` gets a home per caller instead of one audited crate that depends on nothing in the workspace. Delete `php_link`: the linker returns to `php_unit`, and one ceiling measures a format and a linker again; it cannot go into `php_engine`, which calls the compiler that calls it (ADR-0012). Delete `php_streams`: the stream layer, php's `main/streams`, returns into `php_engine`, whose ceiling then measures the layer beside the engine, and what only the layer needs widens the native face every extension sees instead of the sealed layer face (#1959). Delete `api`: the 19 extension crates depend on nine engine crates again. Delete `php_unit`: the unit format reappears in the compiler's output type, `api`'s declaration types and the disassembler's input, with the opcode enum generated twice from one file. Delete `php_proto`: nothing reappears elsewhere; it exists so that protocol tests run without building the engine, and it is the one crate a second engineer may fold back into `php_sapi` without an ADR. Delete `php_ext`: every extension depends on `php_engine` and `php_macros` directly and the "api only" rule returns as a `use`-path scan over 105k lines. Delete `php_macros`: every native writes its declaration twice, once as data and once as the argument reads in its body, which is the four ways the port grew while the crate was a pass-through, six of them parsing php's stub text at startup. Delete `php_bundle`: the enabled list and the tier's name reappear in the binary, in `xtask crosscheck`, in `xtask phpt` and in the embed host. Delete `state`: every extension keeps a `thread_local!`. Delete `engine`: ini-dependent tables become crate statics wrong for the second `Engine` in a process. Delete `Cache`: pcre writes its own `RwLock<HashMap>` and principle 12 cannot be a lint. Delete `Tier`: either `php_engine` checks a `cfg(feature = "jit")` word in three places and depends on `php_jit`, or the check is a `dyn` call per back-edge. Delete `php_jit`: the engine builds, runs every phpt and passes G1's denominator; that is the month-one state. Delete `opgen --check` from revision 1: nothing reappears, because handler bodies are ordinary generic Rust that fail to compile if the spec disagrees (§5.8), so it is gone.

### 4.7 `php_unit`: one declaration model

What it holds: the declaration model (names as declared: resolved, without a leading `\`, case as written, owned bytes interned with the unit's literals at publication, §5.2; a name looked up is borrowed and stripped once at its boundary (#2711); declared types, of which a return type carries a `tentative` bit; parameters with by-ref, variadic and default flags; doc comment; declaration site; attributes with unevaluated arguments; a class's `not_serializable` flag, php's `ZEND_ACC_NOT_SERIALIZABLE`, which `serialize` and `unserialize` read; a property's `virtual` bit, php's `ZEND_ACC_VIRTUAL` without a hook, which `ReflectionProperty::isVirtual` reads and Reflection renders), op arrays and their tables, the constant-expression tree, the opcode enum, and `dump()`. The dependency rules leave the format no other home (ADR-0001).

How declarations are produced. **User code:** `php_compile` emits them as part of a `Unit`. **Natives:** `#[php_function]` and `#[php_class]` emit a builder function, not a `static`, because names are interned and a Rust `static` cannot call the interner. The builder runs once at module startup against the engine's interner and the result is frozen; it constructs values and parses nothing. A parameter default is the constant expression php's stub writes, kept unevaluated in the declaration as a user function's is; when a call leaves the argument absent, the entry evaluates it with the engine's evaluator in the declaring class's scope, a plain value inlined by the macro and anything else cached per request, since an enum case is a request object. A property default and a constant are declared by value, as php declares them. The native entry function itself is not part of the declaration; the `Extension` value pairs each declaration with its entry (§7.1).

How declarations are consumed: the linker in `php_engine` orders classes by their `extends` and `implements` references, allocates identities into the linked tables, and returns an error for a cycle or a missing parent. `php_heap` knows only the handful of intrinsic identities it needs for destruction and GC (stdClass, Closure, Generator, the Throwable family).

**The tentative bit.** A field of the return type in the declaration model, beside nullability, as in `zend_arg_info`. The engine verifies the native's own return against the type as it does any return type; the linker treats an override that drops the type or narrows it incompatibly as a deprecation rather than an error (§6.2); Reflection reports the type through `getTentativeReturnType()` and not `getReturnType()`. Only natives set it: `php_compile` never emits it, PHP has no syntax for it, and `gen_stub.php` reads a doc-comment tag. The bit is invalid on a function, on a `final` method, and on a method with no return type; the macro refuses all three at compile time, the third as a `const` assertion over the return type's `IntoReturn::UNDECLARED`, and `xtask crosscheck` links and renders every extension, so an invalid declaration cannot reach a release. The macro spells it as `#[php(tentative)]` on the method inside a `#[php_class]` impl block, a key the class macro reads, not a fourth attribute: a Rust `-> bool` cannot say "bool, and overriding without it is a deprecation", and a wrapper type for it would put a linker fact into the marshalling vocabulary. `dump()` prints it in php's Reflection wording, "Tentative return" against "Return".

**The safepoints.** `OpArray.safepoints: Box<[OplineIdx]>`, the entry, the loop headers and the returns of the op array, immutable, produced by the compiler at the same time as the line tables: the entry is the opline after the `RECV*` prologue and is number zero, a loop header is a backward jump target, a return is a `RETURN*`. The compiler numbers headers, not back-edges, so a `foreach` with a `continue` has two backward jumps carrying one header's number and php's one counter per header is kept. For the opcodes that are sites, a field php leaves unused for that opcode carries the safepoint's number: `result` on the backward jumps and the `RETURN*` family, per `ops.toml` (§5.7). `Unit::dump` prints operands by the opcode's kinds and an unused field is not printed, so `compile-identity` (§6.1) compares the field as zero and identity is unchanged. This is ADR-0001's rule applied: the fact belongs below both the compiler that produces it and the engine that allocates a word for it.

The constant-expression tree is Zend's `zend_ast` for constant expressions: literals, of which an integer keeps its radix (`Decimal`, `Octal` for `0777`, `OctalExplicit` for `0o644`, an exhaustive enum, since a native default renders as its stub spells it and `mkdir`'s `= 0777` is not `= 511`), named constants, class constants, enum cases, operators, array literals, `new`, and, for 8.5, closures and first-class callables by an index into the owning unit's function table. `php_engine` evaluates it with the same kernels the interpreter uses; Reflection reads the unevaluated form.

### 4.8 `php_proto`

The FastCGI record codec (`M/crates/mahout_sapi_fcgi/src/fcgi.rs`, 335 lines, verbatim), the dev server's HTTP/1.x framing with its caps, chunked scan, percent-decoding, path normalisation, mime and status tables and RFC-1123 dates (≈ 2.9k lines of `server.rs`, verbatim), and CGI environment parsing into plain fields. It produces bytes and plain structs; `php_sapi` turns those into a request description. The dev server serves on one thread, in php's event loop (`sapi/cli/src/server/mod.rs`, after `php_cli_server_do_event_loop`, `Z/sapi/cli/php_cli_server.c:2770-2797`): a wait of at most a second on the listening socket and every open connection, each request read as its bytes arrive and served through the engine one after another, so a request that sleeps holds the next one back, as under the oracle (#1884, as #1779 ruled); `PHP_CLI_SERVER_WORKERS` adds processes, forked before the server serves, and never threads (#1885).

**The production server's head is hyper's** (#2337, #2569, #2570). `sapi/makala` takes the HTTP/1.1 head as hyper parses it and writes the response head as hyper encodes it. A bare LF as a line end, and `Transfer-Encoding` beside `Content-Length`, are accepted as RFC 9112 §2.2 and §6.1 permit, the connection closed after the response in the second case; response header names go out lowercase, the one form HTTP/2 and HTTP/3 allow, where php's own SAPIs pass php's case through. Both hold because makala is the edge: nothing in front of it frames a request a second way. A deployment that puts a front end before makala reopens the strict head parser #2569 declined.

**The production server names itself and refuses what hyper refuses** (#2335, #2609, #2611, #2638). `sapi/makala` answers `php_sapi_name()` with `makala`, and its `phpinfo()` renders the engine's build, as each of php's SAPIs names itself and renders the binary it is. A request-target holding `<`, `>`, `"` or a byte of 0x80 and above unencoded, which RFC 3986 allows in none, is refused 400 before PHP runs, as hyper refuses it, where nginx passes the bytes on to php-fpm. The php-fpm comparison (#2349) and the served phpt lane (#2348) accept each as a by-design row of kind `makala`.

### 4.9 `php_syntax`: the tree

**Typed nodes, built once** (ADR-0003). The generator emits one struct per node kind in `php.ungram` with named child fields, and the tree builder resolves those fields from the parser's event stream at build time. An accessor is a field read. Required markers (`!` only where construction anchors on the child) become non-`Option` fields; everything else is `Option`. This is `zend_ast`'s positional-children shape, which is what a compiler after `zend_compile.c` walks. Nodes live in one arena owned by `Parsed`, addressed by index, in safe Rust; the crate stays `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`.

**No parent links.** The compiler passes scope downward. Mahout's upward walks are 103: 91 in `validate/`, 9 inside the red cursors that implement them and 3 in reparse. Validate is gone (§6.1) and so are the other two files, so every one of the 103 is in a file this copy drops, which is what the argument turns on. A construct that seems to need its parent is a construct whose compiler scope is missing a fact, and that is where it is fixed.

**Trivia stays in the token stream.** The tree references significant tokens only. The token stream is lossless (whitespace, comments, `HEREDOC_INDENT`, `HALT_DATA`), and it is the interface `token_get_all`, `highlight_string` and `__COMPILER_HALT_OFFSET__` consume; the builder records which comment block is a declaration's doc comment as a token index on the declaration node.

**The tree carries every fact `zend_ast` hands `zend_compile.c`**, and the compiler's walk, `emit` in `php_compile`, reads it and re-derives none (#599). Three instances. The doc comment is the token index the builder records above. A node's line is php's `zend_ast.lineno`, one more field the generator emits beside `range` and the doc comment, recorded by the builder on every node as `zend_ast_create_1..5` set it at the pin, where `ZEND_AST_SPEC` is 1: its first non-NULL child's line in php's child order, a token child's being the line it starts on and a node child's its own, and the scanner's line at the reduction for a node with none, which is the token the grammar's state had last read, the last shifted or the lookahead after it, as #601 measured it kind by kind (`zend_ast_create_from_va_list`'s fold of the smallest line over the children is the `#else` branch and is not compiled; #601, the amendment on #599). A literal token's value is what php's scanner makes of it (`zend_scan_escape_string` from the `"…"` rule, `ZEND_STRTOL` with its overflow retry from the `LNUM` rule) before the parser's zval node holds it; `php_syntax` answers it on demand from the token, by the one function the lexer diagnoses with, as `TokenValue { Int(i64), Float(f64), Bytes(Vec<u8>) }`, an int that overflows yielding `Float` as `ZEND_STRTOL`'s retry does, and `php_compile` converts a `TokenValue` to its constant at the one site that reads it and carries no escape table and no radix table. What `zend_compile.c` does with a fact once it holds it stays the compiler's: its resets of `CG(zend_lineno)` (`zend_compile_assign` to the target's line, if-arms, closure bindings, blocks) are `emit`'s. A compiler that reads a token's text a second time against the table the lexer walked to diagnose it is a second parser in miniature (§3, principle 2), and that is the line.

**No interning.** Mahout's `NodeCache` served reparse and salsa; neither consumer exists. Mahout's parse-throughput figure (≥ 25 MB/s) is a regression bar in `xtask bench` so the change is measured rather than assumed.

**What survives unchanged.** The lexer's mode machine, heredoc dedent into trivia, structured interpolation runs, and `__halt_compiler` handling; the event-driven parser and its 37-method driver; the grammar; the 61 hand-pinned `expecting` clauses, the 74 hand-placed error sites in `grammar/` they sit among, the 79 `probed 8.5.x` oracle citations beside them, and `php_token_name`; the dump format (`KIND@start..end "text"`), so 253 of the 549 inherited snapshot tests survive with their trivia lines removed, the 296 of `fixtures_validation.rs` going to `php_compile` with the validator (§6.1); the parser's own recursion cap. The dump renders every fixture, so it lands with the arena and each grammar commit regenerates the fixture files it brings, once each: no snapshot is regenerated twice, and the 253 do not pass through one commit.

### 4.10 Extension crates

**Layout.** `ext/<name>/Cargo.toml`, `src/lib.rs` exporting `extension()`, `src/ffi/` only where a `-sys` crate is bound, `tests/` for `Capture`-driven integration tests. Witnesses are in rustdoc as everywhere else. Stubs are not copied and are read by nothing: `xtask crosscheck` holds the crate's declarations to the oracle's `php --re <name>` rendering (§7.1). The six stub readers the port grew while `php_macros` was a pass-through are deleted crate by crate, each in the commit that moves its crate onto the attributes (revision 34).

**Cross-extension edges.** An extension may depend on another when its php-src counterpart includes that extension's headers: the pdo drivers on `pdo`, `standard` on `random` for `shuffle`, `array_rand`, `str_shuffle` and `uniqid`. What an extension exports to one that depends on it is exactly what php-src declares `PHPAPI` (pdo spells it `PDO_API`) in a header the dependent extension includes, with the types those declarations take, and nothing more. php-src has drawn that line already: each such surface exists because php's dependent extension calls into the other one rather than carrying a second copy, and without it the dependent grows a second port whose behaviour drifts, the shared var hash in session serialisation, the pattern cache in `RegexIterator`, `PHP_MD5_CTX`'s serialised layout in `HashContext`. Each surface is one row of the list below, naming the extension that exports it and every extension that links it, beside the php header it mirrors. The row holds the edge and a lock holds the items: `api-check` fails a library dependency of one `ext/*` crate on another (a dev-dependency links nothing) that no row names with both crates, and writes each extension's `pub` items beyond `extension()` to `ext/<name>/surface.lock`, regenerated and diffed as `api.lock` is and landed in the same diff as the code. The Items and Mirrors cells are a person's prose about what the surface carries and which php declarations it stands for; the lock is the one list of its items. A new edge is a new row, or a new name in one's Surface cell, in the commit that makes the edge.

|---|---|---|

**No shared helper crate under `ext/`.** A function two extensions need is engine policy, and enters `api` (the `open_basedir` check, path absolutisation, the filesystem-access guard Mahout's `check_fs_access` performed at 27 sites), or belongs to the extension that owns it in php-src, which exports it. A `php_ext_common` is the grab-bag this rule exists to prevent.

**Process facts are not an export.** What the engine exposes of the process, its user and groups (`api::process`, §4.4, revision 41), is the native face's and no surface of the list above: an extension that reads them calls the facet, binds none of them itself and takes none from another extension, however many extensions ask.

**State.** An extension declares its state on its `Extension` value and reaches it through the request (§4.4). Request state is one type per extension, built at request start and dropped at request end, in startup order and its reverse. Engine state is one type per extension, built as the engine is built and immutable thereafter. Anything an extension wants to keep between requests and change is a `Cache` inside its engine state, and the engine bounds it. Both state types are private to their crate. No `thread_local!`, no `static` holding a lock, no `Mutex` anywhere under `ext/`; the generated `ext/clippy.toml` and `api-check` deny them. **Persistent resources are lent, not held.** php keeps a persistent stream or database handle in `EG(persistent_list)`, one list per process under php-fpm and one per thread under ZTS (`Z/Zend/zend.c:800-804`), so no two requests use one entry at once. Here they are entries of one pool per `Engine`, `api::Pool<K, V>`, keyed as php keys them (`pfsockopen__host:port`, `stream_socket_client__host`, pdo's `PDO:DBH:DSN=…`). A request that asks for a key takes a live entry out, checked as php checks it (`PHP_STREAM_OPTION_CHECK_LIVENESS`, a driver's `check_liveness`), or opens one; it holds the entry for the rest of the request, where a second ask by that key answers the same resource; and it gives the entry back at request end unless the script closed it (`fclose`'s `PHP_STREAM_FREE_CLOSE_PERSISTENT`), which drops it. `V: Send + 'static` holds no request memory: the descriptor, the transport's and the crypto layer's state, a driver's connection. The resource, its buffers and its context are made fresh at each checkout, as `forget_persistent_resource_id_numbers` (`Z/main/streams/streams.c:68-101`) forgets them. The lock, the liveness check and the bound are the engine's.

**Order.** The bundle lists extensions in php's registration order for the oracle's enabled set, and the engine sorts them as `zend_sort_modules` does over each extension's declared dependencies; the linker orders classes from their declarations, so the order matters for lifecycle phases and the four listings. A witness holds the listings to the oracle; `api-check` verifies that each extension's feature switches on the features of what it requires.

**Features.** Enable and disable live on `php_bundle` only, one feature per non-default extension, default features matching php-src's default `./configure` set as far as ported. An extension crate has no enable feature of its own; it may have a feature for a library choice.

**The `libxml` surface.** `ext/libxml` exports to `ext/dom`, and to `ext/simplexml`, `ext/xsl`, `ext/soap`, `ext/xml`, `ext/xmlreader` and `ext/xmlwriter`, what `php_libxml.h` declares `PHPAPI`, with the libxml2 calls those extensions make over the declarations that header includes, each as a safe type whose `unsafe` stays in `ext/libxml/src/ffi/` (#731, #732): `ffi::Node`, `ffi::Ns`, `ffi::Kind`, `ffi::HashTable`, `ffi::DtdTable` and `ffi::DocString`, the tree of `tree.h` and `entities.h` read and changed one call at a time, with `ffi::Name` and `ffi::Utf8`, the name checks and UTF-8 counts of `xmlValidateName`, `xmlSplitQName2` and `xmlUTF8Strlen`; `ffi::Parser`, `ffi::Source`, `ffi::Parsed`, `ffi::Save`, `ffi::Validate`, `ffi::Schema`, `ffi::Validated` and `ffi::C14nSelect`, `ffi::C14nTarget`, `ffi::C14nOut`, parsing, serialising, validating and canonicalising as `parser.h`, `HTMLparser.h`, `xmlsave.h`, `valid.h`, `xmlschemas.h`, `relaxng.h`, `xinclude.h` and `c14n.h` do them, with `ffi::Fragment`, the push parse of `dom_xml_fragment_parsing_algorithm`, and `ffi::Dump`, the save context and output buffer `php_new_dom_dump_node_to_str_ex` writes the modern classes' own XML serialisation through, and the `XML_PARSE_*`, `HTML_PARSE_*`, `XML_SAVE_*` and attribute and entity type constants their callers pass; `ffi::XPath`, `ffi::Object`, `ffi::Answer`, `ffi::Item`, `ffi::Operand`, `ffi::Called`, `ffi::Returned` and `ffi::Handler`, `xpath.h` with php's `php:function` and registered-function callbacks handed to a closure, and `ffi::Scope`, the declarations one evaluation registers, the list `php_dom_get_in_scope_ns` makes for the modern classes among them; `ffi::NodeObject`, `ffi::SharedDoc`, `ffi::DocProps`, `ffi::ClassType` and `ffi::Borrowed`, the node object and its document reference (`php_libxml_node_object`, `php_libxml_node_ptr`, `php_libxml_ref_obj`, `libxml_doc_props`, `php_libxml_class_type`) with the increments and decrements `php_libxml_increment_node_ptr`, `php_libxml_decrement_node_ptr`, `php_libxml_increment_doc_ref`, `php_libxml_decrement_doc_ref` and `php_libxml_node_decrement_resource` name, and `php_libxml_node_free_list`, `php_libxml_node_free_resource`, `php_libxml_set_old_ns` and `php_libxml_invalidate_node_list_cache_from_doc` behind `Node`; `ffi::PrivateData` and `ffi::Quirks`, `php_libxml_ref_obj`'s `private_data` header, dropped before the document is freed and told through its `ns_hook` when an element whose namespace carries `LIBXML_NS_TAG_HOOK` is freed, and its `quirks_mode`, which dom's modern classes keep their namespace mapper, template contents and HTML5 compatibility in; `ffi::Raised`, `ffi::Route` and `ffi::Level`, one libxml error and how php's handler met it, captured around one call by setting php's structured handler on the calling thread, so no request sees another's; `ffi::Library`, libxml's initialisation and its version; `Export`, `ExportNode`, `register_export`, `import_node` and `ffi::Exported`, the table `php_libxml_register_export` fills at startup (here with `ExtensionBuilder::register`, so it is immutable once the engine is built) and `php_libxml_import_node` reads by the root of an object's class, answering the node and the document the object counts, which `dom_import_simplexml` reads through the object's handler offset in php; `DocumentHandlers` and `DefaultHandlers`, `php_libxml_document_handlers` and `php_libxml_default_document_handlers`, the serialiser a document carries (`NodeObject::set_document_handlers`, dom's modern documents their own, as `dom_set_xml_class` sets it) that every extension saving one of its nodes calls; for `ext/simplexml`, `NodeObject::hold_node`, `php_libxml_increment_node_ptr(obj, node, NULL)`, a node counted and no object named as its own ("only dom uses `_private`"), `Parser::read`, `xmlReadMemory` and `xmlReadFile` over a parser php never configures, `Save::node_file`, `php_libxml_dump_node_to_file`, `XPath::bare`, a bare `xmlXPathNewContext`, and `Node::new_child`, `new_text_child`, `new_ns_prop`, `encode_entities` and `copy_doc`, `xmlNewChild`, `xmlNewTextChild`, `xmlNewNsProp`, `xmlEncodeEntitiesReentrant` and `xmlCopyDoc`; `Errors`, the request's error list and buffer, `php_libxml_uses_internal_errors`, `php_libxml_issue_error`, `php_libxml_pretend_ctx_error_ex` and the routing `php_libxml_ctx_error`, `php_libxml_ctx_warning` and `php_libxml_error_handler` do; `sniff_charset_from_stream`, `php_libxml_sniff_charset_from_stream`, the charset of the last `Content-Type` among the http headers a stream carries as its `wrapper_data`, which `Dom\HTMLDocument::createFromFile` decodes by as `html_document.c` does (#1202); and `NOEMPTYTAG`, `LIBXML_SAVE_NOEMPTYTAG`; for `ext/soap` (#1562), `Parser::soap`, `soap_xmlParse_ex` over `xmlCreateMemoryParserCtxt` or `xmlCreateFileParserCtxt`, blanks and comments dropped, errors silenced and the entity loader disabled for the parse, `Library::build_uri`, `xmlBuildURI`, `Library::from_utf8` and `Library::to_utf8`, `xmlCharEncOutFunc` and `xmlCharEncInFunc` over the handler `xmlFindCharEncodingHandler` finds for a name, and `switch_stream_ctx`, `php_libxml_switch_context`, which `get_sdl` puts a client's stream options in place with around a WSDL fetch, and `Node::new_text_noenc` and `Node::is_text_noenc`, `xmlNewTextLen` with its name set to `xmlStringTextNoenc` and the test of that name by address, the text an `<anyXML>` value is written as by `to_xml_any`. For `ext/xsl` (#1564), `Node::as_ptr` and `Node::from_ptr`, the `xmlNodePtr` libxslt reads and answers, `XPath::lend` with `Function` and `XPath::PHP_STRING`, `PHP_OBJECT` and `CUSTOM`, the functions `xsl_ext_function_string_php`, `xsl_ext_function_object_php` and `xsl_ext_function_trampoline` serve on the transformation's own `XPath` context, `Raised::capture_request`, the loads of a stylesheet's compile, `Raised::generic`, `php_libxml_ctx_error(NULL, ...)` from libxslt's generic handler, `Library::stylesheet_defaults`, `PHP_LIBXML_SANITIZE_GLOBALS` with `xmlSubstituteEntitiesDefault(1)` and `xmlLoadExtDtdDefaultValue` around `xsltParseStylesheetDoc`, and `NodeObjectOf`, `Export::with_node_object` and `node_object`, `Z_LIBXML_NODE_P` of an object of any XML extension through its row of the export table. Every item of the surface is a line of `ext/libxml/surface.lock` (#2251), and `ext/dom`, `ext/simplexml` and `ext/soap` are `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`.

**The `lexbor` surface.** `ext/lexbor` exports to `ext/dom` and `ext/uri` the lexbor calls their php counterparts make; to `ext/dom`, the lexbor calls php's dom makes over the lexbor headers it includes (`php_lexbor.h` declares nothing `PHPAPI`; `html_document.c`, `html5_parser.c`, `inner_outer_html_mixin.c` and `parentnode/css_selectors.c` include `lexbor/html`, `lexbor/encoding` and `lexbor/css` themselves), each as a safe type whose `unsafe` stays in `ext/lexbor/src/ffi/` (#731, #732): `ffi::html::Document`, `lxb_html_document_t` parsed chunk by chunk (`lxb_html_document_parse_chunk_begin`, `_chunk`, `_end`), with `ffi::html::TokenizerError`, `ffi::html::TokenizerErrorId`, `ffi::html::TreeError` and `ffi::html::TreeErrorId`, the tokenizer's and the tree builder's parse errors as `lexbor_libxml2_bridge_report_errors` reads them, and `ffi::html::Observations` and `ffi::html::Quirks`, what `lexbor_libxml2_bridge_copy_observations` copies (php's patches 0001 and 0002); `ffi::html::FragmentParser` and `ffi::html::Fragment`, the fragment parse of `dom_html_fragment_parsing_algorithm` (`lxb_html_document_parse_fragment_chunk_begin`, `_chunk`, `_end` under an element made from a tag name and a namespace URI); `ffi::html::Node`, `ffi::html::NodeKind`, `ffi::html::Namespace`, `ffi::html::Attributes` and `ffi::html::Attr`, the tree the bridge walks (`lxb_dom_node_t`'s links and line, `lxb_dom_element_qualified_name`, `lxb_html_tree_node_is(node, LXB_TAG_TEMPLATE)` and the template's content, `lxb_dom_attr_qualified_name`, `lxb_dom_attr_value`, character data, `lxb_dom_document_type_name`, `_public_id` and `_system_id`), each handle borrowing the document it names; `ffi::encoding::Encoding`, `ffi::encoding::Decoder`, `ffi::encoding::Encoder`, `ffi::encoding::Status`, `Encoder::utf_8_single`, `Encoding::prescan` and the constants `REPLACEMENT_BYTES`, `REPLACEMENT_CODEPOINT`, `MAX_CODEPOINT`, `DECODE_ERROR` and `DECODE_CONTINUE`, the encodings `html_document.c` drives (`lxb_encoding_data_by_name`, `_by_pre_name`, `lxb_encoding_data`, `lxb_encoding_decode_*` and `lxb_encoding_encode_*` with their buffers, `lxb_encoding_decode_utf_8_single`, `lxb_encoding_encode_utf_8_single`, and the `<meta>` pre-scan `lxb_html_encoding_determine`); and `ffi::css::List::parse` with `ffi::css::Refused`, `lxb_css_selectors_parse` and the first log message php shows, the parsed `lxb_css_selector_list_t` copied into an owned tree the matcher `selectors-adapted/selectors.c` becomes in `ext/dom` reads: `ffi::css::List`, `ffi::css::Complex`, `ffi::css::Selector`, `ffi::css::Combinator`, `ffi::css::Kind`, `ffi::css::AttributeMatch`, `ffi::css::Match`, `ffi::css::Modifier`, `ffi::css::Anb`, `ffi::css::PseudoClass`, `ffi::css::PseudoClassFunction` and `ffi::css::PseudoElement`, lexbor's `lxb_css_selector_*` types and ids by the same names. To ext/uri it exports, the same way, the calls `uri_parser_whatwg.c` makes over `lexbor/url` (#1209): `ffi::url::Parser`, the request's pool, parser and `lxb_unicode_idna_t`, which php keeps per request, laid at `RINIT` and destroyed at `post_deactivate` (#2925); `ffi::url::Url`, one `lxb_url_t` in that pool, parsed against an optional base (`Parser::parse`, `lxb_url_parse`, answering `ffi::url::Parsed`), copied (`Parser::clone_url`, `lxb_url_clone`) and given back (`Parser::free`, `lxb_url_destroy`); read (`scheme`, `username`, `password`, `host` as `ffi::url::Host`, `port`, `path`, `query`, `fragment`, the fields of `lxb_url_t`); changed (`Parser::set_protocol`, `set_username`, `set_password`, `set_hostname`, `set_port`, `set_pathname`, `set_search` and `set_hash`, the `lxb_url_api_*_set` calls, a refusal `ffi::url::Refused`); and serialised (`Url::serialize`, `Parser::serialize_idna` and `Parser::host_unicode`, `lxb_url_serialize`, `lxb_url_serialize_idna` and `lxb_url_serialize_host_unicode` over the request's `lxb_unicode_idna_t`, and `ffi::url::ipv4_text` and `ffi::url::ipv6_text`, `lxb_url_serialize_host_ipv4` and `_ipv6`); with `ffi::url::ValidationError` and `ffi::url::ErrorKind`, the parser's `lexbor_plog_t` in the order php's `fill_errors` pops it and `lxb_url_error_type_t` by the same names. lexbor allocates through the request's heap, as php's `PHP_MINIT_FUNCTION(lexbor)` points it at `emalloc` (§5.14's lend, #2925): every call above that can allocate, resize or free takes `ffi::Lent`, which only `ffi::lent` makes, from a request; a value that holds lexbor's memory past one call is an `ffi::Owned`, given back under a lend, `ffi::kept` across the work between its calls; and `ffi::array::ArrayObj` is the `lexbor_array_obj_t` `html5_parser.c` keeps its bridge's work list in. Nothing else of `ext/lexbor` is `pub`, and no C pointer crosses: a node handle cannot outlive its document, a selector list is Rust values, and a URL is its request's pool's.

**Tests.** An extension's tests build `Engine::builder().extensions(&[php_ext_<name>::extension(), ...its dependencies])` with the `Capture` sink (ADR-0002) and never dev-depend on `php_bundle`. An engine with no extensions serves a script that uses Zend alone; this is a witness in `php_engine`. Witnesses in one extension that call another's functions make that crate a dev-dependency.

---

## 5. Execution model

This section is the design of `php_heap` and `php_engine`. Where a structure is named after its Zend counterpart the intent is a faithful port of the algorithm, with Rust's types enforcing the invariants Zend documents in comments.

### 5.1 Values

```
Zval          16 bytes   #[repr(C)]
  value       u64        i64 | f64 | *Refcounted | (u32 lval, u32 extra)
  type        u8         IS_UNDEF=0 NULL TRUE FALSE LONG DOUBLE STRING ARRAY OBJECT RESOURCE REFERENCE
  type_flags  u8         IS_TYPE_REFCOUNTED | IS_TYPE_COLLECTABLE
  _pad        u16
  u2          u32        opline-specific: next opline, cache slot, fe pos, constant flags
```

Zend's type numbering is kept so `IS_UNDEF` is the all-zero value, `IS_TRUE`/`IS_FALSE` are separate tags, and refcounted-ness is a flag test rather than a type-range check. NaN-boxing is not attempted: PHP integers are 64-bit, a 51-bit payload spills every large integer, which is what Mahout measured (ADR-0019/0022: dense 0.904, spill 1.594, +77% wall). Zend's zval is 16 bytes and reaches the target numbers.

```
Refcounted    8 bytes    { rc: Cell<u32>, info: Cell<u32> }
  info        type u8 | flags (NOT_COLLECTABLE, PROTECTED, IMMUTABLE, PERSISTENT) | gc colour + root-buffer index
```

`IMMUTABLE` values skip refcount traffic entirely. Interned strings, constant arrays in op arrays and immutable linked classes carry it. This is what makes sharing across threads sound: no shared value is ever refcounted. Constant arrays stay immutable where `php -n` refcounts them per request: the model is php's under opcache, where they are immutable too. The observables that tell the two apart, a GC root or a refcount over a copied literal, are the divergence row `trace:immutable-literal-arrays`, and a witness that pins one names `[regime.immutable-literals]` (revision 34).

**Ownership, decided.** `Zval` is `Copy` and has no `Drop`. Copying a zval is a bitwise copy of 16 bytes and does not touch a refcount, exactly as `ZVAL_COPY_VALUE` does; `heap.addref(z)` and `heap.release(z, hooks)` are explicit. Consequences: no drop glue can run a destructor (R46 cannot occur); a function that never receives `&mut dyn HeapHooks` provably never runs user code; frame slots are plain cells and handlers never hold two `&mut` into one frame. The cost is discipline against leaks, and it is paid by the instrument, not by callers: the native ABI owns arguments and return values, so a native releases only what it creates and drops; a debug-build leak gate runs at the end of every request in tests; the allocator frees everything at request end regardless.

**The ownership instruments, decided** (revision 87). The freed-use guard stands at the refcounted header's increment and decrement, the one chokepoint every refcount operation reaches, and a refused operation is reported at the next sensing point and at request end, never dropped. A debug build quarantines each bin's last sixteen freed slots, first in first out, checking each slot's mark and first word as it leaves, and drains the quarantine before the limit is consulted. The request leak gate is php's debug shutdown: the shutdown GC before object storage is freed, request state dropped before the heap, every request-lifetime block freed by name, and then an empty heap, or the request ends in an `InternalError` naming the extension that allocated what is left; it does not run after a fatal.

**Owners and views, decided** (revision 93). A native gives up exactly the owners it took and did not hand on. The `api::values` types are views wherever they are used, and one owner type, neither `Copy` nor `Clone`, must-use and dereferencing to the view it owns, is what constructors return and sinks take; one conversion takes an owner from a view, and only the engine may name an owner it did not take. In a debug build the owner type's `Drop` releases nothing and reports the site that made an owner dropped unconsumed for an owner of a counted value in a chunk. An owner of what no count owns leaks nothing and is recorded nowhere, and an owner of a huge block is named by the request's leak sensor at the request's end instead. A release build gives the owner type no `Drop`, so no drop glue runs, as this section decides. The handler layer over raw `Zval` keeps its own discipline.

### 5.2 Strings

`{ Refcounted, hash: Cell<u64>, len: usize, bytes: [u8] }` in one allocation. Bytes, not UTF-8. Hash cached lazily (`hash == 0` means unset, Zend's convention with the top bit forced). `IS_STR_INTERNED`, `IS_STR_PERSISTENT`, `IS_STR_VALID_UTF8` flags. Interning: a process-wide frozen table of known strings (every builtin function, class, method, property, constant and ini name, plus Zend's `known_strings`) built once at startup; compile-time literals interned into the persistent table when a unit enters the shared cache. A unit compiled for one request alone, an `eval` or an uncached include, owns its constant strings as plain strings of the request's, one each time its compile makes one, the engine's table answering first once the request holds it; its main op array's go when that op array's execution ends and the rest when the unit goes, as opcache's `accel_new_interned_string_for_php` leaves them to `destroy_op_array`, and such a unit's backed enum case holds the string value its compile made (revision 150). The request's own table holds the names the engine's declarations, which keep them as bytes, are read by, a request-only unit's constant arrays' strings, and the constants of a program a host hands over as its source, which stands where php reads a file its cache interns (#2985). Numeric-string classification (`is_numeric_string_ex`) ported exactly, including the leading/trailing whitespace rules of 8.0+.
