| Extension | Month-1 source | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Core, standard, spl, reflection, json, ctype, tokenizer, filter, session, random, hash | Rust: port Mahout's bodies, complete against the oracle's `php --re <name>`; `random` engines ported from C exactly (seeded sequences are observable); `hash` via RustCrypto | php writes these; so do we |
| pcre | the libpcre2-8 the pinned keg links, JIT on, found through `pkg-config` and linked statically, never vendored, as `libxml2` and `libsqlite3` are (revisions 32, 69); `pcre2-sys` for its declarations alone (#624, #717); php's compiled-pattern cache as a `Cache` in engine state (4,096 entries) | Behaviour *is* PCRE2's; Mahout's `regex`+`fancy-regex` reached 62% and R121 |
| date | vendored `timelib` (`php-src/ext/date/lib`, MIT) with its bundled tz database, compiled and declared by the workspace crate `timelib_sys` and wrapped in `ext/date/src/ffi/` (revision 109) | `strtotime`/`DateTime::modify` parsing is only reproducible by timelib; Mahout's `tz-rs` reached 29% |
| mbstring | libmbfl vendored byte for byte from `php-src/ext/mbstring/libmbfl` at the tag `oracle.toml` pins, with its `LICENSE`, compiled by `ext/mbstring`'s `build.rs` over a C shim of the Zend names it includes (`zend_string`, `emalloc`, `ZEND_ASSERT` and kin, allocating with `malloc`) and bound in `src/ffi/`; `mbstring.c`, `php_unicode.c` and `mb_gpc.c` ported to Rust; the platform's Oniguruma through `pkg-config` for `mb_ereg*`, as the oracle links it; the crate's `license` is `MIT AND LGPL-2.1-only` (revision 47, #1173) | 79 encodings and php's conversion, error and kana rules are libmbfl's; Mahout's pure-Rust mbstring had 4 and reached 26% (R66) |
| libxml, dom, xml, simplexml, xmlreader, xmlwriter | the platform's `libxml2`, found through `pkg-config libxml-2.0` and linked, never vendored; bound once, in `ext/libxml/src/ffi/`, which the others reach through `ext/libxml` (§4.10); libxml's generic error channel, whose callback is C-variadic, is defined in Rust with `c_variadic` (stable from Rust 1.99) and routed to php's handler as `xmlSetGenericErrorFunc(NULL, php_libxml_error_handler)` routes it (revision 34) | php's DOM is libxml's DOM, and the oracle links the platform's library too |
| `Dom\HTMLDocument` (8.4), `Uri\WhatWg\Url` (8.5) | vendored `lexbor` (Apache-2.0), php-src's copy, compiled by `ext/lexbor`'s `build.rs` and bound in its `src/ffi/`; `ext/lexbor` is a crate of its own, as php's `lexbor` is a module of its own | php bundles lexbor for exactly these |
| uri (8.5, `Uri\Rfc3986`) | `uriparser` (BSD) | php bundles uriparser |
| zlib | a pure-Rust deflate first, held byte for byte to the oracle's zlib at every level; `libz-sys` over the platform's zlib where it cannot match (revision 34) | php's tests observe the compressed bytes, which follow the linked zlib's algorithm; the oracle links the platform's |
| sqlite3, pdo_sqlite | the libsqlite3 the pinned keg links, found through `pkg-config` and linked statically, never vendored, as `libxml2` is (revision 32); `libsqlite3-sys` for its declarations alone (#624, #1510) | the oracle's SQLite and its compile options, not a crate's bundled release |
| openssl | `openssl-sys` over the platform's OpenSSL 3, found through `pkg-config` and never vendored (the CI Linux image carries `libssl-dev`), behind the `openssl` feature, which the `php` binary turns on and the bundle's default set does not (revision 97) | TLS and cipher parity |
| curl | `curl-sys` | |
| sodium | the libsodium the pinned keg links, through `pkg-config`, bound in `ext/sodium/src/ffi/`, never vendored (a `-sys` crate only in its pkg-config mode) (#1540) | the oracle's library, its error edges libsodium's |
| intl | the ICU4C the pinned keg links (icu4c@78), through `pkg-config`, never vendored; php's intl C ported, and its C++ over ICU's C++ classes (`Calendar`, `BreakIterator`, `TimeZone`, `MessageFormat`, php's own `CodePointBreakIterator`) reached through an `extern "C"` C++ shim of the crate's own, compiled by its `build.rs` and bound in `ext/intl/src/ffi/` (#1573) | the oracle's ICU and its data; `icu4x` is not API-identical to what `ext/intl` exposes and carries its own CLDR snapshot |
| gd | the libgd the pinned keg links, through `pkg-config gdlib`, with the image libraries it links, bound in `ext/gd/src/ffi/`, never vendored (#1573) | gd's output bytes are libgd's; php-src's bundled libgd is not what the oracle links |
| ffi | the platform's libffi, as the oracle links it, bound by hand in `ext/ffi/src/ffi/`; `ffi.c` and its C-declaration parser ported (#1573) | the oracle's call ABI |
| gmp | the libgmp the pinned keg links, through `pkg-config`, bound in `ext/gmp/src/ffi/`, never vendored (#1540) | `gmp-mpfr-sys` builds its own GMP, a version the oracle does not run |
| tidy, gettext | the libtidy (tidy-html5) the pinned keg links, through `pkg-config`, and the libintl it links, found as php's `config.m4` finds it since the keg ships no `pkg-config` file, each bound in its crate's `src/ffi/`, never vendored (#1540, #1603); libintl's domain table (`textdomain`, `bindtextdomain`) is the C library's, process-wide as under php's ZTS build and not reset between requests, as php does not reset it, so only a host serving requests concurrently on threads shares it (#1604) | tidy's repair output and gettext's lookups are the libraries' own |
| xsl | the platform's libxslt and libexslt, as the oracle links them, beside `ext/libxml`'s libxml2, whose surface it takes for the document as dom does (#1540) | the oracle's XSLT, as libxml2 is (revision 32) |
| readline | the platform's libedit, as the oracle links it, with the CLI's interactive shell (`php -a`) where php puts it, `readline_cli.c` (#1540); libedit's callbacks carry no user data, so `ext/readline/src/ffi/` keeps the request lent to one call and php's two `strdup` copies in per-thread slots of a `pthread_key_t`, as php's `ZEND_TLS` globals are per thread, the key made once through `pthread_once` (#1606) | the oracle's line editor |
| soap | php's own C ported over `ext/libxml`'s surface, no library of its own, as simplexml (#1540) | |
| bcmath | Rust port of php's own C | php writes it |
| posix, pcntl | `nix`, with `libc` in `src/ffi/` for the calls `nix` lacks (`times`, `getlogin`, `ctermid`, `getpriority`/`setpriority`); each its own crate behind its feature, in every host whose serving process is single-threaded, as the oracle's build carries them (the CLI, CGI and php-fpm's children), and in no host that serves requests on threads, where a `fork()` keeps only the calling thread (revisions 35, 66) | `nix` wraps `fork`, `sigaction`, the credential calls and the user database over libc, which owns that process state; `rustix` declines them for that reason |
| sockets | `nix`, with `libc` in `src/ffi/` for what `nix` lacks, as posix and pcntl; behind its feature, as php builds it with `--enable-sockets` (revision 36) | one binding story for the three socket and process extensions |
| phar | a port of php-src's own C (`phar.c`, `phar_object.c`, `util.c`, `tar.c`, `zip.c`, the stream and dir wrappers), its compression over `ext/zlib`, its signatures over `ext/hash` and `ext/openssl` (revision 36) | php writes it; the `phar://` wrapper and the archive formats are observable byte for byte |
| iconv | `libc` | thin |
| bz2 | a pure-Rust bzip2 first, held byte for byte to the oracle's libbz2 at every block size and work factor, the platform's `libbz2` where none matches, as zlib (revision 39); behind its feature, as php builds it `--with-bz2` | phar's compression and `compress.bzip2://` are observable byte for byte |
| zip | a pure-Rust zip crate, `ZipArchive` and the `zip://` wrapper ported over it, and libzip's observable behaviour re-derived where php's tests read it; deflate through `libz-sys` over the platform's zlib, driven as `zip_algorithm_deflate.c` drives it (memLevel 9), in `ext/zip/src/ffi/`, the one binding the crate has (revision 113, narrowing revision 70); behind its feature | a pure-Rust binding where the surface is narrow enough to re-derive, as zlib and bz2 are held |
| dba | `dba.c` with the flatfile, inifile and cdb handlers ported from php's own C; ndbm over the platform's `<ndbm.h>`, the C library's on macOS as the oracle links it, bound in `ext/dba/src/ffi/` and never vendored; on a platform whose C library has none, the handler is not compiled, as php's configure leaves it out (#1512) | the oracle's ndbm, as `libxml2` and `libsqlite3` are the platform's (revisions 32, 69) |
| mysqlnd, mysqli, pdo_mysql | `mysql_common` with its default features off, for the wire protocol's packets and scrambles; mysqlnd ported over it in `ext/mysqlnd`, every read and write through the engine's streams, and mysqli and pdo_mysql over mysqlnd's exported driver (§4.10), each behind its feature (revision 61, #1345) | php's mysqli and pdo_mysql run only on mysqlnd; `mysql_common` is the one maintained crate without I/O, and nothing observable moves to it: the statistics, the character sets and escaping, result typing, the state machine and its errors, prepared statements, authentication and LOCAL INFILE are mysqlnd's, ported |
| fileinfo, exif, zip, pdo_pgsql, gd, soap, ldap, imap, ffi | month two (§11) | ordered by Packagist `ext-*` requirement frequency |

A `-sys`-backed extension implements the same `Extension` seam as a pure-Rust one; its `ObjectHandlers` impls (DOM nodes, `DateTime`) are where the C object lifetimes are tied to the handle table. The `-sys` glue lives in `ext/<name>/src/ffi/`, one of the homes the `unsafe.homes` rule names, with the same SAFETY discipline as `php_heap`. A library vendored for one extension is compiled and declared by a crate of the workspace's own where the manifest lays one down, as `timelib_sys` is for `date`, itself a home that rule names; the extension's `src/ffi/` keeps the safe wrappers over it (revision 109). An extension that reaches a C library another extension binds, as `dom` reaches the `libxml2` that `libxml` links, does so through the safe types the binding extension exports (§4.10) and keeps no `src/ffi/` of its own, so the library is declared, linked and held to the SAFETY discipline once.

---

## 8. Correctness instrument

There is one executor, so the instrument stands outside it. Each module's interface is its test surface, and where the interface has an oracle in the php binary, the oracle is used. The instruments are `xtask` subcommands that write readings; the harness (`harness-design.md`) says how they are wired.

| Module | Interface | Test adapter | Oracle | Cadence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `php_syntax` | `parse` | none needed | php token differential and verdict agreement (M0, inherited); parse throughput ≥ 25 MB/s in `bench` | per commit |
| `php_heap` | value API + `HeapHooks` + `VmStack` | `Recorder` hooks; Miri; libfuzzer on allocator, array, string; nested `execute` from a native, a bailout inside it, a fiber switch between two nestings for the entry slot | `memory_get_usage()` deltas vs php through the engine | per commit |
| `php_compile` | `compile`, `dump` | text diff; the safepoint invariant asserted on every op array | **php's pre-optimiser op-array dump** | per commit on 6.5k files, nightly on 172k |
| `php_engine::ops` | kernel functions | **witnesses in rustdoc**: a kernel or native without one fails CI | php's bytes | per commit |
| `php_engine` linker | linked tables | `xtask phpt --subset tentative`: the 31 phpts that exercise the tentative bit | phpt | per commit |
| `Tier` in `php_engine` | `execute::<T>`, `EngineBuilder::tier` | `Probe` asserting `(site, number)` sequences on witness scripts: `(Entry, 0)` once per call and never when a `RECV` throws, one `n` for two back-edges to one header, no `Return` for the entry frame | none needed | per commit |
| `step` in `php_engine` | `step` | a witness single-stepped to completion produces the output of `execute::<Nop>`; one witness per `Stepped` arm; a fused compare-and-branch as one step; `Done` with and without a pending exception | `Capture` output | per commit |
| the word and the interrupt in `php_engine` | `Func::word`, `Func::published`, `OpArray.safepoints`, `Limits::interrupt` | every backward jump and return in a witness carries the number of a word; the entry is zero; an `eval` op array's words are sealed; `set_time_limit(1); for (;;) {}` and a `pcntl` signal delivered into a loop | `compile-identity` unchanged; php's fatal text | per commit |
| `php_engine` (host face) | `Engine::builder`/`serve` | **`Capture` sink, in-process**: a fresh `Engine` per test gives the isolation a process gave; a panic is caught at the boundary and reported as a failure | phpt expectations | `Zend/tests` + `tests/` + `sapi/cli` per commit (4-minute budget); full 21.5k nightly |
| `api::Cache` | one method | the unit cache and pcre; two requests on two threads against one `Engine` asserting one build per key; Miri's data-race detection on a `std::thread` test | none | per commit |
| `ext/<name>` | `extension()`, `ObjectHandlers` | `Capture` with that crate and its dependencies linked plus every `.phpt` under `php-src/ext/<name>` through the in-process adapter; `crosscheck` against the oracle's `php --re <name>` rendering, tentative-return row included | phpt, `--re` | per commit for default extensions, nightly for the rest |
| `php_bundle` | the feature list and the order | `api-check` on the order against the crate graph | none | per commit |
| `php_sapi` | hosts, worker | proxy lanes (nginx and Caddy → FastCGI), process-mode phpt for `sapi/cli` | php-fpm behaviour | nightly |
| the phpt runner itself | verdicts | upstream `run-tests.php -p <binary>` under the oracle php over the same corpus; a disagreement is a runner bug | the oracle's verdict table | nightly |
| all seams | lock files | `xtask api-check` | the committed lock | per commit |
| the compiled tier | `Jit` | `jit-design.md` §7: `force` and `exit-fuzz` on every witness per commit and on `Zend/tests` and `tests/` nightly; G1 per commit from the day `php_engine::tier` exists | the oracle with `opcache.jit=tracing` and `off` | per commit; run of record for the flip |

**Witnesses in rustdoc.** Every kernel function and every native carries at least one block. A native, for `witness.coverage`, is a `fn` under `#[php_function]` or a method with a body in a `#[php_class]` impl, at any indentation and visibility; a body-less member and an `implementation =` alias are not subjects. A function carries its own witness; a method carries its own or is covered by its class's, and a class's witness covers exactly the methods its fences call (`->name(`, `::name(`), so every method with a body is called by at least one fence. The block is a `php` fence followed by an `expect` fence, with two attributes on the `expect` fence and one optional third fence:

```rust
/// ```php
/// set_time_limit(1); for (;;) {} ?>never reached
/// ```
/// ```expect exit=255 wall=1s
/// Fatal error: Maximum execution time of 1 second exceeded in witness.php on line 2
/// ```
```

`xtask witness` extracts them, runs the pinned oracle and the engine, and compares stdout, stderr and exit code byte for byte through `oracle::compare`. `exit=N` is a pin; absent means zero is asserted; bare `exit` is the opt-in that fails the run until `--accept` rewrites the fence line with the oracle's number, so the status is a function of snippet and oracle, captured and never authored. `wall=D` is disclosure and gates nothing; `xtask witness` sums the declared durations into a budget line. A `stderr` fence, when present, is the pinned second stream; absent means empty is asserted. The `=` on the `expect` fence line trips `rustdoc::invalid_codeblock_attributes`, which `-D warnings` makes fatal, so every crate that carries witnesses allows that lint at its root; `xtask witness` is the fence's parser and rustdoc is not, and the doctests still run. Output lines are bytes; `%s`, `%d`, `%f` and `%a` are the EXPECTF wildcards, translated by the code the phpt runner owns. A witness id is a content hash of the snippet. The snippet runs as `witness.php` with `<?php` on line 1 and the code on line 2, so php's own error text matches; the oracle module owns that recipe. A nightly job re-runs the oracle to detect php patch drift. Coverage rule: a kernel or native without a witness fails CI. This is Dax's instrument kept, without Dax's language, and it is the thing that sees convergence on a wrong answer because the arbiter is not in the tree.

**Oracle.** One module in `xtask`, pinned by `oracle.toml` (version, digest, invocation recipe; the only statement of the pin), exposes "run this program / this SKIPIF / this `-r` snippet under the harness regime" and returns normalised output, and `compare(id, expected, actual) -> Verdict`, which consults the divergence registry. It owns `-n`, the `BASELINE_INI` list (upstream's `$ini_overwrites`, 38 at the pinned tag), the env, the timeout, the scratch-prefix stripping and the trim set, all read from `oracle.toml`, which also names the JIT regimes the tier's lanes run the oracle under. Witness extraction, phpt skip detection, compile identity, the trace-dump diff, battery capture and differential fuzzing all call it. The `--re` rendering the cross-check holds to is the same pinned binary's, under the same recipe.

**phpt.** The `ScriptExec` seam stays with two adapters: the fresh-process adapter (crash isolation) and the in-process adapter, which is the `Capture` sink under `Engine::serve` on a thread. Outcomes are run-tests.php's set; `--EXTENSIONS--` missing means `Skip` with upstream's reason. The scheduler honours `--CONFLICTS--` (98 corpus files). `--EXPECTF--` is matched by an RE2-semantics engine, since `expectf_to_regex` writes nothing past one but `%f`'s lookahead; `--EXPECTREGEX--` is the test's own PCRE and is matched by the pinned php's `preg_match`, as run-tests.php matches it (#1543). Both predecessors kept phpt out of CI; with a fast engine it fits in four minutes per commit.

**Differential fuzzing.** The inherited grammar generator produces programs, both engines run them under the oracle recipe, any byte difference is a minimised failing case proposed as an `open` registry row. Nightly, plus libfuzzer targets for lexer, parser, string ops, array ops, allocator, `unserialize`, `sprintf`, `preg` on every push. The battery generators (covering arrays over semantic axes: references and copy-on-write, destructor timing, evaluation order, error ladder) are engine-independent and are taken.

**Unsafe core.** Miri on `php_heap`'s test suite per commit; `cargo careful` on the full test suite nightly; `unsafe-audit` asserts the homes the `unsafe.homes` rule names.

**Divergence registry.** One file, `docs/divergence.toml`, owned by the oracle module, each row a witness hash, a phpt path, a compile-identity path, an extension's name or a trace site, with a kind (the lane that reads it: `witness`, `phpt`, `runner`, `compile`, `crosscheck`, `trace`), a cause (whether it is parity debt: `debt` by default, or `php-ub`, `by-design` or `oracle-build`, #2460), a reason a person wrote, a status and a date. Mahout's has 197 entries; the target on day 30 counts the rows whose cause is `debt`, all extension-shaped, and its number is the manifest's `[divergence] target`. The runner control's known disagreements, the compiler's deliberate differences and the tier's two recorded differences are rows in it, not tables of their own.

---

## 9. Performance and memory

### 9.1 Where the predecessors' time went

Mahout's own decomposition of its 49.88× kernel deficit is `17.5 × 2.10 × 1.355`: Tier 0 versus php's interpreter, php's JIT, and a residual. The 17.5× is the term month one removes, and it is not one thing:

| Cost in Mahout's Tier 0 | This design |
|---|---|
| Kernels shared with a second executor return `*Step` sums; the executor re-dispatches on them | Kernels are called by one loop and act directly |
| 8-byte instruction word, `u16 opcode + three u16 slots`, decoded per op | 24-byte opline with typed operands; no decode |
| Operand type branch per op | Operand-kind specialised handlers |
| Summary work-word check per op | Checks only after `throw`-effect ops, at back-edges and at activation |
| Always-on profiling cells and entry/back-edge counters | None in the interpreter; the tier's word is read only in the `Jit` instantiation |
| Property access is a hash lookup in a `PhpArray` | Inline slot through a run-time cache: one compare, one load |
| IC keyed generically | Per-opline cache slot, monomorphic fast path |
| Destructor queue checked and drained at op boundaries | Synchronous at rc→0 |
| Per-request compile: 64.4 ms | Unit cache: 0 ms on hit; single-pass compile on miss |
| Arrays as `Vec<Option<(Key,Value)>>` + `HashMap` index | `zend_hash` layout; packed arrays are 16 bytes per element |

The 2.10× is the tier's term and is month two (§9.3).

### 9.2 Targets on day 30

**The seven kernels** are Mahout's `fib`, `nbody`, `mandelbrot`, `spectralnorm`, `binary_trees`, `fannkuch` and `string_munge` (`~/Projects/mahout/benchmarks/src/`), committed as `docs/perf/workloads/<name>.php`, each at the size where the pinned php interpreter, opcache on and JIT off under the oracle's recipe, takes 0.5–1 s on the darwin-arm64 box, so that a bar over them measures steady state and not warm-up. Where a kernel's one parameter steps past 0.5–1 s, it runs at the smallest size above 0.5 s. `fib` runs at 36, `nbody` at 700000, `mandelbrot` at 1100, `spectralnorm` at 700, `binary_trees` at 16, `fannkuch` at 10, whose one step multiplies its time by about nine, and `string_munge` at 600000, the largest the recipe's `memory_limit` admits. G1, G2, the cost of `step` under recording and the trace-dump diff run on them (`jit-design.md` §4.1, §7).

Measured under Mahout's protocol (paired reps, A/A control, load recorded), against the oracle with opcache enabled and JIT off, on the seven kernels:

| Metric | Bar |
|---|---|
| Compute geomean, engine ÷ php interpreter | ≤ 1.5× (stretch 1.0×) |
| Process start to first byte, `-r 'echo 1;'` | ≤ php |
| Per-request compile on cache hit, Laravel skeleton | 0 |
| Peak RSS on `binary_trees` / `fannkuch` | ≤ 1.2× php |
| `memory_get_usage()` delta on array and string micro-tests | within 5% of php |
| Worker throughput, FastCGI, 8 threads, one process, hello-world and Laravel skeleton | ≥ php-fpm with 8 workers |
| Parse throughput, top-1000 corpus | ≥ 25 MB/s |
| `Cache` hit cost under thread-per-request, pcre workload | less than the compile it saves, per commit from the day pcre lands |
| **G1, door cost**: an engine built with the `jit` feature, the tier bound and `opcache.jit_buffer_size=0`, against one built without the feature, seven kernels and the Laravel skeleton | within the A/A drift bound (Mahout used 1.813%); above 2% the seam costs too much and `jit-design.md` §3 is revisited. Per commit from the day `php_engine::tier` exists, on the kernels from day 2 and on the Laravel skeleton from the day the engine runs it |

Why 1.5× is credible and 1.0× is possible: the interpreter executes the same algorithms as Zend under the same optimising compiler infrastructure, with PGO and whole-program thin LTO already measured at −33% on this toolchain, and without any of the per-op terms above. The `HeapHooks` parameter and the `Copy` zval add no per-op cost; the `MapPtr` indirection is one load through a per-request table, which is Zend's cost too; the tentative bit and the word have no run-time reader in the interpreter. "Insane" on day 30 means: php's interpreter speed on compute, faster start, zero recompilation, and one process with N threads where php needs N processes. Against php with its JIT the gap on pure compute is php's remaining 2.1×, which is month two.

### 9.3 The compiled tier, and what month one builds for it

Month one builds: the `Tier` seam with `Nop` and `Probe`, `step` and `Stepped`, activation raising `Site::Entry`, one zero `Word` per safepoint in `Published` and sealed words for request-scoped op arrays, `OpArray.safepoints` and the `safepoint` column with the compiler's assertion, the `VmStack` entry slot, the interrupt flag on `Request` with its three interpreter checks, `tier.lock`, and G1 per commit.

Month two builds `php_jit` as `jit-design.md` designs it: a tracing tier after `zend_jit_trace.c`, recording by stepping the interpreter with recording exclusive per request, php's guard set and thresholds, Cranelift behind a backend value with Stogov's IR as the named fallback, the interrupt test at every compiled loop header, compensation code at exits for register-resident scalars only and no on-stack replacement entry, deoptimisation as a jump to an opline of the identical frame, traces as engine-owned immutable values in a dual-mapped arena, published under one lock and visible by a release store, freed with the epoch's table, which keeps every unit they inlined (`jit-design.md` §4.5), and one compile worker per engine. What it must clear is `research/jit-research.r1.md` §E.11's G2 to G7: compute geomean ≥ 2.0× over the interpreter and ≤ 1.1× of php's JIT on the same programs; RSS ≤ +10% and code ≤ 2× php's; zero divergence under `force` and `exit-fuzz`; FastCGI throughput ≥ php's JIT; recording overhead reported. G6 and G7 run with `jit.compile` in both settings and the run of record names which. Memory per engine for the words: 8 bytes per safepoint in `Published` plus 4 bytes per safepoint in the unit's table, shared by every request; G4 sees both in RSS. The tier infers nothing from a declared return type, so php's rule that a tentative type is trusted only through an exactly resolved callee has no counterpart yet; a later revision that adds signature-based return inference copies the rule or is wrong on every polymorphic call through a tentative parent.

The backend decision is recorded as an ADR on the day the default flips or is refused, with the G1 to G7 readings of the run of record, in the form Mahout's stage 307 and Dax's ADR-0007 used. Month three's list, in order and each behind its own gate: profile-guided region growth, PHP-to-PHP inlining beyond the recorded depth, refcount inference on the trace, and the IR fallback only if G3 fails on codegen rather than lowering. An LLVM tier is not on it: `research/llvm-research.r1.md` finds every dynamic-language JIT that put LLVM on the hot-compile path measured it and left, and recommends at most a bounded spike over hot regions, gated by its G8 against the Cranelift tier as the baseline, after G3 has a reading, and no crate, thread, word bit or budget line before that. A region is a table entry with the same entry ABI, so the word's layout needs no tier bit (`jit-design.md` §4.5).

### 9.4 Memory

Parity layouts (§5.1–5.4) are the memory plan. Additionally: interned literals and names are shared process-wide once; immutable linked classes are shared; op arrays are shared; run-time caches are per request and sized exactly; tombstones compact on rehash; strings are one allocation; packed arrays hold bare zvals; the allocator releases whole chunks at request end and caches a small number for reuse, as php does. The budget is the RSS bar in §9.2. Under `jit`, the arena is bounded by `opcache.jit_buffer_size` under php's name.

---

## 10. Rust standards and process

- **Toolchain**: latest stable, pinned in `rust-toolchain.toml`, edition 2024, resolver 3. MSRV is the pinned stable.
- **Lints**: `clippy::pedantic` warn with a short, reasoned allow list; `-D warnings` in CI; `unwrap_used`, `expect_used`, `panic`, `unreachable`, `todo`, `unimplemented` **denied** in every crate a request runs through, as `lints.no-panic`'s scope names them (`xtask` and `php_macros` exempt, tests too). Internal invariant failures are `InternalError` fatals with a message, not panics. `disallowed-types` and `disallowed-methods` live in a generated `clippy.toml` at each directory a rule's scope names, the ones `generated.committed` lists, which clippy finds by nearest-file search; the lists are derived from `seams.toml`.
- **`unsafe`**: the homes the `unsafe.homes` rule names (§4.1); every block one operation with a `SAFETY` comment (`undocumented_unsafe_blocks`, `multiple_unsafe_ops_per_block` deny); Miri per commit; documented invariants at the top of each unsafe module.
- **Types over comments**: newtypes for `Slot`, `LiteralIdx`, `CacheSlot`, `Handle`, `ClassId`, `MapPtr<T>`, `SafepointIdx`, `OplineIdx`; `Zval` never exposes its union outside `php_heap`; kernels take `&mut Request` and typed handles, never raw pointers; state machines (`Generator`, `Fiber`, `LazyObject`) are enums. No `Drop` on engine value types; RAII for Rust-side resources only.
- **Visibility discipline**: `pub` across a crate boundary only through a locked module; `php_heap::hot` is `pub` but excluded from `api` and audited. Inside a crate, modules are internal seams and may be as fine-grained as the tests need.
- **Generated code**: only in `build.rs` (`ops.toml` → enum and dispatch; `php.ungram` → typed nodes); never committed, except the generated `clippy.toml` files, which are regenerated and diffed.
- **Profiles**: dev `opt-level = 0` with line-table debug info, packed split debug info and no incremental cache, for disk; release `opt-level = 3`, `lto = "thin"`, `codegen-units = 1`, `panic = "unwind"` (required by `catch_unwind` and fibers); `release-da`, release with debug assertions and overflow checks, without thin LTO or one codegen unit, the build every correctness lane runs (harness-design.md §7); PGO lane in `xtask bench`.
- **Budgets**: per-crate and total line ceilings, interface item counts (§4.5), lock-package count, binary size, clean release build on the dev machine, `docs/` lines and the harness's own hand-written lines; every number is `seams.toml`'s and every CI gate is fed from it.
- **Witness accept**: `xtask witness --accept` is the only writer of `exit=` values.
- **Documentation**: this file; `jit-design.md`; `harness-design.md`; `ops.toml` as the instruction-set reference; rustdoc with witnesses. No tickets, plans or maps in the repository; the phpt burndown and the divergence registry are the status.
- **Living documents.** Each design of record is one file at its current state with a ledger on top; a revision edits the sections it changes and adds one ledger entry with what changed, why, and the check that would show it wrong; git is the dated record; the `rN` files are records and are not edited. A revision that alters a rule the harness enforces changes `seams.toml` in the same commit, and §14 lists what the manifest, the registry, the instruments or the locks gain or lose.
- **ADRs** live in `docs/adr/`, numbered from 0001, written only when a decision in this file is reversed or when a choice would otherwise be "fixed" by a future reader; each ends with the sentence that reader needs. Six exist at this revision. Manifest rules that an ADR decided carry its number, so it prints at the failure. The tier owes one more, the backend's, due at the flip (§9.3).
- **CI on every commit**: fmt, clippy, unit tests, Miri (`php_heap`), witnesses, `Zend/tests` + `tests/` + `sapi/cli`, compile identity on the per-commit corpus, crosscheck for default extensions, fuzz smoke, budgets, unsafe audit, api-check, vocab, G1. Nightly: full corpus, 172k compile identity, `run-tests.php` verdict control, differential fuzz, `cargo careful`, oracle drift, bench under protocol, `force` and `exit-fuzz` over `Zend/tests` and `tests/`.

---

## 11. Thirty days

The same modality as the predecessors is assumed: one lead, agents working in parallel along crate seams from day 3, lanes as modules with locked interfaces. Each week has an exit bar measured by the instruments, not by feel. If a bar is missed, the cut order in §12 applies before the next week begins.

| Days | Work | Exit bar |
|---|---|---|
| **1–2** | Repository, crate skeletons, budgets gate, CI, `seams.toml` and the harness's `permit`, `done`, `status`. Inherit `php_syntax` (generator rewritten to emit typed nodes), `php_macros` (laid down as a pass-through; its expansion is revision 34's), phpt runner, fuzz generator, corpus lock. `oracle.toml` and the oracle module with its recipe. **All eight seams compile as traits and types**: `heap.lock`, `unit.lock`, `api.lock` v0 (drafted from the census before day 1; `xtask port-map` regenerates the join), `sapi.lock`, `ops.lock` (full instruction set with effects and the `safepoint` column), `tier.lock`. `php_engine::tier` with `Tier`, `Nop`, `Site`, `Flow`, `execute::<T>`; the `jit` feature as a name on `php_bundle` that enables nothing yet. `php_unit` types; `php_proto` skeleton with the FastCGI codec's tests green; `php_ext` and `php_bundle` skeletons with the `ext/*` directories and empty `extension()` bodies, so the graph and `api-check`'s manifest rules exist before any body is ported. `Cache` with the unit cache. `build.rs` produces the dispatch table over empty bodies. `compile-identity` running against the oracle with a compiler that emits nothing (all red). Witness runner working against the oracle alone. `Capture` sink. The `run-tests.php` lane wired against the oracle binary itself, so its verdict table is the control from day 1. | CI green on an engine that runs nothing; every instrument writes a reading; `port-map` shows the census's rows (651 at v0), all unresolved; G1 has its denominator from the first engine that runs a witness. |
| **3–7** | Lane A `php_heap` complete: allocator, header, string, array (packed + hash), object, reference, resource, handle table, VM stack with the entry slot and its guard, `Recorder`-hooked Miri suite. Lane B `php_compile` to green on `tests/basic` and `tests/lang` **by op-array identity, before execution**, the dissolved validate's guard list ordered by the corpus, `OpArray.safepoints` and the `safepoint` column with the assertion; `php_engine` spine for procedural PHP: literals, variables, operators with php coercion, strings and interpolation, arrays and `foreach`, functions with activation raising `Site::Entry`, closures with `use`, references, `include`/`require`, `echo`/`print`, output buffering core, `var_dump`/`print_r`/`var_export`, warnings and `error_reporting`; `step` and `Stepped`; the interrupt flag with its three checks; `Word` per safepoint in `Published` and sealed words for request-scoped op arrays; the `Probe` adapter with the first loop witness. Lane C kernels: `zend_sort`, `php_gcvt`, numeric strings, comparison, with witnesses. Lane D extension port begins against `api.lock` with `Capture`, in bundle order, `random` (the first `request_state` user) and `standard` first, one crate at a time. Lane E hosts against the request description and sink. | `compile-identity` ≥ 95% on `tests/`; `tests/lang` ≥ 90%, `tests/basic` 100%, `Zend/tests` ≥ 60%; `port-map` unresolved ≤ 150; G1 within the drift bound on the kernels. |
| **8–14** | Classes: inheritance linker with the three-way compatibility outcome and `#[\ReturnTypeWillChange]`, interfaces, abstract, traits (8.5 binding order), static, constants (typed, `final`), enums, readonly, property hooks, asymmetric visibility (instance and static), promoted ctor props incl. `final`, magic methods, `__destruct` synchronous, `instanceof`, `new` without parens, `clone` and `clone()` with properties, lazy objects; `ObjectHandlers` with `Std` complete. Exceptions with `finally`, multi-catch, `throw` expression. Generators. Types: scalar/union/intersection/DNF, `strict_types`, return types, `never`, coercion tables. `match`, named args, spread, first-class callables, pipe, `(void)`, `#[\NoDiscard]`, attributes with reflection core, `#[\Override]`, `#[\Deprecated]`, `#[\DelayedTargetValidation]`, `goto`, heredoc/nowdoc, `list` in all forms, namespaces and `use`, `declare`, `eval`, `$GLOBALS`/`global`/`static`, `__halt_compiler`. Deprecations table for 8.5. | `compile-identity` ≥ 99% on `Zend/tests`; `Zend/tests` ≥ 95%, `tests/classes` 100%; the `tentative` subset green; the 50-script probe battery ≥ 45/50; `port-map` unresolved ≤ 50. |
| **15–21** | GC port through `HeapHooks` with `gc_status`; fibers, with the two-fibers-two-entry-slots test; `WeakMap`/`WeakReference` as handler adapters; shutdown ordering; error and exception handlers incl. `get_*_handler`; ini system (all Core directives, php.ini discovery, `-d`); streams seam with `file`/`php`/`data`/`glob`/user adapters; CLI SAPI with lazy stdin and all flags; unit cache, map pointers, `opcache_*`; `Engine`/`Request` split and `catch_unwind` boundary; `max_execution_time` and the timer. Extensions ported: standard, spl (the first `#[tentative]` user), reflection with the two tentative accessors, json, ctype, tokenizer, random, hash, filter, session; bindings: pcre2 (the first `engine_state` and `Cache` user), timelib, libmbfl, sqlite. | `compile-identity` 100% on the per-commit corpus; `Zend/tests` ≥ 99.5%, `tests/` 100%, `sapi/cli` ≥ 90%, `ext/standard` ≥ 85%, `ext/pcre` ≥ 95%, `ext/date` ≥ 80%; compute geomean ≤ 2.5×; RSS ≤ 1.5×; `port-map` unresolved: the rows of the crates row 22–30 ports, and no others. |
| **22–30** | Bindings: libxml family, lexbor, uriparser, zlib, openssl, curl, sodium, gmp, bcmath port, posix/pcntl/sockets/iconv, intl. FastCGI SAPI and the worker; `memory_limit`; `-S` dev server. Perf pass: operand-specialisation list from an executed-op census on the corpus, PGO, hot-kernel review under the protocol; G1 on the Laravel skeleton from the day it runs. Full-corpus burndown and divergence registry. This file updated to as-built. | Day-30 parity as defined in §1; compute geomean ≤ 1.5×; RSS ≤ 1.2×; worker throughput ≥ php-fpm; `port-map` unresolved 0; all locks and budgets green. |
| **31** | The tier's prototypes, before any trace is recorded: the per-call cost of `catch_unwind` in the kernel wrappers on the non-unwinding path; the per-opline cost of `step` under recording on the seven kernels; the dual-mapped `mov eax, 7; ret` through two views on the aarch64 box with the cache maintenance and the reused-chunk `isb` question answered; the concurrent publish test on both targets; the interrupt test's cost on the kernels; the Cranelift crate set counted against the lock-package target. Month two's schedule is `jit-design.md` §11. | Each prototype has its reading: the per-call cost of `catch_unwind` on the non-unwinding path, the per-opline cost of `step` under recording and the interrupt test's cost are `bench` readings on the seven kernels (§9.2); the dual-mapped `mov eax, 7; ret` runs through both views on the aarch64 box, with the cache maintenance and the reused-chunk `isb` question answered; the concurrent publish test passes on both targets; the Cranelift crate set is counted, and it fits `[budget] lock_packages`. |

Lane D starts on day 3 against a locked `api`, not on day 15 against a finished engine; that is the single largest schedule decision, and it is possible only because the interface is fixed before the implementation exists. Lane B owns the compile-time fatals from day 3; Lane E writes against the request description and sink from day 3 and never touches engine policy.

---

## 12. Risks, cut order, and what parity will not include

**Cut order.** What parity counts is settled by the rulings §1 and *Not in parity* name, and nothing is cut by default; a cut is a new ruling, and it lands as a line under *Not in parity* with its row. Nothing in `php_heap`, `php_engine`, `php_compile`, GC, fibers, or the tier's month-one seam work is cuttable. If month two slips, the tier ships behind the feature at `opcache.jit=off` and nothing a user sees changes.

**Not in month one, by decision**: the compiled tier itself; on-disk unit cache; mysqli, pdo_mysql, pdo_pgsql; gd; soap, ldap, imap; ffi; zip, exif, fileinfo; Windows; ZTS-style per-thread ini isolation beyond what `Request` gives. These are month two in the order listed in §7.2.

**Not in parity, by decision**: php's optimizer (§6.1, revision 44). The 60 tests of `ext/opcache/tests` that pin a post-optimizer or DFA dump, and `optimize_static_002.phpt`, whose `getStaticVariables()` shows the reference php's DCE elides, are held by one accepted registry row.

**In parity again, by decision**: intl, gd and ffi, which #1166 ruled out, ruled back in on #1573 (revision 75) over the libraries the oracle links (§7.2). Until each crate lands, its tests stay in `phpt:ruled-out-extensions`, and each leaves that row in the commit that adds its crate.

**Not in parity, by decision**: the suites that need a server the recipe does not start, which `seams.toml`'s `[parity] out` lists under #1167. The oracle skips their server-bound tests under the recipe, so those are outside the verdict parity counts (§1), and the extensions are outside parity with their remaining tests, held by the same row, until a person rules one in. That extension then brings its service to every machine that takes a reading, beside the oracle job and the local lane, and its DSN to `oracle.toml`'s `[recipe.env]`.

| Risk | Likelihood | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| Schedule has zero slack | High | Inheritance list is fixed on day 1; cut order is published; weekly bars are measured, not negotiated. |
| A soundness bug in `php_heap` | Medium | Miri per commit, fuzz targets on allocator/array/string, ported invariants, one-op-per-unsafe-block. |
| `libmbfl` is LGPL-2.1, statically linked | Certain | Vendored as its own directory under its `LICENSE`; the crate's `license` field names it; LGPL §6(a) is met by shipping the engine's source; a binary-only release is a person's licence decision, and the same C then builds as a shared library for §6(b). No `encoding_rs` fallback: it would be a second implementation with different output (principle 1). |
| Synchronous destructors and native re-entry recurse on the Rust stack | Medium | Stack-limit checks with php's own `Error` message and threshold; fibers get their own stacks; the calling thread's bounds read at request start as `zend_call_stack_get` reads them, the limit what the thread has, the engine sizing no thread of its own (§5.6, §5.14); the CLI and makala serve on a stack sized for php's depth through a native's callback (§5.6, revision 155). |
| `become` never stabilises | Irrelevant | The shipped dispatch is a `match` loop; tail calls are a measured option, not a dependency. |
| Vendored C (timelib, libmbfl, lexbor, uriparser) complicates the build | Medium | `cc`-built `-sys` crates with pinned php-src tags; no system-library discovery for vendored ones. The platform libraries the default build links are `libxml2`, `libsqlite3` and `libpcre2-8`, the pinned keg's, found by `pkg-config` in `ext/libxml`'s, `ext/pdo_sqlite`'s and `ext/pcre`'s builds, the C library's ndbm for `ext/dba` where it has one, and each library an extension ruled in later binds, as the oracle links it (§7.2, #1540). |
| Agent-driven development regrows process weight | High | No tickets in the repo; ADRs only on reversal; budgets gate documentation and harness lines too. |
| Full-corpus parity slips because `ext/` is 15k of the 21.5k tests | Certain for some extensions | Parity is the oracle's own run (§1); what it excludes is named under *Not in parity* with its ruling, and the extensions lane orders the rest by tests per unit of effort (#1182). |
| `api.lock` v0 is wrong in shape and the port stalls on renames | Medium | `port-map` is the measure; the first 20 census items by call count are kept by name; renames are mechanical. |
| Op-array identity is too strict: php emits something we do not want to emit | Low | Every deliberate difference is a registry row of kind `compile`; the gate found one design error (§5.8) before any code existed. |
| Explicit `release` leaks values in native bodies | Medium | The ABI owns args and returns; the leak gate runs per request in every test; the allocator's request-end release bounds the damage to accounting. |
| `HeapHooks` threading makes some heap signatures awkward | Low | It is one parameter on the operations that can free; Zend has the same set of "may run user code" sites, in comments. |
| Locks become friction and get rubber-stamped | Medium | A lock diff is small and local; review is of the interface, which is the review that matters. |
| Dissolving validate loses probed edge cases in the move | Medium | The 136 annotations and the fixture file are the inventory; each guard lands with its fixture re-homed as compile output. |
| `php_unit`'s constant-expression tree grows into a second compiler | Low | It is `zend_ast`'s constant-expression subset by enumeration; evaluation is one kernel in `php_engine`. |
| Two build scripts reading one `ops.toml` drift | Low | One enum, generated once; a wrong arm is a compile error; `ops.lock` diffs the spec. |
| Typed-node construction under error recovery: a partial node whose required child is missing | Low | Mahout's `check_required_children` soak runs on the new builder before the marker set is trusted. |
| Cross-extension `pub` surfaces grow into a second api | Medium | `api-check` prints each `ext/*` crate's public items beyond `extension()`, held against §4.10's list of surfaces, each of which is what a php header the dependent includes declares `PHPAPI`; an item the list does not carry is a defect. |
| The two phpt runners disagree on many tests early and the lane is muted | Medium | The lane compares verdicts, not pass counts; known disagreements are registry rows of kind `runner`, target zero on day 30. |
| `Cache` hit cost under contention exceeds the compile it saves on a many-core worker | Low | §9.2's measurement per commit from the day pcre lands; the escape is sharding inside `Cache`, which no caller sees. |
| An extension needs mutable cross-request state that is not a cache of immutable values | Low in month one | The known case, persistent connections, is lent from the engine's pool (§4.10, revision 55); anything else is a `Cache` value or engine policy in `api`. |
| A native's return type is real where php's is tentative, or the reverse | Low | `xtask crosscheck` renders each bundled extension as `php --re <name>` does and holds the bytes to the oracle's, and the return row reads "Tentative return" or "Return"; the phpt is reported against the same oracle, which wins. |
| The word at the safepoint, or the mode load under `Off`, costs more than the A/A drift bound | Low | G1 per commit from day 2; the escape is copying the mode and thresholds into request state, and reading the word only in the `Jit` instantiation, which `Nop` already does by folding the branch. |
| A site opcode does not leave `result` unused, so the number has no field; a backward jump opcode is missing from the column | Low | `ops.toml` says which field per opcode and the generated dispatch reads it, so a wrong column is a build error; the compiler's assertion catches a missing opcode on the first op array that has one. |
| Raising `Entry` at activation diverges from where php's counter fires for a typed function, so trace roots differ from php's when a `RECV` throws | Certain, cited (§5.6) | Accepted as a `trace` row of the registry from day one; the `TRACE start` diff against php under `inline` on the seven kernels reports that site and must report nothing else. |
| An owned `UnitView` cannot hold engine-immutable classes by `Arc` | Low | The compile thread stays and `types::build` alone moves back to the recording thread; no interface here changes. |
| Linux `memfd_create` sealing and the two mappings do not behave as the JIT note says; aarch64 needs a per-thread `isb` on chunk reuse | Low to medium | Both are day-31 prototypes on both targets before the arena carries a trace. |
| The compile worker makes `jit_debug` output non-deterministic | Certain under `thread` | The oracle diff of `TRACE` lines runs under `inline`, which `force` implies. |
| A host pools threads and something leaks across requests on the thread | Low | Nothing may be thread-local (§5.14); the request leak gate runs after every request-state drop in tests and names the state type. |

## 13. Inheritance manifest

Exact paths to take, at Mahout `1b3104a8` (walked at `d6460ef0`; the research read `8f119938` for the LLVM note) and Dax `aff0fc5` unless noted. Everything else in both trees is left behind.

| Take | From | Into |
|---|---|---|
| `crates/mahout_lexer`, `mahout_parser` (grammar, driver, events, tree builder, tests), `mahout_source/{lib,diag,line_index}.rs`, `docs/specs/php.ungram`, and the generator `M/xtask/src/{codegen_ast,ungram,codegen}.rs` (3,175 lines, rewritten to emit typed nodes per §4.9; without `codegen.rs` there is no `SyntaxKind` emitter and the other two emit nothing that compiles) | Mahout | `php_syntax` |
| `mahout_source/{magic_method,builtin_attr}.rs`, which neither the lexer nor the grammar reads: every site is `validate/`'s, the linker's or the runtime's | Mahout | `php_compile`, with validate's guards |
| `mahout_native_macros` | Mahout | `php_macros`, rewritten (revision 34): the attribute shape and the receiver-makes-it-an-instance-method rule taken; its `sig = "…"` and `raw` strings not, and its expansion to `::php_ext::abi::` and `::php_ext::__private::` |
| The ABI traits of `mahout_native_abi` | Mahout | `php_engine::api`, as `FromArgument`/`IntoReturn`, every fact a type says an item of the trait (§7.1) |
| `mahout_phpt`'s section parser and `--EXPECTF--` translation; ~600 of the 2,447 lines of `M/crates/mahout_driver/src/bin/phpt_burndown.rs` (corpus walk, category map, `-j` fan-out, oracle-skip logic); `mahout_fuzz_gen` and its battery generators with the thirteen Mahout divergence ids stripped; `fuzz/fuzz_targets/*`; `conformance/php-src-tests.lock.json`; the `corpus/phpt` manifest | Mahout | `xtask`, `fuzz/` |
| The generic half of the `zend_sort` port (`M/crates/mahout_runtime/src/semantics/compare.rs:954-1363`: `php_stable_sort_by`, `zend_sort`, `zend_insert_sort` over a comparator, ~250 lines, verbatim; the live-array half rewritten as `Heap::array_sort`, which is `zend_hash_sort_ex`) | Mahout | `php_heap`, beside `compare` |
| The cast and coercion ladders of `semantics/coerce.rs` (`to_long`, `cast_int`/`cast_float`, `coerce_float_to_int`, `coerce_scalar`, `aggregate_number`, ~800 lines) and the 300-line `ty2_pins` test module, as re-derivations with witnesses | Mahout | `php_engine` kernels |
| The 85 php-exact message wordings of `messages.rs` and `ids.rs`, as data; `MsgId` not taken | Mahout | `php_engine` raise sites |
| `mahout_value/src/convert.rs:121-183`, the `php_gcvt` shape | Mahout | `php_heap` (`php_heap::numeric`, where `double_to_string` is written once, beside the numeric-string read) |
| `crates/mahout_ext_*` bodies (19 crates), adapted through `port-map`; ~3,500 lines of Zend builtins in `mahout_ext_standard` (`class_exists`, `get_object_vars`, `debug_backtrace`, `set_error_handler`, `define`, `gc_*`, `spl_autoload`, `func_get_args`) go to the engine; `base_promotion_pin.rs` is not taken | Mahout | `ext/<name>`, one crate each; `php_engine` for the builtins |
| `mahout_driver`'s feature list and its 19 `register()` calls | Mahout | `php_bundle`: the features and the ordered list |
| `M/crates/mahout_sapi_fcgi/src/fcgi.rs` (335 lines, verbatim) and the engine-free parts of `mahout_sapi_cli/src/server.rs` (≈ 2.9k lines) | Mahout | `php_proto` |
| `docs/perf/measurement-protocol.md`, the ~700-line measurement protocol of `xtask/src/bench.rs` (paired reps, A/A, status labels), `benchmarks/` corpus | Mahout | `xtask bench`, `docs/` |
| `.github/workflows/nightly.yml` dashboard job shape | Mahout | CI |
| `mahout_value/src/*` SAFETY comments and invariant list | Mahout | `php_heap` documentation only; the layouts are Zend's, the 131 extension class ids and the float-printing family do not come along |
| The extension-seam census (`research/data/r2-mahout-ext-seam-census.txt`: the commands, not the numbers) | this investigation | `xtask port-map`, run against `M/crates/mahout_ext_*` on day 2 to produce `api.lock` v0 |
| `oracle.toml` | Dax | root |
| The witness idea, its coverage rule, and `SPEC.md` ticket 095's two marker rules | Dax | `xtask witness`, §8; no code is taken |

Not taken: `mahout_runtime` as a unit; any IR; any JIT crate (`jit1`, `jit2`, `stencil`, `codemem`, `tier2_host`); `mahout_driver`, `mahout_gauge`; `mahout_gc` (re-ported from `zend_gc.c` for root-buffer placement); `mahout_alloc` (re-ported from `zend_alloc.c`); `mahout_syntax/src/{green,red,builder}.rs` (the green/red layer and `NodeCache`); `mahout_parser/src/{validate/,reparse.rs}` (validate's guards move as knowledge into `php_compile`); lexer checkpoints; `mahout_source/{codes,root,level}.rs`; `removed.rs` except its three parse errors; the generated `Visitor` and `check_required_children`; `parser.rs`'s thread-stack policy; `mahout_phpt/src/{wire,worker}.rs` and the adjudication arm; `mahout_sapi_cli/src/worker.rs`; `mahout_fuzz_gen/src/{isolation_stream.rs,generated/isolation_dial.rs}`; `xtask/src/bench.rs`'s vintage ledger and JSON codec; `messages_check`; `xtask/src/native_crosscheck.rs` (it holds nothing to the oracle; `xtask crosscheck` is re-authored as the `--re` rendering diff, ~400 lines); `mahout_native_abi`'s crate boundary; `NativeState`; `RequestCtx::ext_state_mut`'s trait and contract; all of Dax's `lang/`, `engine/joined`, `engine/host/substrate`, `emitters`, `battery`.

What earlier revisions said that is no longer true, so a reader of the records is not misled: `Func` with a `code: Option<NonNull>` slot and a reserved type-feedback word per cache slot (revision 1 §9.3, revision 2 §5.14) are gone, the word's trace id is the entry and feedback is the trace record (ADR-0006); month two builds a tracing tier, not a baseline method JIT; `xtask opgen --check` does not exist; `SMART_BRANCH` fusion is the handler's, not the compiler's; the instruction set has no "minus JIT-only opcodes"; `php_ext::enabled()` is `php_bundle::enabled()`; `heap.lock` never gained `FrameRef::is_entry`; `E` may not contain a lock has its three engine-owned exceptions; `unsafe` has the homes the `unsafe.homes` rule names, not two.

## 14. Harness changes in this revision

Rule 3 of `harness-design.md`: each revision of the design of record lists what the manifest, the registry, the instruments or the locks gain or lose, and the commit that lands it is the commit that changes them.

- **`seams.toml`** exists, written in this commit against every rule of §4.1, §4.2, §4.5 and §10; it gains an `ext/hash` row at 3000. The harness's example grammar was not TOML and lacked four predicates; `harness-design.md` ledger 5 records both.
- **`oracle.toml`** exists: 8.5.9 by digest, the 38-entry baseline, the environment, the timeout, the normalisation, and three JIT regimes; the php-src tree and corpus are at the same tag.
- **`ops.toml`** exists, extracted by `scripts/extract_ops.py`; `ops.lock` is generated from it on day 1. The `safepoint` column keeps `JMPZ`; the compiler's assertion excludes `FAST_RET`.
- **`crates/php_ext/api.lock`** v0 exists at 301 items with `data/port-map.v0.toml` as the join `xtask port-map` regenerates; `engine_limitation` is `gone` with no successor.
- **`docs/divergence.toml`** exists with the three `trace` rows: the two of `jit-design.md` §4.1 and the entry-counter difference of §5.6.
- **Instruments**: unchanged from revision 8; `witness` runs with `rustdoc::invalid_codeblock_attributes` allowed in the crates it reads.
- **Locks**: unchanged from revision 8.
- **`CONTEXT.md`**, **ADRs**: unchanged.

Revision 8's changes, for the record: `[[crate]]` budgets are §4.1's and the `unsafe` homes are declared once as a rule; the `php_bundle` edge names `php_ext`; the `tier` lock has two sections; `port-map` writes a reading; `bench` gains G1 with the tier bound; `phpt` gains the `force` and `exit-fuzz` lanes; `tier.lock` gains `Word::sealed`, `Func::published` as an option and `Limits::interrupt`; `ops.lock`'s `safepoint` column gains `JMPZ`; `CONTEXT.md` gains **Interrupt**; ADR-0006 added, ADR-0004 and ADR-0005 amended.
